# Naming completion plan

**Goal.** One design for command and variable name handling, shared by the LSP, the compiler and analysers, the optimiser and the tools: one identity layer, one lookup owner, one per-site record, one answer type, and registry descriptors instead of command-name special cases. The numbered items below are the work; the design pages they link to are the detail.

**Scope (owner decisions, 23 November 2026).**
- `work` is a long-running design branch with no release target. Fixes reach users through `rust`; `work` releases when the redesign is complete.
- Active: every LSP consumer and the shared compiler, database and registry owners it needs.
- Deferred off this branch: VM and runtime work (engine-only items are listed under the working order), except the engine work the owner put in scope on 26 November 2026: items 117 and 291 (crate moves), 311, 312 (with the 9.1 default release, a release-matched Tcl library owned by the platform-bootstrap owner, and the missing 9.1 commands), 313's VM half, 392f, 420's engine leg and 436. None of it gates Now 1–5. `runtime/rust` stays on Tcl 9.0 until item 88, and differential lanes that name it pin `--tcl-version 9.0`; dialect execution in the engines (Jim, iRules and others: a distant goal), BIG-IP migrations beyond the wrong answers in stage 2, and the licence (item 74, before any release).
- The 2,158-unit migration catalogue is frozen (item 96): it is a cross-check on the phases, not the driver.

**Programme done when** (deep review W16, 26 November 2026). `work` releases when all of these hold:
- item 90's done-when, and item 260's parallel-worktree acceptance (three worktrees on different edits use less disk than one does today);
- item 405's Rust⇔SpecTcl parity gate runs in `xtask-check` with an empty ratchet;
- item 486's consumer cones and item 265's dependency gate pass, and the rank gate (314) lists no exception;
- one crate per package: item 264's check finds no `tcl-lib-*` crate holding two packages' rows (R16);
- item 247's `test-support` body lint finds no test body in any `src` behind `test-support`;
- item 499's test-cleanup ledger is empty: every remaining test checks behaviour, a contract, a public interface or, for Tcl, agreement with the oracles;
- the memo gate (item 495) finds no memo outside `tcl-db`;
- item 106's budget table holds under the `release` benchmark profile, with 106's CI gate on;
- item 330's equivalence packs are gone: the Tcl 8.6 pack is deleted and the iRules pack is `tcl-lib-irules`' source;
- every item not on the deferred list is completed.

Deferred items do not gate the release, except item 74 (the licence), which comes before any release. The engine work in scope above (311, 312 and the rest) is not deferred, so it is in the last line.

**Rules.**
- Keep written source, evaluated names, namespace identity, command slots, live command identity, variable places and display text distinct. A display string never reconstructs identity; repair missing owner transport instead of weakening guards or adding string inverses.
- Oracles: C Tcl 8.4.20, 8.5.19, 8.6.18, 9.0.4 and 9.1.0 with their own `init.tcl` and tcltest, Tk 8.4–9.1, upstream jimsh, and for BIG-IP the build-stamped lab evidence. Vendor files, raw TMSH and schema tables and extracted manuals stay outside the project.
- **Done** means the item's "done when" test runs and passes, and the conformance rows it names (item 205) are green. Source review, a compile check or an assumed behaviour never counts as done.
- Commit coherent, reviewed steps; record failures and checks not run.

**Working order.** Stages run in order. Within stages 1–13 the items of a stage can run in parallel. The Now stages are ordered: each starts when the one before it is done, and inside a Now stage each item's own "after" note gives its dependencies. Phase numbers are those of [design integration](design-integration.md#corrected-order). The [compiler, analyser and SpecTcl integration design](../../design/compiler/compiler-analyser-spectcl-integration.md#7-sequencing) gives the reasons for the Now stages; its waves are these stages.
- **Now, ahead of every other stage (owner decisions, 26 November 2026):** build and iteration time first, then the registry and compiler splits, then the dialect and library model with SpecTcl 3, then the consumers onto it. Every later stage is paid for in rebuilds. The tree may stay broken while crates move; work that needs a green build waits for the crate it touches to build again, and agents finishing another item stop at a clean point rather than start new work in modules about to move. Every item of 117–119, 246–330, 390–392 and 400–498 is in exactly one Now stage, in the waiting list below them, or in the deferred engine work.

Deferred engine-only work: 18, 20, 30, 34, 38, 42, 72's engine part, 88, 135, 141, 155–157, 161, 163, 171, 177, 178, 181, 203, 208, 209, 213–216, and the engine parts of 168, 183, 186 and 193; 137 and 154. Also deferred: the remaining BIG-IP items 111, 223–226 and 234; 142's cross-event `table` half (it needs the session-state model) and 74. Also deferred, with the engine items: 423, 434 and the run-time taint items 475–479, and the engine bugs the designs found: `tclvm`'s `open` failure sets `-errorcode NONE` instead of `POSIX ENOENT`, and `exec false` succeeds ([392](../../design/registry/model/392-error-codes.md), [390](../../design/registry/model/390-package-surfaces.md#bugs-found-while-writing-the-examples)); a stack-depth error carries `NONE` instead of `TCL LIMIT STACK` (the same design); `gets` on a piped `stdin` returns -1 and an empty line ([integration](../../design/compiler/compiler-analyser-spectcl-integration.md#bugs-found)); an object created by `new` inside a method gets the calling object's namespace prefixed to its name ([391](../../design/registry/model/391-handle-types.md)); `tclvm` names a proc by its tail in `wrong # args` (`a::b` and `::a::b` give `"b x"`) ([334](../../design/registry/model/334-spectcl-codegen-vm-runtime.md)); `tclvm -c` stops on a proc with a bad `expr` with `native compiler preflight provider required` (334); `tclvm` lacks the 9.0 commands `chan`, `fcopy`, `fileevent`, `lremove`, `pid`, `socket`, `timerate`, `unload`, `zipfs` and `zlib`, and has a global `my` ([VM on 9.1](../../design/compiler/vm-tcl-9-1.md#bugs-found)); `tclvm` does not dispatch an unknown method to a TclOO class's `unknown` method (`$o bar 1` raises `unknown method "bar"`, where tclsh 8.6–9.1 call `unknown`; found through retcl's `-sync`); `tclvm`'s `regexp -start` does not anchor `\A` at the start offset (`regexp -start 3 -indices -- {\A(\s+)} "abc de" -> r` gives 0, tclsh 9.0 gives 1), so tcllib's `string token text` fails with `Unexpected character ' ' at offset 3`; `tcl dis --dialect tcl8.4` emits an empty unit when one braced expression does not parse (370); and the explorer's interprocedural `hasUnknownCalls`, true for `proc f {} { puts stdout x; close stdout }` and `proc f {} { puts [gets stdin] }` though every command there is a core command ([integration](../../design/compiler/compiler-analyser-spectcl-integration.md#bugs-found)), which waits for the summary framework because its ports keep the explorer's JSON byte-identical. The `lseq`-under-9.1 abort, the library mismatch and the `clock` abort without a library are item 312's; `scan`'s width (wrong under 9.0 and 9.1 too) and `format %b` under 8.4 are item 420's.

[Current state](current-state.md) holds live measurements and shared verification, including the [registry facts](current-state.md#registry-facts-verification).

**Item status**

- **completed** applies when the item's acceptance checks pass on `work`.
- **in progress** applies when part of the work exists on `work` and the rest is open.
- **designed** applies when the approach, owner and acceptance checks are specified.
- **not yet started** applies when a decision, investigation or design is still needed.

TclOO and class-system coverage, measured against tclsh 8.6–9.1 (matrix in the same handover, queue item 2):

1. **[designed]** (merged in part: walking every body is done once under item 83) Walk the bodies that are currently never read: one-member `oo::define C method|constructor|destructor|self method`, `oo::objdefine o method` (objects and class objects), the 9.0 `initialise` script (run at definition time), and `itcl::body`.
   - Done when: the analyser probe (README's `oo_body_probe`, made permanent under item 75) on `oo-dispatch-probe/bodies/` reports `W154` at the `&` in `05-define-method`, `06-define-constructor`, `07-define-destructor`, `09-objdefine-method`, `25-define-self-method`, `26-objdefine-class`, `13-initialise` (tcl9.0) and `19-itcl-body` (with `package require itcl`), exactly as it does for `02-class-method`; today all eight report nothing.
2. **[designed]** (merged in part: walking every body is done once under item 83) Give method, constructor, destructor, `self`/`classmethod`/`private` method, `oo::objdefine`, `oo::singleton`/`oo::abstract`, itcl and snit bodies the procedure dataflow pass; the relative-head guard in `build_method_units` should read the receiver command-world proof instead of refusing every relative head.
   - Done when: every fixture in `oo-dispatch-probe/bodies/` (02–30, including `22-singleton`, `23-abstract`, `24-class-in-ns`, the itcl and snit bodies) reports `W156` at `$b` under tcl8.6 and tcl9.0, as `01-proc` does, and a unit test shows `build_method_units` accepting the relative head in `24-class-in-ns` because the receiver command-world proof holds.
3. **[designed]** Resolve `my`, `self`, `next`, and the 9.0 `link` and `callback` helpers through the object namespace and `::oo::Helpers`, removing the false W123 in entered bodies.
   - Done when: under tcl9.0 the analyser reports no `W123` for `my`, `self`, `next`, `link` or `callback` in `discriminating/m03`–`m06`, `dispatch/b05`, `b08`, `variables/a02`, `a04`, `a06`, `a08`, `a10`, `a18`, `a19` and `frames-semantics/fr08`, while `a19-callback` and `a04-link` under tcl8.6 still report the 9.0-only helpers.
4. **[designed]** Keep classes typed after `superclass`, `filter`, `private variable` and later `oo::define` members, removing the spurious W307 on `[C new]`.
   - Done when: no `W307` on `[C new]` (or `[D new …]`) appears in `discriminating/p01`–`p05`, `q01`–`q05`, `dispatch/b02`, `b06` and `variables/a05`, `a13`, `a16`, `a21`, `a22` under tcl8.6 and tcl9.0, and the method checks on those objects still fire (`dispatch/b02` L6 reports the removed method).
5. **[designed]** Report constructor arity at `C new`/`C create`, honouring TclOO's deletion of an empty-bodied constructor or destructor, and calls to methods removed by `deletemethod` or renamed by `renamemethod`.
   - Done when: `variables/a09-ctor-arity` reports nothing (the empty constructor is deleted, so `C new 1` succeeds in tclsh 8.6–9.1), `a09b-ctor-arity-body` reports `E002` at `C new 1`, `a07-deletemethod` and `a17-renamemethod` report `W308` at `$o b` and `$o a` but not at `$o z`, and `a23-method-arity` keeps both `E002`s.
6. **[designed]** (merged in part: per-site records for nested commands is done once under item 84) Check method calls nested in scripts and substitutions (`catch {o m}`, `puts [o m]`) as at the top level; today named objects, widgets and `after` scripts are checked only at the top level, while procedures and `$o` handles are checked everywhere.
   - Done when: each nested variant in `oo-dispatch-probe/discriminating/` (`r02`, `r03`, `r05`, `r06`, `r07`, `s02`, `s04`, `s08`, `s10`) reports the same `E002`/`W001` as its top-level twin (`r01`, `s01`, `s03`, `s07`, `s09`) under tcl8.6 and tcl9.0.
7. **[designed]** Follow object reshaping: `oo::copy`, `oo::objdefine o class`, `export`/`unexport`, superclass slot operations, unexported built-ins such as `eval`, and 9.0 `oo::configurable` properties.
   - Done when: every `# BAD` line in `dispatch/b01-property-90`, `b02-slots`, `b03-copy`, `b04-class-change`, `b07-export` and `b08-object-builtins` gets a diagnostic (unknown property, removed method, `W308` on the copy, method of the old class, unexported method, unexported `eval`) under tcl9.0, and no unmarked line does; results agree with tclsh 8.6–9.1 runs of the same files.
8. **[designed]** Model namespace ensembles as dispatchers: `-map` (resolved in the ensemble's namespace), exports, `-subcommands`, `-parameters` and `-unknown` (whose handler formals are fixed, so no W214).
   - Done when: `dispatch/c01-ensemble-map` reports `E002` at L6 and an unknown subcommand at L7, `c02-ensemble-export` L8 and `c03-ensemble-parameters` L6 are reported, `c04-ensemble-unknown` has no `W214`, and `c18`/`c18b` resolve the `-map` target in the ensemble's namespace (no `W307`, L3 reported); no unmarked line in `c01`–`c04`, `c18`, `c18b` is reported.
9. **[designed]** Carry arity through `tailcall`, literal `{*}` command prefixes and coroutine commands, and stop W241 on `while 1 { yield … }` generators.
   - Done when: `dispatch/c10-tailcall` L5, `c11-command-prefix` L4 and `c12-coroutine` L4 report arity errors, `c12` and `coroutines/co01-generator` have no `W241`, and `coroutines/co02-coro-arity` reports `E003` at `c a b` with no `W123` for `c`.
10. **[designed]** itcl: accept `package require itcl` as well as `Itcl`, and check object methods, `chain` and `configure` options. snit: model `delegate`. critcl: add a model for `critcl::cproc` and `critcl::ccommand` (measurements in [`oo-dispatch-probe/`](handover/oo-dispatch-probe/README.md); queue item 3 of the handover).
   - Done when: with `package require itcl`, `dispatch/d01` and `d04` and `bodies/17`–`19`, `27` report no `W120`; `d01` L11 (`E002`) and L12 (unknown method), `d02` L10 (method not delegated) and `d03` L4 (`critcl::cproc` arity) are reported, and no unmarked line is; itcl rows match itcl 4.3.5 under tclsh 9.0.

**Foundations:** the [cross-cutting review](cross-cutting-review.md) finds the same faults in every domain study below — no semantic identity layer, no query layer, no common meaning for "unknown", release policy copied instead of owned, conformance data that drives only one side, kernels that cannot take partial knowledge, an IR that loses structure, incomplete coverage of reachable code, semantics hard-coded outside the registry, two drifting engines, silent failures, spelling-only gates, stale contracts and ad hoc measurement. Build those foundations before the domain items (probe harness and conformance matrix, answer and outcome types, identity layer, then release facts, registry descriptors and abstract-value kernels); the domain items then reduce to moving consumers onto owners and deleting copies (items 74–91 number these foundations). Its correctness backlog is stage 2 of the working order, independent of the refactor.

**Architecture:** [tidying the architecture](architecture-tidy-plan.md) sets out one pipeline with one set of facts, the shared structures, the crate layers, a single caching layer, and the phases that retire the duplicate walkers and resolvers; the plans below are parts of it, including [call frames](frame-design.md), [namespaces](namespace-design.md), [expressions](expression-design.md), [packages](package-design.md), [rename and alias tracking](rename-alias-design.md), [scripts, words and writers](words-scripts-design.md) and [lists, dicts and arrays](lists-dicts-arrays-design.md).

**Command lookup** (stage 9 of the working order): centralise it — one lookup-frame value, registry-declared evaluation contexts, one lookup owner with one answer type that every diagnostic, editor feature and the optimiser ask, and consumer conformance vectors. Evidence and steps: [command lookup is not centralised](command-lookup-centralisation.md). Most items below are symptoms of it. Variable lookup has the same fault and should share the frame value: [variable lookup is not centralised](variable-lookup-centralisation.md); the false W210s of item 11 and the missing TclOO, `apply`, `uplevel` and `upvar` array navigation come from it.

The shared causes behind items 1–58 — one frame lookup context, one per-invocation record for nested commands, one instance value, fine-grained transitions, one class fact with option tables on methods, value grammars — are set out with their evidence in [TclOO and Tk naming: root causes](oo-tk-root-causes.md); fix those before the individual symptoms.

Build items 1–10 on one object model specialised per system through registry profiles and SpecTcl, as set out in the [object systems plan](object-systems-plan.md): profile facts replace every `DefinerFamily` match, source and registry classes share one class fact and one method-call checker, objects are values on one path, and member bodies get the procedure pass. New profiles follow for itcl, snit delegation, stooop, XOTcl/NX, critcl and Tk megawidgets.

A third-party package review ([overrides](overrides-package-review.md)) adds: caller-frame script parameters derived from `uplevel 1` in procedure bodies, value grammars for code inside data (override dictionaries of lambdas), body-scoped helpers outside object systems, scoped command overrides, a false W214/lost W301 around `"${name}::$x"` (reproducer kept), and whole-file specialisation of library procedures.

Non-OO evaluation, namespace and alias gaps (queue item 4 of the handover, fixtures in `oo-dispatch-probe/dynamic/`):

11. **[designed]** (merged in part: implicit writes reaching W210 is done once under item 22) Remove false W210 for variables set by `catch` bodies, `regexp` match variables, a callee's `upvar`, `subst` command substitution and `eval` of a literal `list` command.
   - Done when: the analyser reports no `W210` in `dynamic/e02` (L4), `e04`, `e13`, `e27`, `e28` and `variables-lookup/v17`, `frames-semantics/fr01` and `fr09` under tcl8.6 and tcl9.0, while `e02`'s `W001` typo at L7 remains.
12. **[designed]** (merged in part: W214 on fixed-signature callbacks is done once under item 160; "did you mean `!`" is done once under item 195) Exempt fixed-signature callbacks (variable traces, ensemble `-unknown` handlers) from W214, and drop the nonsense "did you mean '!'" suggestion on W123.
   - Done when: `dynamic/e22-trace-var` and `dispatch/c04-ensemble-unknown` report no `W214`, and no `W123` message on any `oo-dispatch-probe/` fixture contains `did you mean '!'` (`grep -r "did you mean '!'"` over the regenerated `.out` files returns nothing).
13. **[completed]** Model 8.x namespace variable "creative writing" (`set x` in `namespace eval` writes an existing global on 8.4–8.6, not on 9.0) per selected dialect.
   - Phase 1: The analyser and editor use the retained namespace receiver to identify the existing global in Tcl 8.x. The eight named fixtures passed on all five pinned C Tcl releases, and the analyser and LSP definition and reference checks agreed across the five profiles.
   - Done when: LSP definition and references for `x` in `variables-lookup/v13-ns-eval-creative` and the analyser on `dynamic/e12`, `e29`, `e31` and `variables-flow/w01`–`w04` follow 8.x creative writing under tcl8.4–8.6 (the write goes to `::x`; `e31` L3 is reported) and namespace-local writing under tcl9.0 (no report), matching tclsh 8.4–9.1 runs of the same files.
14. **[completed]** Report: `namespace code` captured in the wrong namespace, reads of never-set globals, reads after `unset`, calls through a deleted alias target, arity of procedures defined inside procedures, writes to a 9.0 `const`, and calls to a built-in replaced by a procedure with a different signature.
   - The supplied whole-document entry follows the documented stock `tclsh file.tcl` premise. A selected implicit stdout write preserves command and cell facts while the channel evidence holds. The source walk withdraws that evidence when it cannot account for channel changes or callbacks. Explicit live and history-unknown entries keep their own entry facts. Inherited stdout transforms can supply `::helper` in `e23`, supply `::nope` in `e05`, or replace `::set` in `e20`. [Execution evidence](../../../evidence/tcl/item-14-stdout-observer-entry.md)
   - Tcl 9.0 and 9.1 reject `set`, `incr`, `append` and literal `unset` on a constant before any variable trace runs. `lappend` and `dict set` still run read traces. `array unset` on the scalar completes successfully. [Execution evidence](../../../evidence/tcl/constant-write-observer-order.md)
   - Split into separately checkable parts, each with its own fixture in `oo-dispatch-probe/dynamic/`:
     - 14a (verified): `namespace code` captured in the wrong namespace. The selected `namespace inscope` uses the existing namespace and appends extra operands as Tcl list elements. A missing command in a proved materialised script reports at the actual written script operand. The unchanged `e23-ns-code` reports `helper` unresolved in `::` at L6 across all five pinned profiles. The `::lib` calls resolve, and the bound dispatch controls have no `W307`.
     - 14b (verified): reads of never-set globals. The namespace cell owner retains the exact absence at `e05-global` L5 under the stock stdout premise. All five profiles report `W210` at `nope` and leave both `g` reads clean.
     - 14c (verified): reads after `unset`. The analyser reports `e15` and leaves the `v24` existence probe clean. Done when: `e15-unset-read` reports `W210` (or a dedicated code) at `set v` after `unset v`, and `variables-lookup/v24-unset-read` (`info exists u`) stays clean.
     - 14d (verified under Tcl 8.6 and 9.0): calls through a deleted alias target. Done when: `e16-alias-chain` L7 (`two y` after `interp alias {} one {}`) is reported, and L4–L5 keep their current answers.
     - 14e (verified under Tcl 8.6 and 9.0): arity of procedures defined inside procedures. Done when: `e17-proc-inside-proc` L4 (`made` with no argument) reports `E002`, as `e32` does for a top-level procedure.
     - 14f (verified): writes to a 9.0 `const`. The resolver checks immutability on the selected cell generation at the write. The unchanged `e20-const-90` reports `E007` at `limit` on L3 under Tcl 9.0 and 9.1. Tcl 8.4–8.6 report that `const` is unavailable. Controls cover aliases, repeated initialisation, existing mutable cells, namespace recreation and trace veto. An installed stdout transform that replaces `set` withdraws the proof.
     - 14g (verified under Tcl 8.6 and 9.0): built-in replaced by a procedure with a different signature. Done when: `e07-shadow-builtin` reports `E003` at `string length abc` inside `p` (the replacement takes one argument), and `e30-rename-builtin-proc-wrap` (an `args` wrapper) reports no arity error.

Reloading a registry or environment while queries run must not mix generations (the catalogue's environment-registry reload races).

48. **[designed]** (merged into 495, deep review W7: its done-when is step 14's memo gate) Memos inside analysis values and process-global compiler memos become tracked queries or per-query scratch; retire `RetainedModules`' private LRU inside a salsa input.
   - Done when: the memo rule of item 76 finds no memo inside analysis values or process-global compiler memo outside the salsa database, and `RetainedModules`' private LRU is deleted.
49. **[designed]** (merged in part: caching is done once under item 90) One analysis pipeline (fold the fresh-analysis, workspace-scan, recovery, class and provider analyses into salsa tiers), secondary indices as tracked queries, per-body re-analysis on edits (no salsa interning for identity types, [step 14](../../design/compiler/crate-split/14-caching.md); deep review W7), and gates against new memos and leaks outside it with determinism, growth and latency budgets.
   - Done when: the server's fresh-analysis, workspace-scan, recovery, class and provider analyses run as salsa queries; a salsa event-log test shows an edit to one procedure body re-executes only that body's queries; and determinism, growth and latency budget tests run in CI.

Analysis data structures and a typed IR (evidence and full steps: [analysis data structures](analysis-data-structures.md)):

50. **[designed]** Annotated commands in the IR: the resolved `CommandRef`, the selected form, and per word its role, place, value type or domain and body unit, computed once from the registry and packs (today about 990 production lines re-ask the registry for roles by command name and argument strings); `word_exprs` mandatory, flat `args`/`argv_texts` retired.
   - Done when: IR commands carry `CommandRef`, `FormRef` and per-word annotations, `word_exprs` is mandatory, `rg 'argv_texts'` returns nothing in production code, and a `registry-axes` ratchet counting registry role lookups by command name in analysis reaches zero from about 990.
51. **[designed]** Typed keys in every pass: GVN keys over command references and operand values instead of `Vec<String>` spellings; SSA uses and defs as places and symbols; SCCP constant sets with a typed value; blocks by `BlockId` everywhere (dead stores today parse the `_N` suffix of block names); symbol- or place-keyed interval, escape and parameter sets; enums for finding reasons, labels and the top-level function.
   - Done when: `cargo xtask registry-axes` (typed mode, item 53) passes with GVN keyed by command references, SSA by places, blocks by `BlockId` (no `_N` suffix parsing), and a `FunctionKind` instead of `"::top"` comparisons, and the analyser and optimiser goldens are unchanged.
52. **[designed]** Shared structural queries: cached predecessors and dominance (today `predecessors()` rebuilds a map on each of about 18 calls), offset-insensitive `ExprNode` equality instead of comparing rendered text, dense vectors and bitsets.
   - Done when: a test shows `predecessors()` and dominance built once per function across all callers, `ExprNode` equality ignores offsets without rendering (no rendered-text comparison remains, per item 53's gate), and the analyser and optimiser goldens are unchanged.
53. **[designed]** (merged in part: gates and waivers is done once under item 76) Type-level gates: no new `String`-keyed maps of commands, variables, blocks or namespaces in analysis passes, no comparisons of rendered text, no parsing of display names.
   - Done when: `cargo xtask registry-axes` passes on the tree and fails on test inputs that add a `String`-keyed map of commands, variables, blocks or namespaces in an analysis pass, a rendered-text comparison, or a display-name parse.

User procedures, objects and libraries (design: [user and library commands](user-and-library-commands.md)):

54. **[designed]** One command-contract type (identity, forms, per-parameter roles with frames and evaluation contexts, result type, effects, provenance) shared by the registry, packs, stubs and inference, carried on annotated commands.
   - Done when: one command-contract type is produced by the registry, packs, stubs and inference and carried on annotated commands, a test shows a SpecTcl stub and an inferred procedure of the same signature give equal contracts, and the per-source signature types are deleted (grep).
55. **[designed]** Inferred contracts as salsa queries per body unit and call-graph component: roles from parameter traits (caller-frame variables, caller-frame scripts, command prefixes, expressions), types and domains from uses, results from returns, effects from the frame-effect and interprocedural summaries; consumed by W210, W123, E002, navigation and the optimiser.
   - Done when: inferred contracts are salsa queries per body and call-graph component, `dynamic/e04` (callee `upvar`) reports no `W210`, a typo in a script passed as a parameter to `uplevel 1` in `external-overrides/` is reported, and `E002` on `dynamic/e08`, `e32` still fires through the contract.
   - The caller-frame-script slice is item 245 ([script parameters](script-parameters-design.md)).
56. **[designed]** Library contracts: installed source packages found by package discovery analysed per version and dialect and cached on disk by source hash; declared contracts for binary and critcl extensions; class facts from object-system profiles giving method contracts of the same shape.
   - Done when: calls into an installed source package (`packages/lib/foo`, `packages/tm/acme`) get `E002` and navigation from its analysed contract, a second run reads the on-disk cache keyed by source hash without re-analysing, and `dispatch/d03-critcl` `add 1` reports `E002` from a declared contract.
57. **[designed]** Declared and inferred in one format: export inferred contracts as SpecTcl (`spec-author`, Spec Studio `import_package`); declared contracts win for consumers and inference reports contradictions.
   - Done when: exporting an inferred contract as SpecTcl through `spec-author`/Spec Studio and importing it back gives an equal contract, a fixture whose stub contradicts its implementation reports the contradiction, and the declared contract wins in consumers' answers.
58. **[designed]** Integrate contracts with the naming owners: selection through positioned binding answers, inferred contracts on publication and declaration receipts, parameter roles as naming purposes and effects as existing transitions, Logical authority only, existing partial receipts folded into contract views, owner-manifest and consumer-matrix rows ([integration](user-and-library-commands.md#integration-with-the-naming-architecture)).
   - Done when: `cargo xtask owner-resolution` passes with owner-manifest and consumer-matrix rows for contracts, contracts are selected through positioned binding answers (`dynamic/e08-redefine-arity` checks each call against the definition in force there), and the old partial receipts are views of the contract (grep finds no separate stores).

Constants, constant folding and procedure folding (evidence and full steps: [constants and folding](constant-folding-design.md); fixtures in `oo-dispatch-probe/constants/`):

59. **[completed]** One constant value: `ExactValue` (bytes, numeric reading, representation) in SCCP, the expression folder, static loops, return summaries and `ConstOps`, replacing about ten constant types and three literal parsers; float spelling decided once by the release policy.
   - Conformance: `rust/tcl-cli/tests/value_transfers_cli/release_spellings.rs#opt_preserves_constant_fixture_outcomes`.
   - SCCP text carries a contents-only `ExactValue<String>`. An alias read can change an object's current native representation while its SSA contents stay the same, so the lattice leaves that evidence to the original use site. Joins and change detection use the same bitwise double identity. The expression folder shares the `TclValue` numeric payload and the selected double formatter.
   - Expression environments share the folder's `TclValue` numeric payload and retain string bindings as `ExactValue<String>`. Native reads still carry their proofs independently. The `registry-axes` check holds both payloads to their owners.
   - SCCP and static loops use the `NumericValue` payload from `ExactValue` for numbers and booleans. They retain numeric values until the consumer needs text. The lattice keeps booleans distinct from equal integers and compares doubles by their bits. The `registry-axes` check holds the numeric payload to the shared owner.
   - First-iteration analysis passes constant for-loop initialisers through the exact-text owner, preserving a braced `010` without assigning it a numeric reading. Rotation still requires a condition the expression evaluator can prove without a retained native conversion.
   - Direct registry substitution folds retain `ExactValue` through resolved fold results. Legacy callbacks still supply text. The bytecode consumer reads those bytes and uses its existing literal recipe for each selected operation.
   - Decoded literal bytes enter `ExactValue` directly in SCCP and static loop evaluation. The shared ingress classifies canonical integers and retains every byte, including NULs and bytes with no UTF-8 view.
   - Current state: Registry `ConstOps`, materialised procedure returns and compiler projections use `ExactValue`. The `registry-axes` check holds those payloads to the shared owner. The compiler preserves noncanonical numeric spellings and widens raw bytes that have no text view. One `parse_literal_value` remains. The optimiser and pinned tclsh agreed on exit status and both output streams in 30 comparisons across all five Tcl releases. `c18`, `e06`, `e07` and the boolean-return control rewrote under every release. In that run, `c13` and `c14` remained unchanged.
   - Focused checks passed for all 22 `ConstOps` tests, noncanonical numeric bytes and the procedure call rewrite. The broader summary tests reproduced the prior failures for return through a body word and purity of a getter method.
   - Expression environments, native integer-read receipts, the expression folder, selected native result dependencies and SCCP expression projection carry `ExactValue<String>` through their text boundaries. The spelling, numeric interpretation and object coercion evidence stay independent. The `registry-axes` check holds the environment's string payload to the owner. All 100 expression tests, the selected-result SCCP control and all 22 `ConstOps` tests passed. Static loop simulation passed 22 tests and reproduced both baseline switch failures.
   - The merged build matched 86 of 96 previous analyser and optimiser outputs. Tcl 9.0 emitted fewer rewrites in nine fixtures and withdrew `I230` in `c19-info-exists`. All 23 execution comparisons matched exit status, stdout and stderr, including the changed Tcl 9.0 scripts. `c18`, `e06` and `e07` still rewrote on all five pinned releases.
   - Unmaterialised numeric and object facts carry their own proof until exact bytes are available. The reached-site owner selects the original `::expr` for four `c13` calls on all five releases. A portable procedure rewrite still needs dispatch evidence for every entry and proof that the body edit preserves reflection. A `c14` trial replaced `2**64` with `1<<64` and preserved results and object observations under ordinary stdout on Tcl 8.5–9.1. A stdout transform reading `info body p` distinguished the changed body on Tcl 8.6, 9.0 and 9.1. Tcl 8.4 includes the expression, list and return body verbatim in its compile error. The optimiser retains the original source in both cases. [Execution evidence](../../../evidence/tcl/optimizer-c14-stdout-reflection.md)
   - Replacing the final `[p]` alone preserved procedure body bytes but changed the command count the parent could read after sourcing either fixture on Tcl 8.6, 9.0 and 9.1. The `c14` trial also removed the Tcl 8.4 error and changed stdout with `tcl_precision` set to 3 on Tcl 8.5 and 8.6. O103 selected the original `p` on all five releases and retained no proof of normal completion that would allow those call edits. [Execution evidence](../../../evidence/tcl/constant-call-command-count.md)
   - A `proc` execution trace read the published `p` body before the first call. It distinguished a single expression fold in each of `c13` and `c14` on all five pinned releases. [Execution evidence](../../../evidence/tcl/constant-procedure-body-reflection.md)
   - The pinned release matrix matched all 25 pairs on exit status, stdout and stderr. The optimiser rewrote all 15 positive cases and preserved both source-sensitive fixtures on every release.
   - Done when (owner decision, 26 November 2026): `constants/folds/c18-octal-release`, `e06-float` and `e07-float-text` rewrite under tclsh 8.4–9.1. `c13-number-forms` and `c14-float-format` retain their source while the tested rewrites change observable behaviour. Exit status, stdout and stderr agree before and after for all five fixtures on each release. One `parse_literal_value` remains, and the other constant types are views of `ExactValue`, held by a `registry-axes` ratchet.
60. **[completed]** One evaluator per command: registry folds for `string` case conversion, `reverse`, `map`, `format`, `scan` and `concat` call `tcl-cmd-core`; codegen's `fold_const_branch` and `concat_cmd` use the shared folders; one `format` path for every entry point; the `string compare --`/`equal --`/`string is` wrong folds from the test-scrub handover are fixed.
   - Phase 1: The registry's format fold uses the command core's template and renderer. String case conversion, reverse, map, scan and concat use shared kernels, and folded codegen branches use the Tcl expression grammar. `c02`, `c03` and `c17` rewrote and ran equivalently on all five pinned C Tcl releases. The compare, equal and string-is controls passed against the C Tcl and Jim oracles.
   - Done when: `constants/folds/c02-string-ops`, `c03-format` and `c17-string-concat` and the test-scrub `string compare --`, `string equal --` and `string is` rows fold to tclsh's values per release, and codegen's `fold_const_branch` and `concat_cmd` call the shared folders (no `.trim()` or local number parsing left in `bytecoded.rs`).
61. **[completed]** One SCCP fixed-point driver produces optimiser facts and diagnostic original value facts.
   - Phase 1: Both consumers use the same reachable-block traversal, entry opening, predecessor selection, phi joins, fixed-point stages and terminator edge transfer. Barrier widening, fresh SSA clobbers and prior-value reads share one lattice transfer. Source receipt checks and optimiser completion and existence tracking are callbacks in the same sweep. All 96 paired analyser and optimiser golden comparisons matched on the rebased build. All three new solver tests passed, together with the loop-jump and host-barrier agreement checks. Three other switch and catch checks reproduced the same failures on the baseline.
   - Done when: one SCCP solver produces both the optimiser facts and the diagnostic "original value facts" (the second solver is deleted), and the analyser and optimiser goldens on `constants/folds/` are unchanged.
62. **[designed]** Specialised procedure summaries: fold calls with constant arguments, recursion under a fuel budget, constant procedures inside `expr`, and cross-file and library calls through contract queries (today O103 folds zero-argument, single-file, non-recursive calls only); bounded constant loops through `static_loops`; one boolean spelling for `ConstantReturn`.
   - Done when: `constants/folds/c07-pure-proc`, `c08-recursive` (within the fuel budget), `d13-const-proc-in-expr` and `c06-loop` fold with execution before and after equal under tclsh 8.6 and 9.0, `c09-impure-proc`, `c11-redefined`, `c12-renamed-builtin` and `c15-upvar-proc` do not fold, and `e01-bool-return` gives one boolean spelling.
63. **[designed]** (merged in part: `tcl_precision` as a whole-program fact is done once under item 165) Whole-program absence facts (no `tcl::mathfunc` override, no `tcl_precision` write, no intervening `unknown`) to unlock math-function folds and 8.5–8.6 float folds where proven.
   - Done when: `constants/folds/d09-mathfunc-override` does not fold, a math-function call in a fixture with no override folds, and `c14-float-format` folds under 8.5 and 8.6 only when no `tcl_precision` write is proven, with execution before and after equal.
64. **[designed]** No silent no-op entry point: `optimise()` and `optimise_with_dialect(…, None)` fold nothing; remove them or make "release unknown" fold what every release agrees on; no folder defaults to 9.0 when the release is absent.
   - Done when: `optimise()` and the `None` dialect are gone from public entry points, or `optimise_with_dialect(…, None)` on `constants/folds/` yields exactly the folds every release agrees on (the `NONE-VS-9.0` lines in `constants/optimiser.out`), and no folder defaults to 9.0 when the release is absent (grep).
65. **[designed]** (merged in part: conformance rows is done once under item 75) Execution-checked fold conformance as a committed test: every fold fixture run before and after rewriting under every tclsh and jimsh per release (today the registry differential is mostly 9.0-only and the optimiser coverage values were checked by hand).
   - Done when: a committed test regenerates `constants/execution.tsv`, running every fold fixture before and after rewriting under tclsh 8.4–9.1 and jimsh with all rows equal, and fails rather than skips when an interpreter is missing.

Name resolution end to end (validation of the plans above against two code inventories: [name-resolution validation](name-resolution-validation.md); fixtures in `oo-dispatch-probe/name-validation/`):

66. **[designed]** Split the frame value: `Frame` per activation (identity, kind, namespace identity, level relation, receiver) and `NameWorld` at each program point (path, imports, aliases, defined commands, variable links, instance variables, traces) from the one binding walk; both lookup owners take a frame and a point and return one `Answer<T>` with provenance and authority, extending `SourceInvocationBinding` (which evolves into the annotated command) and `SsaSourceView::read_reference`.
   - Done when: `Frame` and `NameWorld` exist and both lookup owners return `Answer<T>` with provenance and authority; `name-validation/` (`p01`–`p03`, `v01`–`v04`, `m01`) and `frames/` give tclsh's answers through LSP definition and hover; and the replaced frame and context types are removed, held by the `walker-ownership` ratchet.
67. **[designed]** (merged in part: navigation after `source` is done once under item 100) A reverse index per identity derived only from forward answers at every site (proven and possible kept apart), replacing `fallback_proc_by_simple_name`, `invocation_references_named`, the minifier's `find_proc_call_sites` and the per-feature variable models behind references, rename and highlights.
   - Done when: references and rename on `namespaces/nav/` and `rename-alias/editor-rename/before/` come from the reverse index and agree with forward answers (proven and possible kept apart), and `fallback_proc_by_simple_name`, `invocation_references_named` and `find_proc_call_sites` are deleted, listed in `cargo xtask retired-api-gate`.
68. **[designed]** (merged in part: caching is done once under item 90) The cross-file name world: files and packages export contracts and transitions; binding applies them at `source`, `package require` and workspace assumptions; the workspace index stores exports and the reverse index and stops resolving (`command_settlement.rs`, `namespace_imports.rs`).
   - Done when: a two-file fixture (`source` plus `package require` of `packages/lib/foo`) gives definition, hover and `E002` across files from exported contracts, and `command_settlement.rs` and `namespace_imports.rs` no longer resolve, listed in `cargo xtask retired-api-gate`.
69. **[designed]** One candidate generator: the byte `NativeNameProjection` with per-interpreter recipes; relative `namespace path` entries made absolute when bound; the string candidate family becomes display-only, then goes; every resolver that passes no path (`alias.rs`, `type_infer.rs`, class resolution, the LSP's class lookup) moves to the owner.
   - Done when: `name-validation/p01-relative-path` resolves through `NativeNameProjection`, `alias.rs`, `type_infer.rs`, class resolution and the LSP's class lookup call the owner, and the string candidate family is deleted, with `cargo xtask resolution-drift` failing on a reintroduced copy.
70. **[designed]** Variable name text: the name-end rule in `tcl_core_types::naming`, one variable-reference grammar per release and dialect in `tcl-lexer` (`word_parts::scan_var_ref`) built on it, both re-exported through `tcl-syntax::naming`, one element splitter in the lexer taking the selected protocol, one `static::` rule; retire the three scanners, four splitters, about ten `contains('(')` tests and the optimiser's text counters; add a drift gate.
   - Done when: `name-validation/v01`–`v04` and `variables-lookup/v20`–`v22` give tclsh's answers per release through `word_parts::scan_var_ref`, the analyser and the LSP; `rg "contains\('\('\)"` on variable names returns nothing; and a new drift gate fails on a reintroduced scanner or splitter.
71. **[designed]** Builtins only through a resolved `CommandRef` (no registry lookup by written word in analysis, codegen or the LSP); one subcommand-prefix rule; one import-pattern matcher (`NativeGlobProtocol`).
   - Done when: `cargo xtask retired-api-gate` finds no registry lookup by written word in analysis, codegen or the LSP; `name-validation/p02-shadow-builtin` navigates to the namespace procedure; subcommand-prefix rows give one answer; and `namespaces/ns03-export-import` patterns match through `NativeGlobProtocol` only.
72. **[designed]** (merged in part: the level-word grammar is done once under item 19; the engine traits is done once under item 88) Engine resolution once: one lookup loop per engine over a `tcl-runtime-api` namespace-walk trait (today two in the VM and one in the runtime), one execution name policy with live-object adapters (today 970 and 772 lines), `tcloo_linearise` in both engines (the runtime has its own C3), one level grammar, one compiled-locals decision read by codegen, the bytecode, the VM and the runtime; engine caches invalidated by the shared identity tokens on the same events.
   - Done when (engine part deferred): `name-validation/m01-linearisation` and the command-resolution vectors run green in the VM and the runtime through one lookup loop per engine over a `tcl-runtime-api` trait and `tcloo_linearise` (the runtime's own C3 deleted), and codegen, the bytecode and both engines read one compiled-locals decision.
73. **[designed]** (merged in part: caching is done once under item 90) Name caches in three salsa tiers (per-body binding facts and forward answers, per-file and per-package exports, the reverse index); delete the analyser and signature-scan maps, workspace link maps, `VariableSymbolMap`, the per-URI class analysis and SCCP's `frame_clobbers` memo; in architecture phase 3 the analyser walk and signature scan stop resolving and only produce declarations; add the minifier, codegen, MCP, graphs, explorer, diagram, SpecTcl static drive and test generators to consumer conformance.
   - Done when: name caches are the three binding tiers (per-body binding facts and forward answers, per-file and per-package exports, the reverse index), and `VariableSymbolMap`, the per-URI class analysis and the analyser and signature-scan maps are deleted (grep). (Deep review W7: SCCP's `frame_clobbers` memo is a row of `ownership/memos.tsv` that item 495 disposes of, and consumer conformance is item 85's agreement harness.)

Foundations, architecture and object systems (the structural work the domain items depend on; evidence and detail in the [cross-cutting review](cross-cutting-review.md), the [architecture plan](architecture-tidy-plan.md), [root causes](oo-tk-root-causes.md), the [object systems plan](object-systems-plan.md) and the [overrides review](overrides-package-review.md)):

74. **[not yet started]** (deferred by the owner on 23 November 2026; revisit before release) Decide the licence (Tcl or `MIT OR Apache-2.0` instead of AGPL-3.0-or-later), then change `LICENSE`, every `Cargo.toml`, the source headers and their checker, the xtask banner, AGENTS.md, `deny.toml` and the marketplace manifests; set the dependency licence policy.
   - Done when: `LICENSE`, every `Cargo.toml` `license` field, the source headers, `deny.toml` and the marketplace manifests name the chosen licence; the header checker and `cargo deny check licenses` pass with the new policy.
75. **[completed]** (lead item for conformance rows, shared with items 25, 28, 37, 65, 131, 198) Probe harness and conformance framework (faults 5 and 14): vector tables as rows, implementations as columns (tclsh 8.4–9.1, Jim, the VM, the runtime, the folder, analysis queries, each LSP feature, the optimiser, writers and tools), one reported test per row (`insta`, `libtest-mimic`); the `oo-dispatch-probe/` fixtures become rows and the temporary probes in its README become permanent runners.
   - Done when: one command (`cargo xtask conformance`, `libtest-mimic` harness) runs every fixture under `oo-dispatch-probe/*/` as a row with columns tclsh 8.4–9.1, jimsh, the VM, the runtime, the folder, the analyser and each LSP feature, reporting one test per row, and reproduces the committed `dispatch.out`, `frames.out`, `variables.out` and `dynamic.out` as snapshots with no diff. The README no longer tells anyone to "add this test temporarily".
   - Verified 25 November 2026. `cargo xtask conformance` runs all 815 fixtures (924 tests with the artefact checks) through C Tcl 8.4–9.1, jimsh, the VM and the runtime (each run on a fresh copy of the fixture's directory), the analyser, the folder and the editor features at `;# CALL` and `;# READ` marks, in about 14 minutes on 4 cores. Two consecutive `--update` and check runs agree on every row, and a hand edit to `dispatch.out` fails its row. The committed files themselves are the snapshots instead of `insta` `.snap` files, so the existing `.out` names and links stay valid; `frames.out` and `variables-lookup.out` reproduce the archived answers byte for byte where the analyser is unchanged. Jim has no dialect profile, so its rows have analyser columns only. The rows found overlapping optimiser rewrites in five fixtures (item 244) and VM panics on invalid UTF-8 in `encodings-channels/e05` and `e15` (engine work, deferred); both are recorded in the snapshots.
76. **[completed]** (lead item for gates and waivers, shared with items 53, 114) Semantic gates (fault 12): the walker and resolver manifest with an only-shrinks `walker-ownership` ratchet, the crate-dependency rule (`cargo-deny` wrappers), the memo rule, the release-table lint and host-primitive lints (`split_whitespace`, `trim`, `.chars().count()`, `contains('(')`); contract claims linked to vector rows or tests (fault 13).
   - Done when: `cargo xtask check` runs the `walker-ownership`, crate-layer (`cargo-deny` `[bans]` wrappers), memo-type, release-table, host-primitive and contract-evidence gates and passes on the tree. Each gate has an xtask test that seeds one reintroduction and asserts the gate fails: a new walker absent from the manifest, an upward crate dependency, a `OnceLock<Mutex<HashMap>>` memo, a hand-written operator table, `split_whitespace` on Tcl text, and a contract claim with no linked row.
   - The [ownership contract](ownership/README.md) links the gates, ratchets and reintroduction tests.
77. **[in progress]** Identity layer (fault 1): interned, typed keys with incarnation or generation and provenance for command, namespace, variable place, frame, body unit, instance, package and interpreter (the catalogue has five interpreter-subject units that need it; item 179 covers only the selectors); display text derived and never parsed; the engines' tokens exposed in the same vocabulary through `tcl-runtime-api`.
   - Done when: a test over `dynamic/e09-delete-proc.tcl` and `rename-alias/r01-rename-basic.tcl` gets two different `CommandRef` incarnations for a deleted-and-recreated procedure in analysis, and the same split from both engines' tokens through `tcl-runtime-api`, so the conformance runner's engine-versus-analysis identity column is green. `rg` finds no function that builds an identity from display text in any crate under `rust/` (deep review W9: the two crates it named are split by items 90 and 289).
   - Verified: [source and engine identity checks](current-state.md#items-77-and-78-validation).
   - Status 25 November 2026: reopened to in progress for the display-text clause. Native source declaration cards (`command_binding/source_transition_advice/native_declared_body.rs`) now keep their structured `SignatureNamespaceScope` and derive an optional authored key from it with the new `SourceNamespaceKey::authored_path` (C components) and `authored_jim_namespace` (Jim counted bytes) constructors, and compare the two structurally. A non-UTF-8 namespace (`namespace eval a\u0000b {}`, which materialises `C0 80`) keeps its card with no authored key (`namespace_identity_tests`). The Jim wrapper namespace and seven `checked_namespace_path_key(..).map(authored)` sites use the same constructors. Still open: `command_binding/ensemble_dispatch.rs` builds a relative ensemble map target's command key with `rooted_member_key(&frozen.namespace.display()?, head)`, which needs a slot lookup that keeps the ensemble's no-global-fallback rule. Compatibility `lookup_namespace` strings copied from `display()` beside their keys (`binding_projection.rs`, `catalogue_candidates.rs`, `deferred_entries.rs`, `pre_handler_failure.rs`, compared in `compilation_lookup_advice.rs`) and `ResolveContext.namespace` in `var_resolve/namespace_identity.rs` are labels next to the retained key, not identities, and are left as they are.
   - Crate (26 November 2026): the identity and key types move to their own crate, `tcl-names`, as step 3 of item 90's crate split (item 248). This item's remaining work folds into item 274, inside `tcl-names`: no new code for this item lands in the modules item 248 moves until they have moved.
78. **[completed]** (amended by 439: the shared editor outcome is `Abstain { code, at, reason, missing }`, whose `AbstainCode::Lookup(UnknownReason)` carries 78's reasons, see [335](../../design/registry/model/335-spectcl-source-tools.md); and by 98: one `Authority` enum replaces `NamingAuthority`) Answer and outcome types (fault 3): one `Answer<T>` (proven, bounded set, absent, unknown with a reason) with provenance and naming authority, one editor outcome (done, or refused with a reason the user sees); transitions mark only what they touch as unknown (persistent maps such as `imbl`); abstentions measured separately from wrong answers.
   - Done when: `Answer<T>` (proven, bounded set, absent, unknown with reason) is the only answer type the lookup owner returns, and `rg` finds no `MayPresent` or other private presence enum. `prepareRename` and MCP `rename` at the unresolved `::util::scale` call in `editor-actions/workspace/nosource.tcl` return a refusal with a reason instead of `null`. The conformance report prints abstentions and wrong answers as separate counts per column.
   - Verified: [answer, refusal and conformance checks](current-state.md#items-77-and-78-validation).
   - Gaps closed 25 November 2026: the private `ReportCandidatePresence`, `OriginalEventPresence` and `OriginalIrulesFlowPresence` enums are now `Knowledge<T>` (which gained `as_proven`/`into_proven`). `ContentsPresence` stays as the one public variable-contents lattice: it carries no value and is `Copy` across its 253 uses. `cargo xtask conformance` ends with an `engine agree abstain wrong` table per VM and runtime column, where an abstention is an explicit refusal (`refused:`, `not supported`, `not implemented`) and any other difference from C Tcl of the same release is wrong.

The `tk/` widget, image and font rows carry the per-site records 391b reads (use after `destroy` is 391d's row).

   - Done when: the analyser on `external-overrides/w214-reproducer.tcl` reports no W214 and still reports the W301. `tclvm` on a script that raises an error in file mode prints the error and exits non-zero. 90. **[in progress]** (lead item for the crate split and for removing hand-written caches, shared with items 47, 48, 49, 68, 73, 77, 101, 103; scheduled ahead of every other stage, 26 November 2026) Crates and caching ([crate split design](../../design/compiler/crate-split-design.md)): split `tcl-compiler` into layered crates whose cycles are broken by design; data and loaders below features, domain crates contributing data and hooks through interfaces, tools on the semantic model; then the caches the split leaves, deleted or moved into one database at the top; build time, binary size and runtime latency measured per split.
   - Why first: 96 of `tcl-compiler`'s 121 modules form one cycle, so any edit rebuilds the whole crate and every crate above it, and every later stage of this plan would otherwise land in that one crate and need moving again.
   - Goal: a clean rearchitecture into many shared crates with clean interfaces for every consumer, fast builds, minimal caching and good performance. `tcl-compiler` is the first piece and `tcl-registry` (items 117 and 118) the second.
   - Breakage: the tree may stay broken between steps. Steps move code directly rather than build shims to stay green. The gates, failset comparisons and measurements run when each crate first builds again. This is the "`work` branch exception" to the green-step rules of the [engineering guide](../../design/rust/engineering-guide.md) and the [tidy plan](architecture-tidy-plan.md#phases).
   - No façade: there are no long-lived re-exports. Each extraction rewrites its consumers in the same change, and `retired_api_gate` forbids the old `tcl_compiler::` path from then on.
   - Steps, in this order (the item numbers are not the order):
     - step 0, test dependencies: 246 for `tcl-compiler` (in parts 266, 267, 268), then 269–272 for the other crates whose dev-dependencies cascade;
     - step 1, the map: 247, then 273;
     - step 2, `tcl-source`: 251, then 281;
     - step 3, `tcl-names`: 248; item 77's redesign (274) follows after Now 5 (D-D revised);
     - step 4, `tcl-values`: 280, item 81's value kernel, which binding needs, so it moves before binding and before the rest of the analysis move;
     - step 5, `tcl-model`: 249, 275, 276, 277;
     - step 6, `tcl-ir`: 252;
     - step 7, binding against lowering and SSA: 278 (decided: inversion), 250, 279;
     - step 8, `tcl-binding`: 253, then 282 (no `tcl-invocation` crate in this split);
     - step 9, `tcl-lowering`: 254;
     - step 10, `tcl-analysis`: 255;
     - step 11, `tcl-native-plan`: 256, then 283;
     - step 12, `tcl-diagnostics` and `tcl-optimiser`: 257, 284, 285;
     - step 13, the unit crate (`tcl-unit`) and the database: 258, 286, 287;
     - step 14, caching: item 495.
   - Each step has its own design in [`crate-split/`](../../design/compiler/crate-split/), and steps 0–14 are all written. Each builds on the ledger and the measurements the step before it left.
   - Alongside: item 261 splits `tcl-registry` into a core plus surface crates, and items 117 and 118 shrink it. It sits under every one of these crates. Item 117b, the evaluation half (the `value_transfer` and `resolved_invocation` analysis driver into `tcl-analysis`), lands after item 255, because until then the analysis layer is inside `tcl-compiler`'s cycle. The rest of the workspace (288–295 and 314–318) and the other build-time levers (296–300) have their own designs under `docs/design/workspace/` and `docs/design/build-time/`.
   - Last, step 14 (item 495): caching, limited to the caches the split leaves. Every memo row gets a disposition ([step 14](../../design/compiler/crate-split/14-caching.md)): a constant, deleted, an input or state. Three caches (binding per file, source words, retained modules) may become queries of the one database at the top (item 286's `tcl-db`), and only if deleting them costs more than 5% on the latency gate. The per-body caching tiers (items 101 and 103, and 46, 47, 49 and 73; 48 is merged into 495) are not part of this item: they come after items 83 and 84 (stages 10–11), per [caching](caching-design.md#plan) step 4. Item 85's `tcl-semantic` crate, between `tcl-unit` and `tcl-diagnostics`, lands its API boundary in Now 7 (D-C) and its model in phase 6.
   - Measurement record: each crate's first green build records, before and after, the analysis latency on the analysis-performance corpus (cold, and after a one-line edit) and the VM benchmarks in `scripts/perf/interp/`, under the `release` benchmark profile through item 106a's harness, in CI's benchmark job and never in agent worktrees (opt-level-1 test builds and the `ci` profile lose cross-crate inlining without LTO, so they do not measure it), beside item 259's build cost. The thresholds are item 106's table. Each extraction step's "done when" includes it.
   - Done when:
     - Items 246–258, 266–273, 275–287 and 495 are completed (274 completes with item 77, stage 5).
     - Item 259's isolated edits each compile only their dependency cone, and each cone's crates compiled, peak RSS and target-directory growth are at or below the targets the split's design records.
     - The memo gate finds zero `Mutex`, `OnceLock`, `thread_local!` memos or `Box::leak` outside `tcl-db`.
     - Release build time and the `tcl-lsp-server` native and WASM binary sizes are recorded before and after each split in the analysis-performance suite, and neither grows by more than 5%.
     - The measurement record shows analysis latency and the VM benchmarks no more than 5% slower than before the split.
   - 26 November 2026 (deep review W15): in progress. Of step 0, items 246, 266 and 268–271 are completed, 267 is reopened for the test rewrite (W1), and 272 is being finished; decisions 278 and 282 are made.
91. **[completed]** Third-party adoption by payoff: `imbl` and `slotmap`, `cargo-deny` wrappers, `insta` with `libtest-mimic`, a time-boxed `ascent` trial, `rowan`/`cstree` only if structured words show the need; replace hand-rolled generic code (AES/OpenPGP first, then line index, URIs, ANSI, JSON escaping, base32, punycode, CRC/Adler, similarity, DOT), each behind its stated condition.
   - Conformance: `rust/tcl-cmd-core/src/base32.rs#each_encoding_is_the_packages` and `rust/tcl-cmd-core/src/base32.rs#decode_answers_only_a_canonical_encoding`.
   - Phase 1: AES-CFB uses RustCrypto, CRC/Adler use library implementations, and similarity uses strsim. The namespace publication ledger uses `imbl` for persistent ordered entries. The `insta` snapshot test passed inside the `libtest-mimic` conformance harness. Base32 uses `data-encoding` with Tcllib's error and padding rules. Tcllib fixtures passed on four pinned releases, and the old and new Base32 implementations agreed over 1,712,384 input shapes. OpenPGP armor uses `base64`. Its old and new decoders agreed on bytes and exact error messages across 242,183 inputs. Native GnuPG fixtures, WASM type checking, Clippy and lockfile checks passed.
   - `glomar-pgp` owns binary header and length decoding, session-key derivation, CFB and authenticated decryption. The UCS adapter assembles short first partial chunks and preserves the existing format limits, public errors and `flate2` ZIP/ZLIB behaviour. The depth control accepts 17 wrappers and refuses 18. The replacement matched 1,134 round trips, 207 truncation cases and 338 compression cases, including GnuPG fixtures. Browser and WASI checks passed, including maximum advertised lengths executed under WASI. The candidate dependency graph passed the repository policy. The three consuming workspace graphs reported only the existing `chrono` advisory.
   - The `slotmap` trial rejected stale keys natively and under Wasmtime. Sparse and dense iteration both changed allocation order after retirement, so neither replaces the ordered candidate stores directly. `line-index` matched normalised LF/CRLF input in both builds. It counted a lone CR in raw document text as a column and takes UTF-8 text, while the current owner also indexes arbitrary native bytes and repairs incrementally edited snapshots. The `tcl::idna` registry entry declares the Tcl package surface. A Rust Punycode implementation would add behaviour to that surface.
   - URI parsing and decoding use `fluent-uri`, and item 152 routed callers through the shared owners. `fluent-uri`'s forward encoding matched the component and path policies over every Unicode scalar and 512 byte spellings. `tcl-platform` retains forward escaping to preserve its dependency-free contract.
   - The `cargo-deny` wrapper now audits every tracked workspace before returning failure. With the pinned `cargo-deny` and cached advisory database, all 12 workspaces passed the bans, licence and source checks. Six reported the existing `chrono` advisory RUSTSEC-2026-0543, and the wrapper exited with status 1 after checking all 12.
   - Ascent trial: Ascent 0.8.1 and the existing caller-frame escape worklist agreed on named and unbounded escapes for 21,808 frame results across 80 deterministic call graphs. Five-run medians were 76 ms for the worklist and 72 ms for Ascent natively, and 105 ms and 73 ms under Wasmtime. The browser target also type-checked. Keep the worklist in production until the phase 6 call-edge or taint workload can establish a benefit on real inputs.
   - The DOT trial with `petgraph` changed named node IDs and parallel-edge output. The ANSI trial changed grouped escape sequences and precedence for explicit colour settings and environment overrides. Both producers still use their current emitters. The structured-words work in phase 2 determines whether to trial `rowan` or `cstree`.
   - Registry snapshots and BIG-IP JSON string output use `serde_json` for the standard encoding and preserve each format's treatment of ASCII DEL. The focused comparison matched all Unicode scalar values, four registry snapshot streams and both BIG-IP graph goldens. Registry native Clippy, browser-WASM type checking and the host lockfile checks passed.
   - Done when: `deny.toml` admits `imbl`, `slotmap`, `insta` and `libtest-mimic` and the native and WASM builds pass with them, and `rg` finds no hand-rolled AES or OpenPGP code. The existing crypto tests pass on the replacement. The `ascent` trial's outcome and each remaining replacement's condition (line index, URIs, ANSI, JSON escaping, base32, punycode, CRC/Adler, similarity, DOT) are recorded, with the replaced code deleted.
