**Order:** the phase order, the structural decisions that make the designs one design (binding and value fixpoint, `Frame` versus name world, one per-site record, identity types, summaries versus contracts, the answer type and receipts, the potential-execution owner, native linearisation recipes) and the dependencies behind them are in [design integration](design-integration.md); it supersedes the order previously given here.

Validation spikes, phase 0, before the phases that rely on them ([design integration](design-integration.md)):

92. **[completed]** [Measured per-point binding-state memory and time](handover/name-world-cost.md) on the 687-line file and the analysis-performance corpus. The medium case met both budgets, so phase 3 can retain the current representation.
   - Done when: the spike reports retained memory and analysis time with and without per-point `ModuleCommandBindings` on the 687-line file (the "medium" case in `caching-perf.out`) and on the analysis-performance corpus. Pass: per-point states add at most 10% to analysis time and at most 25 MB to retained memory on the 687-line file. Fail: the design moves to sparse deltas or `imbl` before phase 3.
93. **[completed]** Annotated commands for nested sites. All 16 marked sites have records under Tcl 8.6.18, 9.0.4 and 9.1.0, and those records agree with Tcl wherever an existing consumer does. [Probe results and reproduction](handover/oo-dispatch-probe/annotated-frame-commands.md)
   - Done when: a probe builds the annotated command for every reached command in `frames/` (f01–f14, g01, g02), nested ones included, from today's binding, and compares its command answer with `frames.out` and tclsh. Pass: every `;# CALL` site has a record and matches tclsh wherever an existing consumer does. Fail: any reached site has no record, and the missing sites are listed.
94. **[completed]** (24 November 2026: the five original cases converged in one round with zero re-bound sites. A computed-level companion converged in two rounds and re-bound three sites. Fresh and incremental sealed site bytes matched in all eight cases. See the [spike results](binding-value-fixpoint-spike.md).) Binding and value fixpoint: run the bounded loop on the `oo-dispatch-probe/dynamic/` dynamic-head, `upvar $n` and `uplevel $lvl` fixtures; count iterations and re-bound sites.
   - Done when: the bounded binding and value loop runs on the `dynamic/` dynamic-head, `upvar $n` and `uplevel $lvl` fixtures (`e01`, `e03`, `e04`, `e21`, `e18`) with iteration and re-bound-site counts recorded. Pass: every fixture converges within the fixed limit (four iterations), and fresh and incremental runs give byte-identical sealed realms. Fail: any fixture hits the limit or differs between the two runs.
95. **[completed]** Per-body salsa query: move W210 for one body unit onto a per-body query and measure re-analysis after a one-line edit.
   - Done when: W210 for one body unit runs as a per-body salsa query, and a one-line edit inside one procedure of the 687-line file is measured. Pass: the salsa event log shows only that body's W210 query re-executed, and re-analysis takes at most 50 ms, against 1.27 s for the whole file today. Fail: any other body re-executes or the bound is missed.
   - On 24 November 2026, replacing one line in `IsOverridden` re-executed 1 of 17 procedure W210 queries in each of five trials (37.37–43.97 ms), with the same warnings and spans as fresh whole-file analysis. A separate edit that retained the nested return crossed 50 ms. [Measurements and reproduction](caching-design.md#per-procedure-w210-spike).

Architecture review of the earlier decisions together with the new design ([architecture review](architecture-review.md)):

96. **[completed]** (23 November 2026: every unit tagged in [`catalogue-remap.tsv`](catalogue-remap.tsv): 1,174 deferred (704 VM and runtime, the rest BIG-IP and iRules), 539 keep, 360 retarget to a phase owner, 85 delete with their phase; of the 570 adopted units, 272 keep, 207 retarget, 39 are deleted by a later phase and 52 are deferred. About 30 close calls between keep and retarget are marked "unsure" in their reason and are re-checked when their phase starts) (owner decisions, 23 November 2026) **Catalogue: freeze and re-map.** No new admissions (the ledger stopped at admission 35, with 570 of 2,158 units adopted in source and none verified). Tag every unit *keep*, *delete with phase N* or *re-target to the phase-N owner*, and never migrate a caller onto an owner scheduled for deletion; the 570 adopted units are kept or marked for deletion. The catalogue then cross-checks the phases instead of driving them. **Release: no target.** `work` is a long-running design branch; fixes reach users through `rust`. Done when the catalogue carries a tag on every unit, the tags agree with the phase lists in the working order, and no plan text directs work by catalogue unit.
97. **[designed]** One positioned record: `SourceInvocationBinding` evolves into the annotated command; `invocation_at_source` drops its unused `head` parameter (403 call sites); the roughly 30 positioned command, namespace and variable queries become facets of that record keeping their authorities.
   - Done when: `invocation_at_source` takes only `offset`, its 403 call sites compile, and `rg` finds none of the named positioned queries (`source_logical_command_candidates_at`, `source_class_proc_call_at`, `command_is_absent_at` and the rest) as public functions, each now a facet of the annotated command. The `name-validation/` and `frames/` definition and hover rows stay green.
98. **[designed]** Authorities and receipts capped: one fixed authority enum (Display, Logical, Native, normal completion, Edit; `Authority` replaces `NamingAuthority`: Diagnostic→Display, Navigation→Logical, Runtime→Native), one receipt type per authority with a shared currency key replacing per-module custody checks; a Display authority for written-word fallbacks used only by display features; a gate requiring a manifest reason for any new `Original*`/`Logical*`/`Native*`/`Source*` type (2,079 today).
   - Done when: one `Authority` enum with exactly Display, Logical, Native, normal completion and Edit exists, with one receipt type per authority sharing one currency key, and `rg` finds no per-module custody check. An xtask gate fails on a new `Original*`, `Logical*`, `Native*` or `Source*` type with no owner-manifest reason; its seeded test proves this, and the count (2,079 today) only shrinks.
99. **[designed]** Contracts by question, with evidence: reorganise `name-handling.md` (4,611 lines), the consumer matrix and the owner manifest by question rather than consumer; delete per-consumer sections as consumers migrate; tie each remaining claim to a vector row or test; retire the string candidate family's canonical status and oracle-less proof records as acceptance evidence; assign one owner per phase for cross-cutting changes.
   - Done when: `docs/design/contracts/name-handling.md`, the consumer matrix and the owner manifest are organised by question with no per-consumer sections. The contract-evidence gate (item 76) fails on any behavioural claim that links to no vector row or test, and passes on the tree. The string candidate family is no longer marked canonical in `command-resolution.md`.

Editor actions and tool equivalents ([editor actions](editor-actions-design.md); benchmark and fixtures in `oo-dispatch-probe/editor-actions/`):

100. **[designed]** (lead item for navigation after `source`, shared with items 67, 136) (merged in part: refusals with a reason (item 78's outcome type) is done once under item 148) Fix rename's precision now (release scope): `source` with a literal or evaluable path (including `[file join [file dirname [info script]] …]`) imports the sourced file's exports instead of suppressing navigation in the whole file; rename coverage and collision checks scoped to the files and bodies that can see the identity, so an unrelated workspace file or unknown effect no longer refuses every rename; `prepareRename` returns the refusal reason instead of `null`.
   - Done when: `editor-actions/rename_reason.py` on workspace B shows definition of `[compute 5` in `main.tcl` (with the `[file join [file dirname [info script]] …]` line) and in `literal.tcl` returning the declaration. Rename of `::util::scale` returns edits in both files, and `prepareRename` on any refused case returns an error with its reason instead of `null`. On workspace A, renaming `compute` succeeds although `http.tcl` and the other library files are in the workspace.
101. **[designed]** (merged in part: caching is done once under item 90) Separate binding and full-pipeline tiers per body unit: editor point and collection queries read only binding facts (annotated commands, name world, places, exports) and never wait for SSA/SCCP/diagnostics; a one-line edit re-binds one body unit (today the whole file: 12.5 s for 5,451 lines).
   - Done when: a server test on workspace A records salsa query execution after a one-line edit to `http.tcl` and shows only the edited body unit re-bound, with no SSA, SCCP or diagnostics query run before `definition` and `prepareRename` answer. `rename_bench.py` measures that definition at 100 ms or less (13 s today).
102. **[designed]** Positioned lookups through an interval index over each body's annotated commands, so point queries cost no more on a large file (today 1.6–3.4 s per request on `http.tcl` after analysis).
   - Done when: `rename_bench.py` measures definition, hover and `prepareRename` on `http.tcl` after analysis at 50 ms or less at p95 (1.6–3.4 s today). The same request on the six-line file of workspace C and on `http.tcl` differs by less than 2×.
103. **[designed]** (merged in part: caching is done once under item 90) A persistent workspace tier: per-file declarations, exports and reverse-index contributions from the binding tier, cached on disk by content hash, dialect and pack key; the cold scan builds only that tier (today 58 s for 10,513 lines); requests never re-analyse other files (today 20 s for requests on a 15-line file in that workspace); incremental inventory refresh instead of a rescan on rename.
   - Done when: `rename_bench.py` on workspace A measures the cold scan at 5 s or less (58 s today) and a restart from the on-disk tier at 1 s or less. Requests in `main.tcl` take 300 ms or less (20 s today), and the server log shows no other file analysed. A rename triggers no `refresh_stale_complete_host_inventory_for_rename` rescan.
   - Deep review (W14, F2, 26 November 2026): the tier persists binding's summary-free tier only (D-E), and uses the image cache's directory under `tcl-userdirs`, its key scheme (including `reader_build`) and its bounds (30 days, 256 MiB shared). Stage 13; it gates nothing earlier.
104. **[designed]** (the tier plan in `tcl-session`, the tiers run in `tcl-ide-edit`; after 290) One action engine over a session (workspace root plus overlays) used by the LSP server, MCP and the CLI; the server's rename tiers move into it; MCP keeps sessions instead of a fresh single-file analysis per call; the agreement harness requires identical edits or refusals across surfaces.
   - Done when: the agreement harness runs rename, references and the refactors through the LSP server, MCP and the `tcl` CLI on `editor-actions/workspace/` and `rename-alias/editor-rename/`, and gets byte-identical edits or identical refusal reasons. `rg` finds the server's rename tiers only in the action engine crate. An MCP call sequence on one workspace analyses each file once, as its session log shows.
105. **[designed]** (merged in part: refusals with a reason (item 78's outcome type) is done once under item 148) Cancellation, `workDoneProgress` and post-edit verification: superseded requests cancelled through salsa; progress for workspace renames and scans; every rename re-binds the edited body units and checks that each edited site now names the renamed identity and nothing else changed target.
   - Done when: a server test sends `didChange` during a workspace references request and gets `RequestCancelled` (-32800) within 100 ms, and a workspace rename emits `$/progress` begin and end. A seeded rename whose edit would make another site resolve to a different identity is refused with that reason by the post-edit check.
106. **[designed]** (the single budget table, deep review F11: items 306, 416, 483, 495 and item 90's measurement record cite it and do not restate it; its harness is 106a, in Now 1; this item's CI gate stays in stage 13) Action budgets as gates: the editor-actions workspaces in the analysis-performance suite, under the `release` benchmark profile in CI's benchmark job, never in agent worktrees. The table:
   - point query at most 50 ms p95 warm; point query at most 100 ms after a one-line edit (the W210 spike's 50 ms is a per-procedure target inside it); collection query at most 300 ms on 10,000 lines; local transform at most 100 ms;
   - cold: 103's binding tier at most 5 s for 10,513 lines and 1 s from the on-disk tier; summaries at most 300 ms per 10,000 lines, parse and binding excluded (483); tcllib 2.0 end to end recorded, with its target set from 106a's baseline;
   - server start within 10% of the time and resident memory 306 records today (306, 416); 50 user packs under 50 ms warm (416);
   - resident memory per 10,000 lines of open workspace (F5): recorded by 106a from the Now-1 baseline (step 14's probe gives 230 MB after 40 documents today), and no regression against it;
   - engines and artefacts (F12): `scripts/perf/interp` and analysis latency no more than 5% slower per split step, and the `tcl-lsp-server` native and WASM binaries no more than 5% larger (item 90); each VSIX, the JetBrains zip, the web bundle and the WASI module at most 10% over 306's base across 307; the VM's 9.1 move (312) and the crate moves (291) record against the same rows;
   - a partial-wave answer is provisional and excluded from the measurement (D-E).
   - Done when: the editor-actions workspaces run as a CI benchmark on release builds and fail the job when a class misses its target: point query at most 50 ms p95 warm, collection query at most 300 ms on 10,000 lines, local transform at most 100 ms, point query at most 100 ms after a one-line edit. The job passes on the tree.
106a. **[in progress]** (Now 1, beside 259; deep review F1) 106's benchmark harness without its CI gate: the editor-actions workspaces and tcllib 2.0 run under the `release` benchmark profile in CI's benchmark job (or on the tank runner), recording every row of 106's table, so each split step reports latency against one baseline. It runs alongside and blocks no step; regressions against it gate later stages, and 106's absolute gate comes in stage 13 with 101 and 103.
   - Done when: the harness runs on the current tree, its numbers are recorded in [build-test-cost](../build-test-cost/current-state.md) as the Now-1 baseline, and item 90's measurement record and item 483 read them.
   - Harness implemented in `scripts/perf/split_baseline.py`, with the manual non-gating `split-baseline` mode of `perf.yml`. Its deterministic small, 10,000-line and cross-file workspaces and verified Tcllib 2.0 run under `release`; the release CI baseline records all 51 rows in the single build-cost snapshot, with 29 measured and 22 explicitly unavailable. Invalid navigation and scan-timeout samples supply no percentile. Tcllib is infrastructure-unavailable after three runner shutdowns before upload; a healthy-runner remeasurement is required to set its end-to-end target. Owner decision, 27 November: keep scripts/CI scope and explicitly record unavailable summary/pack interfaces and unbuilt artefacts, rather than adding Rust instrumentation or package builds.
   - 27 November 2026 review: reopened. The 10,000-line workspace's scans time out at 600 s and its editor rows were never reached; the cross-file workspace's definition, references and after-edit rows have 0/20 valid samples; neither is diagnosed. Tcllib's three exit-143 runner deaths are unproven as infrastructure. There is no stored machine-readable baseline (the JSON is a 90-day CI artefact) and `split_baseline.py` has no compare mode; the cases ran on different hosts. The after-edit row waits for diagnostics, not a point query, and memory per 10,000 lines is extrapolated from 6- and 9-line files. Remaining: fix and re-measure those rows, commit the baseline JSON with a compare mode on one pinned host, add a navigation-only after-edit row.

Remaining sprawl outside the earlier studies ([remaining sprawl](remaining-sprawl.md)):

107. **[completed]** (merged in part: BIG-IP event order is done once under item 111) The orphan-monitor lint, AS3 conversion and transitive iRule context read every reference in LTM pool and member rules through the parser from item 222. AS3 conversion carries the monitor list and required count where the target can express them and reports any loss. `explain-flow` orders the events through the registry, including client ClientHello before client handshake and the server-side TLS sequence. MCP's scaffold takes its SSL profiles from the registry (`bfc0ef87`), with PERSIST kept as an attachment alternative for eligible events. The resolution gate honours only its own attached waiver tag. The explorer's Shimmer and Taint views use the compiler's catalogue severities.
   - Verification: focused regressions cover quoted and compound pool and member rules, AS3 rule loss, TLS order, side-specific MCP profiles, waiver isolation and the explorer's S102 and T100 severities.
108. **[designed]** (re-scoped by the deep review, W10: the editors read the catalogue at run time, as item 413 lists pack codes, so no TypeScript or Kotlin catalogue is generated) Diagnostic severity and category in the registry's diagnostic catalogue, which the editors read from the server at run time; producers stop choosing severity by code string; one `Severity` type; one category vocabulary; the BIG-IP query WASM, Spec Studio samples and explorer views go through the diagnostic policy.
   - Done when: the registry's diagnostic catalogue carries severity and category, the VS Code and JetBrains clients read them from the server at run time, and `rg` finds no producer choosing severity by matching a code string. `rg 'enum Severity'` returns one definition. An agreement test shows S102 and taint at the same severity in the LSP, `tcl diag`, MCP, the explorer, the BIG-IP query WASM and Spec Studio samples, with noqa honoured in each.
109. **[designed]** (owner `tcl-session`, created by 290 in Now 5; the CLI and MCP adopt it afterwards) One dialect-selection call (detector plus document, folder, ini and session precedence, lone-CR normalisation, filename tier) used by the LSP's open and closed paths, the CLI and MCP; the CLI and MCP read `dialect=` from the ini.
   - Owner crate (23 November 2026, superseded by `tcl-session` because 289 dissolves `tcl-lsp-core`): `tcl-lsp-core`. `tcl-lsp-server`, `tcl-cli`, `tcl-mcp` and `f5-cli` all depend on it, and it already holds `config_ini.rs`, which supplies the folder, ini and session layers. It also sees `tcl_registry::dialects::detect_dialect`, which stays the content and filename tier. `tcl-dialect` and `tcl-registry` are lower but cannot read the ini layers without pulling configuration down into them.
   - Done when: an agreement test opens `x.irul` containing `# tcl-lsp: dialect=tcl8.4` (once with LF, once with lone CRs) and gets Tcl 8.4 from the LSP open path, the closed-document workspace index, `tcl diag` and MCP. A project whose `.tcl-lsp.ini` says `dialect=tcl9.0` gives 9.0 for an undirected file in all four. `rg` finds no hard-coded `"tcl8.6"` default in `tcl-cli`.
110. **[designed]** (lead item for positions and ranges, shared with items 143, 144) Positions: one line rule for client-facing ranges (`new_lsp` semantics) wherever a range leaves the process; one position and range type (exclusive end, UTF-16) for every tool; delete the hand-written line-start and UTF-16 helpers; one documented unit convention for diagnostic JSON across CLI, MCP, explorer, report generator and the BIG-IP query.
   - Done when: `rg` finds no hand-written line-start helper and one `char_col_to_utf16` outside the position owner. A gate fails on `LineIndex::new` building any range that leaves the process. An agreement test on a file with a lone CR and `éé` before the diagnostic gets the same 0-based UTF-16 exclusive-end range from the CLI, MCP, the explorer, the report generator, the BIG-IP query and `f5-xc`.
111. **[in progress]** (lead item for BIG-IP event order, shared with items 107) (the address and path half is done (items 227–233); the event-order projections, monitor expressions and the graph copies are open) BIG-IP domain data from one owner: event order, event phases and profile requirements from the registry for `f5-cli`, MCP, the report generator, `f5-xc` and the VS Code skeleton; partition formatting from `tcl-bigip::object_names`; one BIG-IP object graph builder shared by `tcl-bigip`, `bigip-report-gen` and `tcl-bigip-query`. Decided by Q4 and Q8: `tcl-bigip::object_names` owns paths and partitions (absent partition rendered as `(no partition)`), `tcl-bigip::value` typed addresses, services and route domains with original spelling kept, the iControl REST layer owns `~` encoding; event order, phases and profile requirements come from registry projections; migrate the path helpers in `tcl-bigip` stats and conversion, `f5-cli` split and remote I/O, report model/APM/graph, query path and filter code, and the MCP and XC adapters ([BIG-IP names and addresses](bigip-addresses-design.md)).
   - Done when: `f5-cli` `explain_flow`, MCP `irule_gen`, the report generator's front end, `f5-xc` and the VS Code iRule skeleton take event order, phases and profile requirements from registry projections, checked by `--check` generation with `rg` finding no hand-written event list. `bigip-report-gen` and `tcl-bigip-query` build their object graph through `tcl-bigip`'s builder (one builder by `rg`). The monitor consumers pass item 107's tests. The address and path half is already done; the remaining migrations are deferred off this branch.
112. **[designed]** Tcl error messages from one set of builders in `tcl-cmd-core`/`tcl-value-syntax` (bad-value description, wrong-# args, return options) used by both engines and the analysis, checked against the tclsh error-message vectors.
   - Done when: the tclsh error-message vectors (wrong # args, a bad value that is a multi-word list, return options) run through the VM, the runtime and the analysis and match tclsh 8.4–9.1 byte for byte. `rg '"wrong # args'` in `tcl-vm` and `runtime/rust` finds only the shared builder in `tcl-cmd-core`/`tcl-value-syntax`.
113. **[designed]** (owner `tcl-session`, created by 290 in Now 5; the CLI and MCP adopt it afterwards) One configuration stack (global < editor < project < invocation) built by a shared function for the LSP, CLI and MCP; MCP gains the project layer and caches the global layer.
   - Owner crate (23 November 2026, superseded by `tcl-session` because 289 dissolves `tcl-lsp-core`): `tcl-lsp-core`. It holds `config_ini.rs` (`global_layer`, `project_layer_for`, `merge_settings`), depends only on `tcl-core-types` for it, and is the lowest crate that `tcl-lsp-server`, `tcl-cli` and `tcl-mcp` (and `f5-cli`) all depend on. `tcl-cli-support` is not a server dependency, so it cannot be the owner.
   - Done when: an agreement test with a global ini, a project ini and invocation flags gets the same merged settings from the LSP, the CLI and MCP through one `config_stack(…)` call, with MCP applying the project layer. A counting test shows MCP reads the global file once across ten calls. `rg 'fn invocation_layer'` returns one definition.
114. **[completed]** (merged in part: gates and waivers is done once under item 76) One waiver syntax and matcher for every xtask gate (tag-exact, comment-block scoped), with a test that each gate ignores the others' tags.
   - Done when: all xtask gates use one waiver matcher in `xtask/src/util`, tag-exact and scoped to the comment block. An xtask test places each gate's waiver tag next to every other gate's hit and asserts that only the matching gate is silenced; a `number-drift-ok:` waiver beside a `resolution_drift` hit fails the gate.
115. **[designed]** One tracing facade: environment flags read once into a typed configuration, `tracing`-style spans for timings, no direct `eprintln!` or `env::var` in library crates; editor clients take snippets and iRule skeletons from the server, and the editor READMEs' extension maps are generated.
   - Split:
     - 115a: environment flags read once into a typed configuration. Done when: `rg 'env::var\("TCL_LSP'` in library crates returns only the configuration module, and a test changing a flag after start-up has no effect until restart.
     - 115b: tracing facade for logging and timing. Done when: a lint fails on `eprintln!` or `Instant::now` used for timing in library crates (341 `eprintln!` in `tcl-compiler` today, spread over its successor crates after item 90) and passes on the tree, with timings emitted as `tracing` spans.
     - 115c: editor snippets and the iRule skeleton come from the server. Done when: VS Code's 16 static snippets and hand-written iRule skeleton are deleted, and a completion test in a `tcl8.4` file offers no `tcl-try` snippet.
     - 115d: editor README extension maps generated. Done when: the `gen_editor_*` generator writes the Neovim, Emacs and Helix README extension maps under its `--check` gate, and Neovim maps `irul` to the iRules language.

The registry ([registry review](registry-review.md)):

116. **[completed]** (lead item for Tk `destroy`/`bell` arity, shared with items 123, 129) Tk `destroy` accepts zero operands and `bell` accepts at most three. The release matrix checks observed Tcl, Tk and Jim usage against the registry's arity and synopsis, and compares C Tcl usage with TclVM and the standalone runtime. Differences require an exact reviewed entry. Tk and Jim have no project-engine execution environments.
117. **[designed]** (scheduled with item 90's crate split, 26 November 2026: it shrinks the crate every other crate rebuilds; [design](../../design/registry/registry-split/117-native-model.md)) Move the 58,000 lines of `native_*` interpreter algorithms out of the registry (the VM imports them in 164 files, 127 of them production), and the `value_transfer`/`resolved_invocation` analysis driver into the analysis layer; reconcile `project-layout.md` rule 3 with the shared-utility manifest. It runs in two parts:
   - 117a, in Now 2 (it can land at any time): the `tcl-native-model` crate beside `tcl-runtime-api`, below the registry core, takes the runtime-protocol half of `native_*` and the compile vocabulary;
   - 117b, after item 255: per-command semantics move into the surface crates, the trait stays in the registry core, and only the analysis driver goes to `tcl-analysis`.
   - Done when: `rg 'tcl_registry::native_' rust/tcl-vm runtime/rust` returns zero sites and `cargo tree -p tcl-native-model` contains no `tcl-registry` (117a). `rg` finds the `value_transfer` and `resolved_invocation` analysis driver only in `tcl-analysis`, with per-command semantics in the surface crates and the trait in the registry core (117b). `project-layout.md` rule 3 and the shared-utility manifest name the same owner, enforced by the crate-layer gate.
118. **[designed]** (scheduled with item 90's crate split, 26 November 2026: it shrinks the crate every other crate rebuilds; [design](../../design/registry/registry-split/118-bigip-schema.md)) A BIG-IP configuration-schema data crate, `tcl-bigip-schema`, for the 171,000 lines of `bigip/`, `base_objects/` and `profile_defaults/`, with `tcl-bigip`'s model generated from it and gated, and the 216 vocabulary comparisons the `registry-axes` ratchet counts in `tcl-bigip` (earlier text said 219) moved onto it. Decided by Q4: a dedicated configuration-schema data crate owning TMSH kind/property and section descriptors, reference-edge definitions, shipped base identities and profile-default snapshots, with `tcl-bigip`'s generic model generated from it plus explicit annotations. Q4 named `tcl-registry` as a second consumer, but it needs no normal edge to the crate, and neither do `tcl-lib-irules` and `tcl-lib-iapps`. `f5/` is not schema: it is the f5-tcl evidence model, which `tcl-compiler` reads in 20 files, and item 301 moves it. The 121k hand-written lines in `bigip/data/` stay Rust until a measurement says otherwise. It runs in two parts (review, 26 November 2026), so the build win does not wait for the generator:
   - **118a [completed]**, in Now 2 with the registry split: the data move. `bigip/`, `base_objects/` and `profile_defaults/` move into `tcl-bigip-schema` unchanged. The crate's role is `schema`: only `tcl-bigip`, `tcl-bigip-*` and `xtask` depend on it.
   - 118b, with item 119 at item 90's last step: `tcl-bigip`'s model generated from the crate (the recovered generator ported) and the 216 comparisons moved onto it.
   - Done when: `tcl-bigip-schema` holds `bigip/`, `base_objects/` and `profile_defaults/`, none of them remain in `tcl-registry`, and `cargo tree -p tcl-registry` lists no `tcl-bigip-schema` (118a). `tcl-bigip`'s model is generated from it under a `--check` gate, and `registry-axes` reports zero (216 today) vocabulary comparisons in `tcl-bigip` (118b).
119. **[designed]** (with 118b, at item 90's last step, Now 5) Generators in the repository with `--check` gates for every file marked generated (about 52,700 lines in the registry plus `tcl-bigip/src/model/gen/` have none); the SpecTcl equivalence packs' check in `xtask-check`; the report-generator WASM rebuilt by the build, not by hand. Decided by Q2 and Q3: rebuild base-object extraction from a versioned manifest of shipped files; derive reference edges from property descriptors; port profile-default extraction to Rust over the SCF parser; keep event properties and profile relationships as authored data with provenance and checks; recover historical mappings from `553b71b6dd` and `58633c7049`; vendor files and raw tables stay in private storage (no checkout path or build dependency in the project).
   - Done when: an xtask lint finds no file carrying an `@generated` header without a registered generator and `--check` in `xtask-check`, covering about 52,700 lines today. The SpecTcl equivalence-pack check runs in `xtask-check`. A CI step rebuilds the report-generator WASM and fails if the checked-in artefact differs.
120. **[designed]** (merged into 400/402, D32: the 3.0 `operation` row and effect rows; the remaining Rust work is deriving `CommandSpec`'s hooks and flags from them, in Now 6) One statement per fact in a spec: one operation field from which lowering, codegen, native-compilation and handler hooks are derived; one effect descriptor from which traits, `assigns_variable_at`, mutator flags and taint subcommand lists are derived (with item 80); the synopsis stated once; registry-internal name-keyed tables such as `value_transfer`'s `variable_words` derived from `arg_roles`; the `registry-axes` lint extended inside the registry.
   - Done when: `CommandSpec` has one `operation` field, with the lowering, codegen, native-compilation and handler hooks derived from it, and one effect descriptor from which the traits, `assigns_variable_at`, mutator flags and taint subcommand lists derive. `hover.synopsis` is deleted in favour of `forms`. The extended `registry-axes` lint scans `tcl-registry` and finds zero name-keyed tables such as `variable_words`.
121. **[designed]** Documentation complete in the registry: `clock format` and other specifier documentation moved from `tcl-lsp-core`; event prose with a completeness gate; spec-field documentation written once for rustdoc, the Spec Studio schema and help; MCP `command_info` for every dialect.
   - Done when: `rg --files rust | rg hover/format_specifiers.rs` finds nothing (deep review W9: `tcl-lsp-core` is split by 289), with the `clock format` documentation served from the registry. An event-prose completeness gate passes. Spec-field documentation is generated once for rustdoc, the Spec Studio schema and help. MCP `command_info` returns documentation for `lsort` under `tcl8.6`, `wm` under Tk and `HTTP::uri` under iRules.
122. **[designed]** Release cost: per-release facts in release-keyed tables instead of about 400 `V9_1` branches across 144 registry files (with item 79); a new command touches one file.
   - Done when: the release-table lint finds zero `V9_1` (or other release) match arms in the registry core and the dialect and library crates outside release-keyed tables (about 400 in `tcl-registry` today). A test adds a new command by one file, or one `.tclspec` row, in its dialect or library crate without editing a central list, and the command resolves (deep review W9: after 264 `tcl-registry` has no `commands/`).
Command-definition fixes (Tcl, Tk, bundled packages; each confirmed against tclsh 8.4–9.1 or Tk 9.0.3 and the analyser — evidence in [registry definition errors](registry-definition-errors.md), harness in `oo-dispatch-probe/definitions/`; iRules and BIG-IP definitions are reviewed separately):

123. **[completed]** (merged in part: Tk `destroy`/`bell` arity is done once under item 116) False errors on valid code — widen arity, with release windows where the command changed:
     - Tk `destroy`: minimum 0 (`commands/tk/destroy.rs`).
     - `http::formatQuery`: minimum 0, even count required from the bundled http 2.9 (8.6+), any count before (`commands/stdlib/http__formatquery.rs`).
     - `http::meta token ?headerName?` on 9.0+ (`http__meta.rs`).
     - `http::registerError sock ?msg?` (`http__registererror.rs`).
     - `tcltest::cleanupTests ?calledFromAllFile?` (`tcltest__cleanuptests.rs`).
     - 8.4 legacy `puts ?-nonewline? ?channelId? string` and `puts channelId string nonewline` (`commands/tcl/puts_.rs`).
     - TclOO `link` with no arguments (`commands/tcl/oo_link.rs`).
   - Done when: the definitions `probe.rs` cases for each listed call report no E002 in the releases where tclsh accepts them: `destroy`, `http::formatQuery` (even count from 8.6), `http::meta tok hdr` on 9.0+, `http::registerError sock msg`, `tcltest::cleanupTests 1`, 8.4 `puts` forms and `link`. Each release outside its window reports E002 where tclsh refuses it, and `compare.py` lists none of them as false arity.
124. **[completed]** Names accepted as commands that are not Tcl commands — remove or scope:
     - `tcl::mathop` and `tcl::mathfunc` (namespaces; `mathop.rs`, `mathfunc.rs`): keep as namespace documentation, not command specs.
     - Bare operator names (`+ - * / % ** ! ~ & | ^ << >> == != < <= > >= eq ne in ni lt le gt ge`): resolve only as `::tcl::mathop::*` or after `namespace path`/`namespace import` brings them in.

     - About 120 Tcl C test-binary commands (`noop`, `lstring`, `lgen`, `gettimes`, `test*`, source "Tcl test binary (tclTest.c)"): offer only to a `tcltest`-executable environment.
     - `memory` (only `TCL_MEM_DEBUG` builds), `registry` (Windows, needs `package require registry`), `bgerror` (application-defined handler: document, do not resolve as built-in), `next`/`self`/`my` outside method bodies.
     - Delete the uncompiled duplicate `commands/tcl/fcopy_.rs`.
   - Done when: the analyser reports an unknown command for bare `+ 1 2` and for `noop`, `memory`, `registry`, `next`, `self` and `my` outside method bodies, and stops reporting it once `namespace path ::tcl::mathop` is added. Completion offers none of `http`, `filename`, `disabled_in_irules` or `pkg_mkindex`. `commands/tcl/fcopy_.rs` is deleted, and `compare.py` lists no claimed-nonexistent rows for these names.
125. **[completed]** (merged in part: release rosters is done once under item 185) Commands offered in releases that lack them — add availability:
     - `unload` from 8.5.
     - `msgcat` by bundled version: `mcexists`, `mcforgetpackage`, `mcloadedlocales`, `mcpackageconfig`, `mcpackagelocale` from 8.6; `mcflset`, `mcflmset` from 8.5; `mcn`, `mcpackagenamespaceget`, `mcutil` from 9.0.

     - `oo::Helpers::classvariable`, `link`, `mymethod` from 9.0; `tcltest::bytestring` not in 9.0/9.1.
     - Package commands keyed to the package version each release bundles, not to "any Tcl".
   - Done when: the definitions `probe.rs` cases report the command unknown in releases that lack it and accept it where tclsh has it: `unload` on 8.4, the listed `msgcat` commands by bundled version, the `http` 2.10 commands before 9.0, `oo::Helpers` `classvariable`/`link`/`mymethod` before 9.0, and `tcltest::bytestring` on 9.0/9.1. `compare.py` reports zero claimed-nonexistent rows for them.
126. **[completed]** Match the exported TclOO metaclass methods. `oo::class` rejects direct `new` and `createWithNamespace` calls. Tcl 9.0 and 9.1 expose `new` on `oo::abstract`, `oo::configurable` and `oo::singleton` while keeping `createWithNamespace` unexported. The internal method descriptors remain available to class-body resolution.
127. **[completed]** Arity that changed between releases — add `arity_windows`:
     - `catch` (8.4: `script ?varName?`), `close` and `chan close` (direction from 8.6), `linsert` (8.4/8.5: at least one element), `lrepeat` (8.5: at least one element), `source` (`-encoding` from 8.5), `package require|present` (8.4: one version), `file delete`, `file mkdir`, `glob`, `global`, `variable` (no-argument forms from 8.6), `file stat|lstat` (variable optional from 9.0), `encoding convertfrom|convertto` (options from 9.0), `http::register` (4th argument from 9.0), `msgcat::mcpreferences` (arguments from 9.0), `unicode tonf*` (`-profile` arity).
   - Done when: the definitions `probe.rs` cases for every listed command give E002 exactly where tclsh 8.4–9.1 gives "wrong # args", with `arity_windows` in the specs (for example `catch {} a b` on 8.4, `lrepeat 2` on 8.5, `file stat f` on 9.0), and `compare.py` lists none of them.
128. **[completed]** Option-value pairs not modelled — use `Arity::stepped` or option grammars so odd counts are reported: `fconfigure`, `chan configure`, `fcopy`, `chan copy`, `clock add`, `clock format`, `clock scan`, `zlib gunzip`, `http::formatQuery`, `pkg::create`.
   - Done when: the definitions `probe.rs` cases give an arity or option error for an odd option-value count to each listed command, matching tclsh 8.4–9.1, and none for even counts. `fconfigure stdout -blocking` and the matching `chan configure` form query one option; the checks preserve that valid form.
129. **[completed]** (merged in part: Tk `destroy`/`bell` arity is done once under item 116) Wrong maxima or minima: `chan puts` (at most 3), `string compare` and `string equal` (at most 5), `string is` (at most 5 after the class), `string map` and `string match` (at most 3), `regexp` (at least 2), `if` (at least 2), Tk `bell` (at most 3).
   - Done when: the definitions `probe.rs` cases give E002 one past each corrected maximum (`chan puts` with 4 arguments, `string compare` with 6, `string map` with 4, Tk `bell` with 4) and one below each corrected minimum (`regexp x`, `if x`), matching tclsh and Tk 9.0.3, and `compare.py` lists none of them as missed arity.
130. **[completed]** Missing definitions: `tcl::tm::add`, `tcl::tm::list`, `tcl::tm::remove` (8.5+, `tm.n`), `http::mapReply`, `pkg_compareExtension`, 8.4 `tclLdAout`.
   - Done when: hover, completion and arity checks work for `tcl::tm::add`, `tcl::tm::list`, `tcl::tm::remove` (8.5+), `http::mapReply`, `pkg_compareExtension` and 8.4 `tclLdAout`. The definitions harness reports none of them as unknown in the releases tclsh provides them, and `tcl::tm::add` on 8.4 is reported unknown.
131. **[completed]** (merged in part: conformance rows is done once under item 75) The definitions gate runs Tcl and Tk 8.4–9.1, pinned Jim and the Tcllib `oo::util` and `struct::list` packages. It analyses each call independently, checks authored option and value cases, and fails CI and release tags for differences outside the exact reviewed allow-list.
   - Done when: `cargo xtask definitions-gate` runs `oo-dispatch-probe/definitions/` over tclsh 8.4–9.1, Tk and jimsh, one analysis per call, in CI, and passes against the reviewed allow-list. Reverting item 116's `destroy` fix makes the gate fail with a new false-arity row.
   - Verified 24 November 2026. The workflow first ran after its job-level `runner.temp` was moved to step `env`; all 13 lanes pass in CI (68,117 calls) once Jim's `os.fork` is skipped (probing it forked the probe and duplicated rows after `END`). Two engine-usage rows for `tcl::unsupported::assemble` alternate between `mismatch` and `unsupported` from run to run and carry both verdicts. With `destroy` reverted to `Arity::at_least(1)`, the Tk 8.4 lane reports `destroy 0 false-arity`, which no allow-list row covers.

Fixes found in the studies but not yet planned:

132. **[completed]** `var_escape` and `var_scoping` use the analysis's `ContextRegistry` for WASM slot eligibility and type inference under the selected dialect ([variable lookup](variable-lookup-centralisation.md)).
   - Conformance: `rust/tcl-compiler/src/type_infer/scope_alias_metadata_tests.rs#dialect_variable_writes_retain_the_selected_types_and_frame_policy`.
   - Phase 1: The selected registry supplies scope, escape and type facts. The regression verifies the inferred types and absence of WASM slots for Jim's `loop` counter and F5's `sharedvar` binding. The counter appears under the Jim profile, and all five pinned C Tcl releases reject `loop`.
   - Done when: `rg build_default rust/tcl-compiler/src/var_scoping.rs` returns zero sites and `var_escape`/`var_scoping` take the analysis's `ContextRegistry`. A test analyses a `jimtcl` and an `f5-irules` file whose variable is written by a command that exists only in that dialect, and gets the slot eligibility and type that dialect implies.
133. **[designed]** Memory SSA keys `Global`/`NamespaceVar` locations by the literal subject without qualification (`memory_ssa.rs`), so `global x`, `global ::x` and `upvar #0 ::x` can become different locations from the place binder's; key memory SSA by place ([validation](name-resolution-validation.md); inferred, add a fixture first).
   - Done when: a new fixture in `oo-dispatch-probe/name-validation/` (`v05-global-memory-ssa.tcl`) writes `::x` through `global x`, `global ::x` and `upvar #0 ::x` in one procedure and reads it back. A test asserts that memory SSA gives the three one `MemoryLocation`, equal to the place binder's `PlaceRef`, and the fixture's dead-store and W210 results agree with tclsh 8.6 and 9.0.
134. **[designed]** Two instance-variable collectors (the analyser's `analyser/oo/{itcl,snit}.rs` and lowering's `declarations/class_members.rs`) feed the LSP and dataflow separately; one collector from the object-system profile.
   - Done when: `rg` finds instance-variable collection only in the object-system profile, with none in `analyser/oo/itcl.rs`, `analyser/oo/snit.rs` or `declarations/class_members.rs`. On `bodies/17-itcl-method.tcl` and `bodies/28-snit-method.tcl` the LSP (completion, references) and dataflow see the same instance variables.
135. **[designed]** The runtime's `var_home` (`namespace/variable_tables.rs`) applies no 8.x global fallback while its `vars.rs` does; add a fixture and route both through the shared variable lookup bases.
   - Done when: `dynamic/e12-namespace-eval-set.tcl` and `dynamic/e29-namespace-eval-var-90.tcl` give the same output under the runtime's `run_script` as under tclsh 8.4, 8.5, 8.6, 9.0 and 9.1. `var_home` and `vars.rs` route through the shared variable lookup bases, with no second fallback rule found by `rg`. Deferred off this branch.
136. **[designed]** (merged in part: navigation after `source` is done once under item 100) Definition and references disagree after a `source` line: in a file starting `source [file join [file dirname [info script]] lib util.tcl]`, references to `::util::scale` find its declaration in the sourced file while definition finds nothing ([editor actions](editor-actions-design.md)); both must read the one binding answer (with items 67 and 100).
   - Done when: in `editor-actions/workspace/main.tcl` (first line `source [file join [file dirname [info script]] lib util.tcl]`), LSP definition on `::util::scale` returns its declaration in `lib/util.tcl`, the location references reports. A test asserts both come from the same binding answer.
137. **[designed]** The VM reports `domain error: argument not in valid range` for `if {NaN}`, where tclsh 8.6 and 9.0 report `floating point value is Not a Number` (observed on the VM build of 21 November; re-check on HEAD and add to the expression vectors).
   - Done when: `if {NaN}` (`constants/folds/e03-ifnan.tcl`) run under `tclvm --tcl-version 8.6` and `9.0` on HEAD prints `floating point value is Not a Number` as tclsh 8.6 and 9.0 do. The case is a row in the expression vectors, green for tclsh, the VM and the runtime.
138. **[not yet started]** (investigation not started) tclsh 9.0.4 and 9.1.0 segfault when `zlib` subcommands are called with dummy arguments in a child interpreter (the definitions harness, run unattended); reduce to a minimal reproducer, check the VM and runtime on the same input, and report upstream if it is in C Tcl.

The VM and the runtime run it without crashing. Either an upstream Tcl ticket ID is recorded in `registry-definition-errors.md`, or the cause is shown to be outside C Tcl.
139. **[in progress]** (fix `9a0dc3fbd3` is on `work`; re-verification after integration not done) Jim external-entry rebinding for O103 (the 99 → 42 wrong answer fixed by `9a0dc3fbd3`): re-verify after integration, as the coordinator handover requires, and keep the fixture in the execution-checked fold suite (item 65).

The `unknown_future_entry` fixture is a row in item 65's execution-checked fold suite, returning 99 before and after rewriting under all five tclsh releases and jimsh.
140. **[designed]** Frame housekeeping from [call frames](frame-design.md) step 7: rename the colliding frame types, and correct the contract and runtime model documents that cite `Vm::enter_level` and the non-existent `frame_depth_stash`.
   - Done when: `rg 'enter_level|frame_depth_stash' docs/` returns zero sites, and `rg` finds each frame type name defined once across `rust/`. The frame contract's claims link to `frames-semantics/` rows under the contract-evidence gate.
141. **[designed]** Packages step 1 remainder from [packages](package-design.md): use an absolute library path in the VM's `auto_path`.
   - Done when: `tclvm` run from a different working directory gives absolute entries for `file pathtype` on every `auto_path` element. `packages/p04-ifneeded-require.tcl` succeeds after a `cd` in the VM as under tclsh 8.6–9.1. Deferred off this branch.
142. **[in progress]** (owner decision, 23 November 2026) Extend W156 to signed values used as `class match` keys and cross-event `table` values.
   - Conformance: `rust/tcl-lsp-server/src/current_diagnostics/tests.rs#a_workspace_data_group_promotes_a_standalone_irule_signed_scan_hint`.
   - Phase 1 verified for `class`: The registry descriptor identifies the `class match` and `class search` item. W156 hints when the data group is unknown or ambiguous. A known integer key such as 129 after a signed byte scan promotes the finding to a warning. BIG-IP configuration validation and open configurations in the standalone iRule's workspace use typed keys from the same schema owner (items 111 and 118). The endpoint regression passed for conflicting declarations, edits, closure and stale report rejection.
   - **W304 owns the option terminator.** A leading `-` is certain once the operand is proved negative. A signed scan value can also be non-negative. Keep the terminator suggestion distinct from the unsigned-key finding.
   - **`table`: full, cross-event.** W156 follows a signed scan value written with `table set`/`add`/`replace` to comparisons on `table lookup` results in any event and connection, keyed by the table key (and subtable). This needs the session-state model of `table` keys across events (shared with the fakeCMP and `static::` work); `table incr` with a negative delta stays legitimate, and values remain unsigned 64-bit.
   - Done when (`class`): `class match` with and without `--` and `class search` hint for an unknown group. A known integer data group holding 129 promotes the finding after a signed byte scan. Cover both the configuration and standalone workspace data-group sources.
   - Done when (`table`, deferred): a `table set` in `CLIENT_DATA` read by `table lookup` in `HTTP_REQUEST` and compared with 0x81 reports W156. `table incr k -1` reports nothing.
143. **[completed]** (with items 110 and 151) Use the shared position converter for APL names and the native executable owner for Tcl shell discovery.
   - Phase 1: APL names now use LineIndex for zero-based UTF-16 columns while retaining scalar offsets. Native versioned Tcl shell lookup lives in `tcl-host-native`, with package and oracle callers using that owner. The APL range test covers `éé` and an astral character. The native lookup test finds `tclsh9.1` in a later PATH entry ahead of older names.
   - Split:
     - 143a: `tcl-bigip`'s `offset_to_line_char` documentation. Done when: the doc comment states 0-based UTF-16, and a unit test on a line containing `éé` asserts that convention, or the function is deleted in favour of item 110's range type.
     - 143b: duplicate interpreter lookup. Done when: `rg 'fn find_tclsh' rust/tcl-pkg` returns zero sites and `tcl-pkg` calls the production equivalent of `tcl_test_support::locate_tclsh` (item 151), whose tests find `tclsh9.1`.

More sprawl (second survey, [remaining sprawl](remaining-sprawl.md#second-survey)):

144. **[completed]** (with item 110) MCP diagnostic and fix ranges use UTF-16 like its edits. Explorer and CLI `diff` use byte columns under `startCol` and `endCol`.

   - Done when: an MCP test on `proc p {} { set s "éé"; return [expr {$s == "x"}] }` reports the `==` diagnostic at character 41 (UTF-16), the same unit as its edits. An agreement test shows the explorer's and CLI `diff`'s `startCol` in the same unit, with one documented key spelling per surface.
145. **[completed]** One `SourceEdit { span, text }` and one checked `apply_edits` (sort by `(start, end)`, refuse overlaps, refuse out-of-bounds) replacing `Refactoring::apply`, `minify.rs` `apply_edits` (offset/length), the CLI minimiser's `apply_edits` (start/end, panics) and `tcl-bigip-query`'s `splice_edits`; every refactor re-checks its result.

Native and logical inline-variable candidates share a post-edit parse and argument-shape check. All nine recipe-specific refusal tests passed. The wider refactor group reproduced 55 baseline failures. The two CLI rename-plan failures are recorded in the [tooling handover](handover/irules-registry-tooling.md).
   - Done when: `rg` finds no `Refactoring::apply`, no `apply_edits` in `minify.rs` or the CLI minimiser, and no `splice_edits`, all replaced by one `SourceEdit { span, text }` and `apply_edits`. Property tests show overlaps and out-of-bounds spans refused with an error, never a panic or a clamp. Every refactor has a test that a result failing its re-check is refused.
146. **[completed]** One workspace scan policy uses `SKIP_SCAN_DIRS` and `is_tcl_source` beside `TCL_SOURCE_EXTENSIONS` across the LSP, CLI, xtask, SpecTcl discovery, `tcl pkg discover` and Spec Studio. Package installation has separate CAS and fetch copies.

The fixture checks pruning and source classification alongside each caller's handling of manifests, installed dependencies, embedded examples and explicit files. The registry has one `SKIP_SCAN_DIRS` and one `is_tcl_source`.
   - Done when: a tree with `target/`, `tmp/`, `build/`, `dist/` and `.venv/` plus `.tk`, `.itk` and `.irul` files gives the same pruning and Tcl-source classification decisions across callers. Check purpose-specific outputs separately. SpecTcl finds packs and manifests, xtask also inspects embedded examples, package discovery excludes installed dependencies, and Studio accepts explicitly selected files. `rg` finds one `SKIP_SCAN_DIRS` and one `is_tcl_source`.
147. **[completed]** (merged in part: version parsing and comparison is done once under item 31) Release identifiers: delete the duplicate `TclVersion::dialect_name`/`dialect_profile_name` and the 28 `format!("tcl{}", …)` sites; one lenient `TclVersion::parse` for every version text (beta and alpha suffixes); `NativeCVersion` derived from `TclVersion`; the 39 release-named enums' mappings in one release table (with items 79 and 122).
   - Done when: `rg` finds no `dialect_name` or `dialect_profile_name` duplicate and no `format!("tcl{}"` site, and `NativeCVersion` has no hand-written mapping. `TclVersion::parse` accepts `8.6b1` and `9.1b0`. A test shows `package require Tcl 8.6b1` setting the 8.6 floor in the version gate as well as in detection.
   - Conformance rows: `rust/tcl-dialect/tests/data/package_version_oracle.txt` and `rust/tcl-dialect/data/release-facts.tsv`. The prerelease detection/version-gate case remains a completion requirement.
   - Phase 1: `TclVersion::profile_name_for` and `source_tree_name` own the release-to-profile and source-tree spellings; `rg` finds no `format!("tcl{}"` site in `rust/` or `runtime/` (the release-facts generator in `xtask` writes the table they read). `TclVersion::parse` accepts `8.6b1` and `9.1b0`, and `a_prerelease_package_require_tcl_sets_its_release_floor` shows `8.6b1` and `9.0b1` setting their release's floor in the version gate.
148. **[completed]** (lead item for refusals with a reason (item 78's outcome type), shared with items 36, 100, 105, 180) MCP failure shape: `isError` for real failures, a `refused` object with a reason instead of `null` or `{"error"}` inside a success result (with items 89 and 104); fact and diagnostic tools analyse the same normalised text.

151. **[completed]** (lead item for tclsh discovery, shared with item 143) Package discovery prefers `tclsh9.1` when it is installed under its versioned name. Native tools share one executable lookup with Unix execute-bit checks and Windows `PATHEXT` ordering. Release-specific witnesses, including the VM CLI, use the pinned oracle lookup. [Verification](current-state.md#item-151-verification)

152. **[completed]** Smaller items: one TSV fixture reader with escape decoding and one release key form for oracle data; route the remaining URI bypasses (`tcl-bigip-io` percent encoder, `strip_prefix("file://")` in `f5-cli` and `tcl-bigip`, the server's direct `Uri::from_file_path`) through `uri_norm`; audit the server's mixed `std` and `tokio` mutexes for locks held across `.await`.
   - Conformance: `rust/tcl-test-support/src/fixture.rs#tsv_rows_keep_empty_cells_and_decode_annotated_fields` and `rust/tcl-test-support/src/fixture.rs#oracle_keys_use_the_canonical_release_vocabulary`.
   - Phase 1: File URI encoding and host-path conversion use the platform owner. All 57 related Linux tests passed. A portable regression compares a pack registration built from the Windows document-key projection with the server’s `didOpen` normaliser. The production watcher builder passed the workspace test for the pack fixture, a verbatim drive path and a UNC path. The separate native Windows pack/open test still needs a Windows runner. The shared TSV reader handles raw cells, first and last partitions, bounded tails and explicit escape decoding. The fixture gate reports zero direct splits, zero noncanonical release keys and zero patchlevel directories. This pass moved 1,193 capture directories with 10,336 unchanged payloads. Replay verified the 246 naming-corner rows. The namespace callback and array/OO replay scripts still lack pre-existing receipt JSON files. The server denies `await_holding_lock`, and its [async mutex audit](../../design/contracts/lsp-transport-liveness.md#async-mutex-audit) records the reasons for the Tokio guard groups.
   - Split:
     - 152a: one TSV fixture reader. Done when: a ratchet counting hand-written TSV readers (about 300 in 184 files) reads zero, and the shared reader's tests decode `\t`, `\n` and `\\` escapes.
