Done when: `rg` finds no `strip_prefix("file://")` in `f5-cli` or `tcl-bigip`, no percent encoder in `tcl-bigip-io`, and no `Uri::from_file_path` in `tcl-lsp-server` outside `uri_norm`. A Windows-path test gives one document key for a pack registration and an opened file.
     - 152d: server locks held across `.await`. Done when: `clippy::await_holding_lock` is `deny` in `tcl-lsp-server` and passes, and each `tokio::sync::Mutex` guard held across an `.await` is listed with its reason in the audit.

Fourteen tricky areas of Tcl (summary, current-source status and cross-cutting themes: [audit summary](tcl-areas-audit.md); fixtures under `oo-dispatch-probe/`):

Wrong rewrites and engine wrong answers first ([audit summary](tcl-areas-audit.md); each confirmed on the current source):

153. **[completed]** Optimiser: the regex folds (O129) decline `regexp`/`regsub` with `-all` or `-start` when the pattern contains `\A`, `\m`, `\M`, `\y`, `\Y` or `\B` until the engine is fixed (`tcl-registry/src/value_transfer/regex.rs`); never forward or fold `errorInfo`/`errorCode` across an edge where a command may raise (`sv14`, `sv17`); never fold a call to a command carrying an execution trace (`tr20`, from the module's traced commands); O116 keeps `\x` escapes as escapes instead of writing raw characters (`e03`); treat `tcl::unsupported::representation` as observing representations so folds of its operand are declined or documented (`t07`). Verified all 49 [executed rewrite rows](handover/oo-dispatch-probe/tcl-areas-head/rewrites-executed.tsv) on Tcl 8.6.18 and 9.0.4. The targeted conformance tests also ran across Tcl 8.4–9.1.
154. **[in progress]** Regex engine: `RegexEngine::exec` (`tcl-cmd-core/src/regex.rs:321`) and `AreEngine` (`tcl-regex/src/cmd_core.rs:74`) match from the restart offset with no left context, keeping `notbol` for `^`; release-keyed REG_* error texts in `tcl-regex/src/defs.rs` with the copies in `tcl-cmd-core/src/regex.rs` and `runtime/rust/src/cmd_switch.rs` deleted; `lsearch -glob` and `string_match_bytes` match non-UTF-8 values by their own units. Done when `r10`, `r13`, `r14`, `r16` match tclsh 8.4–9.1 in both engines and the folder. The restart change has landed: `AreEngine` matches `cps[offset..]` under the caller's `REG_NOTBOL`, and `-start end` is the string length as in C. `r14` matches tclsh 8.4–9.1 in the VM and the runtime, and so does `r13` apart from `pref-3` on 8.4/8.5, a capture-preference difference. Past the end, spans keep the caller's offset (`{10 9}` for `-start 10`), and item 153's restart decline is lifted: O129 folds those forms, checked against tclsh 8.4–9.1. 8.4's `regexp` suppresses `^` at every positive restart offset, newline or not (`TclVersion::regexp_restart_after_newline_is_bol`). `r13`'s `pref-3` now matches too: 8.4/8.5 split `x{0,n}` into a prefix and a capturing final `x` (`TclVersion::regex_star_is_iteration`, `REG_SPLITSTAR`), 8.6 on cut an iteration by its operand's preference, and a backreference star settles its extent before the rest is tried. The error texts and the glob units remain.
155. **[designed]** (merged in part: VM silent exits and the runtime library is done once under item 30) VM crashes and silent exits: `scan`/`split` of a lone surrogate (`tcl-vm/src/value/string_rendering.rs:36` panics), `encoding system nosuch` inside `catch` (exit 1, no message), `interp cancel` on an idle child (exit 1), 9.1 `lseq` (exit 1), and the runtime's 9.0 NUL in `scan \x00 %c`. Done when `e05`, `e12`, `e15` (encodings), `i06` and the `lseq` fixture behave as tclsh in both engines.
156. **[designed]** POSIX and lookup error codes: route `open`, `cd`, `file`, `glob`, `exec` and channel failures through the `HostError` → `POSIX errno msg` mapping (`tcl-cmd-core/src/encoding/original_names.rs:177`) in both engines; the runtime reports `TCL LOOKUP VARNAME name` for failed reads and sets an errorCode for `$x`; `errorCode` survives `interp eval` in the runtime. Done when `e08`, `e19`, `e31`, `e32`, `sv06`, `sv16` and `i12` match tclsh 8.6/9.0.
157. **[designed]** Other engine wrong answers: VM TclOO `unknown` method dispatch (`tcl-vm/src/cmd_oo/method_resolution.rs:291`, `u10`); runtime `namespace import` calls `auto_import` (`u12`); `tcl_interactive`/`tcl_rcFileName` in file mode in both engines; VM 8.4 read-trace error escaping `catch` (`tr04`, `tr21`); `case` and the legacy `puts ch str nonewline` in 8.x mode; `scan %ld` beyond 64 bits; runtime `expr {0o10}` returning its spelling; 9.1 `cbrt` precision; runtime return-options key order and `-errorstack` frames; VM caught `thread::send` errors resurfacing from `vwait` (`t10`, `t11`); alias wrong-# args wording from 8.5 (`i15`); coroutine resume and `coroutine` usage texts from one owner (`NativeCoroutineResumeArity`). Done when each named fixture matches tclsh.

Traces ([traces](traces-design.md)):

158. **[completed]** (25 November 2026: `command_binding/trace_registrations.rs` owns the trace facts: each statement's `TraceRegistration`s come from `TraceTransition` once, feeding `var_observability`'s per-point `TracedNames` (the `TRACED` flag is deleted) and the module projection (`populate_trace_facts`, moved from lowering) with its name-key policy (`variable_observation_names.rs` deleted). `outcome_defs`, taint and GVN ask `variable_may_be_traced` / `command_may_be_traced`; the four refactor checks ask `traits_may_register_trace`; `retired-api-gate` retires the old spellings and owns `ESTABLISHES_VARIABLE_TRACE` and `traced_commands.contains`. The `traces/` rows are unchanged. Left for later items: keying registrations by resolved place and `CommandRef` instead of root-free text, and moving the remaining direct `traced_variables.contains` readers onto the owner) One trace fact owner in binding: per-point trace registrations in the name world keyed by place and `CommandRef`, built from `TraceTransition`; `var_observability`'s `TRACED` flag, `variable_observation_names`, `outcome_defs::is_traced`, GVN's check and the four refactor checks become views or go.
   - Done when `var_observability`'s `TRACED` flag, `variable_observation_names`, `outcome_defs::is_traced` and GVN's own trace check are deleted or are views over the binding trace owner, a `cargo xtask retired-api-gate` entry fails if any of them returns, the four checks in `tcl-lsp-core/src/refactor` call the owner, and the `traces/` rows `tr01`–`tr23` in `tcl-areas-head/analyser-optimiser-head.tsv` are unchanged.
159. **[designed]** Trace firing as a call: each callback lowered as a deferred body unit whose frame-effect summary applies at every access or call that may fire it, so folds decline exactly where a callback may write and proceed elsewhere (`tr02`, `tr03`, `tr14`, `tr15`, `tr20`, `tr22`, `tr23`).
   - Done when `tr02`, `tr03`, `tr14`, `tr15`, `tr20`, `tr22` and `tr23` run the same under tclsh 8.6 and 9.0 before and after `tcl opt` (no `DIFF` row in `rewrites-executed.tsv`, `tr22`/`tr23` `.optimised.tcl` deleted), with the decline coming from the callback's frame-effect summary, and a unit test shows a trace whose callback writes nothing still lets a fold of another name go ahead.
160. **[designed]** (lead item for W214 on fixed-signature callbacks, shared with items 12, 176) W210 reads the registration's operations (a write-only trace does not make a later read defined; `trace add variable y write cb; puts $y` is reported); W214 reads the registry's appended callback arity for every trace callback (`tr02`–`tr04`, `tr13`); references and rename include `trace info execution|command f` (`tr10`, after a current-source re-check).
   - Done when `trace add variable y write cb; puts $y` reports W210 and `tr13` does not, no W214 remains in `traces/analyser-mcp.out` (`tr02`–`tr04`, `tr13`), and MCP `find_references` on `f` in `tr10` returns six references, including the `trace info execution|command f` words, with `rename` editing all six.
161. **[designed]** (merged in part: the engine traits is done once under item 88) Engine trace contract in `tcl-runtime-api` (registration, removal, `trace info` order, firing order) implemented by both engines, with the 23 fixtures as conformance rows for every release (Jim rows are jimsh oracle and analysis rows only; dialect execution in the engines is out of scope on `work`, item 196).
   - Done when the trace contract (registration, removal, `trace info` order, firing order) is a `tcl-runtime-api` trait implemented by both engines, and the 23 `traces/` fixtures run as conformance rows in both engines' suites matching tclsh 8.4–9.1 (including `tr04` and `tr21` on 8.4), with the Jim rows checked against jimsh (`invalid command name "trace"`) by the analyser only.

Special and global variables ([special variables](special-variables-design.md)):

162. **[designed]** (merged in part: implicit writes reaching W210 is done once under item 22) W210 sees the error edge: binding's `materialise_error_storage` (`command_binding/compiled_preflight.rs:362`) defines `errorInfo`/`errorCode` on the error edge of `catch`/`try` bodies in SSA (`sv16`, `e19`); 9.x has no global fallback for unqualified reads in `namespace eval` (`sv10`).
   - Done when `catch {error boom}; puts $errorCode`, line 2 of `sv16` and `errors-returns/e19` give no W210, and `sv10` reports W210 under `tcl9.0` and not under `tcl8.6`, each pinned as an analyser test.
163. **[designed]** One host start-up for both engines from `NativeBootstrapProtocol` plus a shared init that sets `tcl_interactive`, `tcl_rcFileName`, `argv`/`argc`/`argv0`, `auto_path` and `tcl_library` (delete `set_argv`, fold `set_startup_globals`); release-gated `tcl_platform` keys (no `ebpf`, `runtime`, `wasi*`, `wasm` outside a test overlay; per-release key sets).
   - Done when `sv01`–`sv04`, `sv12` and `sv13` match tclsh 8.4–9.1 in both engines in a runner-generated `special-variables/engines.tsv` (bar host-path values), `tcl_platform` has no `ebpf`, `runtime`, `wasi*` or `wasm` key outside the test overlay, and `grep -rn 'fn set_argv\|fn set_startup_globals' rust runtime` returns nothing.
164. **[completed]** Compiler startup existence and editor special-variable queries use the active registry, with release-specific rosters and an ownership gate against pack-blind predicates.
   - Phase 1: All 17 registry startup tests passed with the five pinned Tcl releases, Jim 0.84 and a pinned Tcl/Tk 8.6.18 `wish`. The five editor hover and completion tests passed for selected-release availability and array keys. The compiler still reproduces the existing F5 `$argv` diagnostic failure.
   - Conformance: `rust/tcl-registry/src/special_vars/tests.rs#startup_oracle_rosters_come_from_the_selected_registry`.
   - Done when a gate fails on `special_vars::is_*` outside `tcl-registry`, the calls in `dead_stores.rs`, `unused_bindings.rs` and `existence_branches.rs` are gone with `special-variables/` diagnostics unchanged, LSP hover on `$tcl_precision` under 9.0 is empty, `$tcl_platform(` under 8.4 completes seven keys, `$tcl_` completes the release's roster, and the `sv01`/`sv02` rows are generated from the registry and match tclsh, wish and jimsh.
165. **[designed]** (lead item for `tcl_precision` as a whole-program fact, shared with items 63) Precision as a whole-program fact: the "program writes `tcl_precision`" fact from the name world instead of a byte search, failing closed when absent, and read by O100/O101 as well as value transfer (the case did not reproduce on the current source; keep `sv18` and the Tcl 9 page's case as regression rows).
   - Done when `sv18` and `tcl9-changes/t07` under 8.6 get no O100, O101 or value-transfer rewrite (`sv18.optimised.tcl` deleted, no `DIFF` row in `rewrites-executed.tsv`), a test with the precision fact missing shows the folds decline with a reason, and `grep -rn 'tcl_precision'` in the optimiser finds no byte search.

Coroutines ([coroutines](coroutines-design.md)):

166. **[designed]** (merged in part: per-site records for nested commands is done once under item 84) Created commands at nested sites: publish the command named by `defines_command_at` for invocations inside command substitutions as for statements (`command_binding`, `resolved_invocation/source_definitions.rs`), so `puts [coroutine c g]; c` is clean (`co01`, `co02`, `co03`, `co05`, `co10`–`co12`); definition and hover on a resume reach the `coroutine` invocation; resume arity (at most one value after `yield`) checked from a coroutine contract.
   - Done when `puts [coroutine c g]; c` (the discriminating pair in `coroutines/analyser-mcp.out`) is clean, `co01`, `co02`, `co03`, `co05` and `co10`–`co12` lose their W123s, LSP definition and hover on `c` in `co01` reach the `coroutine` invocation, and `c a b` against a body that only `yield`s reports a warning.
167. **[designed]** Generators are not W241: a `SUSPENDS_ACTIVATION` effect on `yield`/`yieldto` leaves the activation for the loop-exit rule (`analyser/bounds_checks.rs`); one registry query for "ends the activation" and one for "suspends" shared by `cfg_builder/flow_facts.rs` and `tcl-lsp-core/src/source_graph/sequence_capture.rs:139`.
   - Done when `co01` and `co05` report no W241 while `while 1 {incr i}` still does, and a registry unit test shows `cfg_builder/flow_facts.rs` and `source_graph/sequence_capture.rs` use the same "ends the activation" and "suspends" queries, listing `tailcall`, `return`, `yield` and `break`.
168. **[designed]** A coroutine entry context for the static `Frame` and suspension points in lowering and SSA (locals kept, globals and the name world invalidated across `yield`); one resume-arity owner for both engines; WASM coroutines implemented or refused at compile time as a recorded non-goal.
   - Done when `co04`'s `info exists ::outer` gives no W211, an optimiser test (executed before and after) shows a global written between resumes is not folded across `yield`, `co02` and `co11` match tclsh 8.6/9.0 in both engines from `NativeCoroutineResumeArity`, and a WASM compile of `co01` either runs or is refused with a diagnostic, recorded in `backend-constraints.md`.

Event loop and deferred code ([event loop](event-loop-design.md)):

169. **[in progress]** One deferral descriptor: `BodyExecutionSpec::DeferredGlobalScript` (entry frame and trigger) as the only stated fact, with `DEFERS_BODY`, `BodyKind::Structural`, `script_timing` and `script_body_flow` derived; `future_bodies.rs` walks `fileevent`, `bind` and trace callbacks as well as `after`; delete the uncompiled `commands/tcl/fileevent_.rs`.
   - Done when a registry test asserts every spec with `DEFERS_BODY` states a `BodyExecutionSpec::DeferredGlobalScript` and that `DEFERS_BODY`, `BodyKind::Structural`, `script_timing` and `script_body_flow` are derived from it, `future_bodies.rs` yields deferred bodies for `event-loop/ev09` (`fileevent`), `event-loop/ev13` (`bind`) and `traces/tr02` (trace callback), and `commands/tcl/fileevent_.rs` is deleted with the registry tests passing.
   - 25 November 2026: `future_bodies` already yields global future bodies for `fileevent`, `chan event` and Tk `bind` as well as `after` (`channel_and_tk_event_scripts_are_future_global_bodies`), and `commands/tcl/fileevent_.rs` is gone. A variable trace callback is not a deferred global body: tclsh 8.6 runs it at the accessor's level plus one, so `traces/tr02` belongs to item 159. `every_deferring_spec_states_a_deferred_body_execution` lists the 26 specs that carry `DEFERS_BODY` without a deferred descriptor (bodies run on a later call such as `proc` and `snit::method`, trace callbacks and Tk tag bindings). The list only shrinks; deriving the trait from the descriptor remains open.
170. **[designed]** (merged in part: implicit writes reaching W210 is done once under item 22) W210 and W220 read `DeferredWrites` at wait points (`vwait`, `update`), and deferred reads of names nothing writes are reported (`ev12`); the false W210/W220 rows did not reproduce on the current source, so `ev02`, `ev03`, `ev08`, `ev10`, `ev11` become regression rows.
   - Done when `ev02`, `ev03`, `ev08`, `ev10` and `ev11` give no W210 and `ev08` no W220 as analyser regression tests, `set x 1; after 0 {set ::y 2}; puts $y` with no wait still reports W210, and `ev12` reports the `puts $local` read while `ev07` stays clean.
171. **[designed]** (merged in part: the engine traits is done once under item 88) One event-loop driver in `tcl-cmd-core/src/event.rs` behind a host trait (`service_ready`, `process_one`, the `vwait` loop, background-error dispatch), shrinking both `cmd_event.rs` files to registration; `chan` and `fileevent` in the VM or a recorded non-goal; profile the debug VM's 35 ms event turn.
   - Done when both engines call the driver in `tcl-cmd-core/src/event.rs`, both `cmd_event.rs` files hold only registration and host glue, every `event-loop/` fixture matches tclsh 8.6/9.0 in both engines, `ev09`/`ev09b` pass in the VM or are marked expected-unsupported with the constraint recorded, and `ev16` matches tclsh in the debug VM or its cost is recorded in the VM performance notes.

Errors and return codes ([error handling](error-handling-design.md)):

172. **[in progress]** (the #2142 `finally` reachability fix is in the source; the registry completion model is not started) Completion codes from the registry: intercept and convert sets on the effect descriptors of `catch`, `try`, loops, `switch`, `eval`, `uplevel` and procedure calls, with `CompletionCodeLattice` replacing `flow_facts::Completion` and `try_construct.rs`'s special cases falling out of the model (keeping the #2142 fixtures green).
   - Done when the effect descriptors of `catch`, `try`, loops, `switch`, `eval`, `uplevel` and procedure calls carry intercept and convert sets, `flow_facts::Completion` is deleted in favour of `CompletionCodeLattice`, `try_construct.rs` has no `finally` special cases, and the #2142 fixtures, `errors-returns/e06` and `errors-returns/e27` still pass.
   - 25 November 2026: `flow_facts::Completion` is deleted. The CFG's flow facts carry a `CompletionCodeLattice` read from each statement's registry completion route (`TCL_OK` falls through, `break`/`continue` jump to a loop, a process exit contributes no code), branch completions join, and the lattice's `may_complete_normally`, `may_jump_to_loop` and `leaves_procedure` replace the three-state enum. The CFG, optimiser and analyser test sets fail exactly as before. Intercept and convert sets on the effect descriptors and the `try_construct.rs` `finally` cases remain open.
173. **[designed]** (merged in part: implicit writes reaching W210 is done once under item 22) Diagnostics from that model: `break`/`continue` reaching a procedure or script boundary (`e04`, `e12`, `e22`), custom codes reaching the top level, unreachable commands as a hint sharing O107's facts (`e14`); fix the `catch`-wrapped `for` W210 (`e25`) and the `catch` result-variable W210 (`e15`); 8.4's one-variable `catch`; one W002/W123 policy (8.5 `try`/`throw`/`lmap` give W002 only).
   - Done when `errors-returns/e04`, `e12` and `e22` report the boundary diagnostic, `e23` the custom-code one and `e14` the unreachable hint with the `Unnecessary` tag, no other `errors-returns/` fixture gets them, `e15` and `e25` give no W210, and the 8.4 and 8.5 rows of `errors-returns/analyser-mcp.out` agree (one-variable `catch` on 8.4; `try`, `throw` and `lmap` on 8.5 give W002 only).
174. **[in progress]** One owner for completion-code words, `trap` prefix matching and the return-option factories in `tcl-cmd-core` (replacing VM `cmd_try.rs:164`, runtime `cmd_error.rs:777`/`:790`/`:1150`, registry `completion.rs:54`/`:128`, `tcl-diagram/src/data.rs:633` and both `native_return_merge.rs` factories); one completion-code type (delete the runtime's `interp::Code`); retire the ~55 `Statement::Catch`/`Try` matchers onto the annotated command's intercepted codes with an only-shrinks ratchet; correct `break_.rs:57`'s documentation.
   - Phase 1 verified: `tcl-cmd-core` owns the completion type, named-code parsing, trap prefixes and literal return options. Runtime error, trap and option-storage tests exercised the migration. TclVM and the runtime matched pinned tclsh 8.6.18 and 9.0.4 on exit status, stdout and stderr for `e01`, `e05`, `e13` and `e21` in all 16 comparisons. The intercepted-code matcher migration and its ratchet are phase 5.
   - Done when (phase 1): one owner parses named completion codes, the runtime consumes the common `Code` type, and both engines call the common `trap` matcher and literal return-options factory. The `break` documentation reflects Tcl’s procedure-boundary behaviour. Run `errors-returns/e01`, `e05`, `e13` and `e21` against tclsh 8.6/9.0 and both engines.
   - Done when (phase 5): consumers read the annotated command’s intercepted codes, and the ratchet holds the shrinking `Statement::Catch` and `Statement::Try` matcher count.

`unknown` and autoloading ([unknown and autoload](unknown-autoload-design.md)):

175. **[designed]** (merged in part: "did you mean `!`" is done once under item 195) W123 from one owner: commands defined through `auto_index` and `tclIndex` are present (`u04`, `u14`); `namespace unknown` handlers scoped per namespace from the per-point world, not a file-wide flag; the server's `command_refinement.rs` W123 filter folded into binding as a package-database provider giving `Answer::Unknown` with a reason; a procedure summary for user `unknown` procs (accepts every name, chains, unknown) replacing `analyser/oo/unknown_proc.rs`; "did you mean" from command names only (no `le` for `ls`, no `!`).
   - Done when, as tests in `analyser/diagnostics/unresolved.rs`, `u04` and `u14` give no W123, the `namespace unknown` variant (handler in `h`) reports W123 for `zzz` in `other` and at global only, and `ls` gets no `le` or `!` suggestion; `command_refinement.rs` no longer edits diagnostics and the CLI, MCP `analyze` and the server give the same W123 set on `unknown-autoload/`; and `analyser/oo/unknown_proc.rs` is deleted with W123 unchanged on the test corpus.
176. **[designed]** (merged in part: W214 on fixed-signature callbacks is done once under item 160) One `tclIndex`/`auto_index` reader shared by `package_resolver.rs:452`, `original_names/index_loader.rs` and analysed `set auto_index(…)` rows; navigation and hover through autoload receipts moved from `tcl-lsp-server/src/navigation/` into `tcl-lsp-core` so MCP and the CLI follow them; fixed-signature descriptors for ensemble `-unknown`, `namespace unknown` and the TclOO `unknown` method (W214 on `u09`), with the method name read from the TclOO profile instead of the literal `"unknown"`.
   - Done when one function reads both `tclIndex` and `auto_index` (`package_resolver.rs:452` and `index_loader.rs` copies deleted), MCP `goto_definition` and `hover` on the `auto_index`-defined call in `u04` return the defining location, `u09` gives no W214, and the TclOO `unknown` method name is read from the profile (`grep '"unknown"'` in the analyser's method dispatch returns nothing).
177. **[designed]** (merged in part: VM silent exits and the runtime library is done once under item 30) The runtime runner loads the standard library by default (or the harness passes `--init`); measure the VM's `auto_mkindex` and child-interpreter cost (17–20 s and about 1 s per `interp create` in debug).
   - Done when the plain runtime runner (or the harness) loads `init.tcl` by default and every `u*` fixture matches tclsh 8.6/9.0 under it with `u07` printing `1 0`, and the VM's `auto_mkindex` (`u05`) and per-`interp create` costs are measured and recorded in the VM performance notes.

Multiple interpreters ([interpreters](interps-design.md)):

178. **[designed]** (merged in part: the engine traits is done once under item 88) Engines: VM `interp share`/`transfer` implemented or a catchable error; runtime parent→child aliases, command-limit enforcement, `share`/`transfer`/`cancel`; unimplemented subcommands fail as catchable Tcl errors naming the subcommand (no "native handler policy is not selected"); `safe::` available in the runtime runner; an `Interpreters` engine trait in `tcl-runtime-api` with the subcommand table, path resolution and hide/expose code behind it.
   - Done when `i02`, `i06`, `i07`, `i10`, `i12` and `i14` match tclsh 8.6/9.0 in both engines, `catch` sees unimplemented subcommands in `i06` and `i14` with a message naming the subcommand (`grep -rn 'native handler policy is not selected'` returns nothing), `i04` runs `safe::` in the runtime runner, and the subcommand table exists once behind the `Interpreters` trait.
179. **[in progress]** Analysis: interpreter selectors on every `interp` word that takes a command, script or variable (`eval`, `alias`, `bgerror`, `hide`, `expose`, `invokehidden`, `limit -command`, `target`, the child-command ensemble) in the registry's evaluation-context descriptors; one `interp create` reader (delete `parse_interp_create_words` and `interp_path_key`; never apply the synopsis quick fix that deletes `-safe`); child bodies as body units in their own interpreter world; `safe::*` auto-loadable without `package require` (`i09` W120); W312 only where injection is possible.
   - Done when every `interp` subcommand that takes a command, script or variable word has a registry selector with a unit test, `parse_interp_create_words` and `interp_path_key` are deleted, `interps/i16` gives no E003 and no code action deletes `-safe` while W129 fires in `b`, the W210s in `i05`/`i10` and the W123 in `i03` are gone, `i09` has no W120, and `i01`, `i02`, `i04`, `i05` lose their W312s while `interp eval $c $user` keeps it.
   - 25 November 2026: one `interp create` reader. `parse_interp_create_words`, `parse_interp_create_path` and `interp_path_key` are deleted; the visibility capture takes the path the registry's `InterpreterTransition::Create` states, and interpreter keys are the canonical Tcl list form of the path (`tcl_syntax::list`). `safe::*` carries no `required_package`: tclsh 8.4.20-9.1.0 autoload it from `tclIndex`, so `interps/i09` has no W120 (`safe_commands_autoload_without_require`). The registry selectors on the other `interp` words, `i16`, the `i03`/`i05`/`i10` findings and W312 remain open.
180. **[designed]** (merged in part: refusals with a reason (item 78's outcome type) is done once under item 148) Editor features read the selector: alias target words resolve in the target interpreter, `bgerror` handlers in the child, references and rename stay within one interpreter and refuse with a reason when it is unknown (re-run the navigation table on the current source first; several analyser rows did not reproduce).
   - Done when the interps navigation table, re-run on current source, is right or a stated refusal on every row: alias targets in `i02` and `i12` resolve in the target interpreter, `bgerror` handlers in `i10` in the child, references of `local` in `i11` exclude the child body, and `rename` refuses with a reason when the interpreter is unknown.

`exec` and pipelines ([exec](exec-design.md)):

181. **[designed]** Stop the VM running the wrong program until pipelines exist: `exec` raises a catchable error for operator words, switches and `&`, honours `-keepnewline`/`--`, strips one newline, and raises `child process exited abnormally` with `CHILDSTATUS` on a non-zero exit and an error on unredirected stderr (`tcl-cmd-core/src/platform.rs`).
   - Done when `exec-pipes/e01`, `e05` and `e08` match tclsh 8.6/9.0 under the VM or fail with the catchable "pipelines not supported" error, and VM tests check `-keepnewline`, `--`, the single stripped newline, `child process exited abnormally` with `CHILDSTATUS` on a non-zero exit, and the error on unredirected stderr.
182. **[designed]** One pipeline grammar in `tcl-syntax` with per-release operator and switch tables in the registry `exec` spec, pinned by vectors from tclsh 8.4–9.1 and Jim, used by `open_.rs:139`, `source_paths.rs` and `cmd_chan.rs:168` instead of first-byte tests; host pipelines (a `Process` operation returning output, status, stderr and pids) for both engines, or a recorded non-goal with a refusal naming the engine (not "WASM").
   - Done when one `tcl-syntax` pipeline grammar with per-release registry tables is pinned by a vector file generated from tclsh 8.4–9.1 and jimsh, `open_.rs:139`, `source_paths.rs` and `cmd_chan.rs:168` call it with no first-byte `|` test left, the `exec_.rs` hover is generated from it, and `exec-pipes/e01`–`e08` and `e10` match tclsh in both engines or fail with a refusal naming the engine (no "WASM" in the text).
183. **[designed]** Diagnostics: dangling or leading `|`, a redirection without a target, an unknown switch, `&` not last; redirection injection for substituted words not proven free of operator prefixes (hint untainted, warning tainted, `--` noted as not protecting); find why taint reports nothing for `exec sh -c [gets stdin]`, `socket $user 80` and `interp eval $c $user` under 9.0 and add regression tests; re-rank W103 so `open |[list …]` with literal elements is a hint; fold the unsafe-command rosters in `tcl-spec-hooks` and `irules_policy.rs` into registry traits.
   - Done when the `exec-pipes/` analysis rows report the new codes for a dangling or leading `|`, a redirection without a target, an unknown switch and `&` not last (outside `catch`); `e08`'s three substitutions are reported (hint untainted, warning tainted) and a proven `string map` value is not; `exec sh -c [gets stdin]`, `socket $user 80` and `interp eval $c $user` under `tcl9.0` give T100/T104/T105 through MCP `analyze`, `review` and `validate` as regression tests; `e07` gives hints only; and the unsafe rosters in `tcl-spec-hooks` and `irules_policy.rs` are deleted in favour of registry traits.

Threads and newer subsystems ([threads and subsystems](threads-subsystems-design.md)):

184. **[completed]** VM Thread as a package (`package ifneeded Thread`, commands installed on `package require`, `package provide Thread` answers), with the leaked `thread::send` error fixed; a registry Thread profile (`thread::*`, `tsv::*`, `tpool::*`, `thread::send` scripts as cross-world bodies, `tsv` as shared state) so W120 fires without `package require Thread` (`t09`) and hover works; delete `is_unregistered_thread_command` (`bpf-tcl-ir/src/lower.rs:1216`).
   - Done when `threads-subsystems/t01`–`t03` pass under the VM with the SDK path removed, `t10` and `t11` match tclsh 9.0 under both VM pins, `t09` reports W120 for `thread::create` before `package require Thread` and hover on `thread::send` answers, and `grep -n is_unregistered_thread_command rust/bpf-tcl-ir/src/lower.rs` returns nothing.
   - 25 November 2026: the Thread pack (`thread::*`, `tsv::*`, `tpool::*`, 59 commands with hover from the Thread 3.0 manual pages) is part of the base registry, with a hosted `Thread` placement in every Tcl environment, so `thread::id` before `package require Thread` reports W120 (`w120_fires_for_a_thread_command_without_require`, `threads-subsystems/t09`). `is_unregistered_thread_command` is deleted: the eBPF lowering asks `tcl_registry::commands::thread::is_thread_command`. `thread::create` and `tpool::post` declare their scripts as deferred, and `thread::send`/`tsv::get` declare their result variable through `THREAD_RESULT`. The VM Thread package (`t01`–`t03`, `t10`, `t11`) remains open.
   - 25 November 2026 (second pass): the VM offers Thread as a package. `Vm::enable_threads` registers `load` for statically linked packages and `package ifneeded Thread 3.0.4` (2.8.13 on 8.x pins); `package require Thread` runs `load {} Thread`, which installs `thread::*`, `tsv::*` (now with `tsv::array`), `tpool::*` and `thread::mutex|cond|rwmutex` and provides the package. Workers have them from their start. Before the require, `info commands ::thread::create` is empty and `package provide Thread` is empty, as in tclsh.
     - `thread::send -async id script var` now delivers the reply to `var` and `vwait` waits for it. A sync send's error carries the worker's `-errorcode` (one list element, as Thread 3.0.4 does), so a caught error no longer leaves a stale `errorCode` behind to resurface on the next send.
     - Checked against tclsh 9.0.4 with Thread 3.0.4 (the Google Cloud SDK copy, `TCL_LSP_PROBE_PACKAGES=/opt/google-cloud-sdk/platform/bundledpythonunix/lib`): `t01`, `t02`, `t03`, `t09`, `t10` and `t11` give the same output under `tclvm --tcl-version 8.6` and `--tcl-version 9.0`, with the variable unset for the VM runs. `grep is_unregistered_thread_command rust/bpf-tcl-ir/src/lower.rs` returns nothing and `thread::send` hover is in the registry pack.
     - Not covered here: a sender's `-errorinfo` omits the worker's own frames (tclsh appends them), and the `engines.tsv` snapshots for `threads-subsystems/` are not regenerated. `cmd_thread_e2e::workers_inherit_a_separate_host_command_surface` already failed before this change (`NativePreflightRequired`). `tcl-vm --lib` failing set 21 and `--test it_4` 13, both before and after.
185. **[in progress]** (lead item for release rosters, shared with items 125, 193) One generated release roster for `tcl::process`, `zipfs`, `tcl::build-info`, `timerate` and `tcl::unsupported::*` (no runtime `::timerate` under 8.6; one diagnostic for `zipfs` under 8.6, not W002 plus W123); `tcl::process` and background pids after host pipelines; `zipfs` or a recorded non-goal; `tcl_platform(threaded)` and `build-info threaded` from one fact; the missing `tcl::unsupported` commands as recorded non-goals with specs; Thread installed for 8.6 in the oracle set.
   - Done when the roster is generated from each tclsh's `info commands`; under 8.6 the runtime has no `::timerate` and `tcl::unsupported::timerate` is known, under 9.0 it gives one diagnostic; `zipfs` under 8.6 gives one diagnostic, not W002 plus W123; `threads-subsystems/t04` matches tclsh 9.0, `t05` matches or refuses as documented, `t06` agrees with itself in both engines and `t07` lists the same names or a documented refusal; and the 8.6 oracle runs `t01`–`t03` with Thread installed.
   - Phase 1: A head with a command-availability finding no longer also draws W123, so `zipfs` under 8.6 gives one W002 (`a_command_the_release_lacks_is_reported_once`). The generated roster, `timerate`, `tcl::process` in the engines and the Thread oracle remain open.

Encodings and channels ([encodings and channels](encodings-channels-design.md)):

186. **[designed]** (merged in part: the engine traits is done once under item 88) A `Channels` trait in `tcl-runtime-api` and one input path in `tcl-cmd-core/src/channel.rs` (translation, eofchar, codec with profile, character counts) used by both engines' reads, deleting their lossy and raw paths; `chan` in the VM and `chan names/pending/pipe` in the runtime, or recorded non-goals (`e07`, `e09`, `e11`, `e13`, `e14`).
   - Done when both engines implement the `tcl-runtime-api` `Channels` trait and read through `tcl-cmd-core/src/channel.rs` with their lossy and raw paths deleted, `encodings-channels/e07`, `e09`, `e13` and `e14` match tclsh 8.6/9.0 in both engines, and `e11` runs (or `chan names/pending/pipe` are recorded non-goals with a refusal naming the engine).
187. **[designed]** One script reader in `tcl-cmd-core` (BOM, CRLF, lone CR, Ctrl-Z, release and system-encoding rules) used by `tcl-vm-cli`, `run_script` and `source`, and by the LSP's `source_decode.rs` with the dialect passed (no pinned `V8_6`/`tcl8`); BOM rule over (release, system encoding) so 8.6 with UTF-8 gives no W123 on a leading BOM; W107 worded by release; Ctrl-Z modelled in the lexer with a hint for text after it.
   - Done when every `encodings-channels/raw/` file and `e10` match tclsh 8.6/9.0 in both engines through the one reader, `source_decode.rs` has no pinned `V8_6`/`tcl8`, `raw/s01` gives no W123 under 8.6 with UTF-8, the LSP's decoded text for `raw/s04` and `raw/s05` equals each tclsh's with W107 worded by release, and `raw/s06` analyses only `puts before` with a hint on the text after Ctrl-Z.
188. **[in progress]** One encoding catalogue generated from each release's `library/encoding` (names, `SystemEncoding`/`ChannelEncoding` as projections), one `encoding` subcommand table for both engines and the registry (runtime `encoding profiles` on 9.0); checked value grammars with release gates for `-translation`, `-profile`, `-eofchar` and `-encoding`; channel handles as instance values (creation, closure, configuration) with W103 reading the `open` argument's value.
   - Done when `encoding names` in both engines matches tclsh per release or the difference is a documented non-goal, the runtime answers `encoding profiles` on 9.0, the four 9.0 probes in `encodings-channels/analyser-mcp.out` (`-translation`, `-profile`, `-eofchar`, `-encoding`) each give one diagnostic, `e11`'s closed-handle use is reported and `e06`'s W103 is gone.
   - 25 November 2026: an option the release lacks is reported once. On 8.6, `encoding convertfrom -profile …` and `-failindex` give W004 only; the arity check no longer counts the option's words as surplus (E003), so `e04`'s 8.6 lines carry one diagnostic each (`an_option_the_release_lacks_is_reported_once`). `e06`'s W103 needs an absolute-path value fact through `file join [pwd] …`, and `e11` needs item 212's channel instances.
   - 25 November 2026 (second pass): the runtime answers `encoding profiles` (`replace strict tcl8`) and `encoding user` on 9.0 and 9.1 and lists both in its subcommand table; 8.6 still refuses them, and the wrong-argument sentences match tclsh 8.6.18, 9.0.4 and 9.1.0 (`encoding_profiles_and_user_exist_from_9_0`). Open, so the item stays in progress:
     - `encoding names` still lists 4 names in both engines against tclsh 9.0.4's 95; there is no generated catalogue and no recorded non-goal, and the VM, runtime and registry subcommand tables are still three tables.
     - Of the four 9.0 probes, `-profile bogus` and `-eofchar {x y}` give one W146 each; `-translation sideways` and `-encoding nosuch-enc` give none. tclsh 9.0.4 rejects both on a file channel, but a reflected channel created with no mode accepts bad translation words (`channel_empty_masks_and_directionless_values_match_reference_tcl`), and an unknown encoding name may come from `encoding dirs`, so the validators abstain until the channel's kind and the encoding search path are known facts (item 212).
     - `e11`'s closed handle needs item 212's channel instances. `e06`'s W103 needs a registry descriptor for commands whose result is an absolute path (`pwd`, `file normalize`, `file join` over one); the draft patch spelled those commands in a table and re-split the command text, which the registry rule forbids, so it was not taken.

Regular expressions and patterns ([regex and patterns](regex-patterns-design.md)):

189. **[designed]** (merged in part: implicit writes reaching W210 is done once under item 22) Lower `switch` with `-matchvar`/`-indexvar` as writes of those variables before each selected body from the registry's selection effects (`lowering/structured.rs:204`, `value_transfer/selection.rs:588`), so `r11` gives no W210 and rename, references and definition work on them; an invalid-pattern diagnostic over the shared `tcl-regex` parse for literal patterns and argument-shape errors (`-inline` with match variables, `-matchvar` without `-regexp`).
   - Done when `regex-patterns/r06` and `r11` give no W210 and LSP rename, references and definition work on the `-matchvar`/`-indexvar` variables, and every literal error case in `regex-patterns/analyser-mcp.out` (bad patterns in `r10`, `-inline` with match variables, `-matchvar` without `-regexp`) is reported while no dynamic pattern is.
190. **[completed]** Rewrite W303 over the `tcl-regex` parse with measured slow cases (or retire it), so `{\(a|b\)+}` is clean; name and document the two Tcl glob matchers (`Tcl_StringCaseMatch` and the C-unit name matcher) and mark `tcl-cli`'s `glob_matches` and `dynamic_names::pattern_matches` as non-Tcl in the walker manifest.
   - Phase 1: W303 now reads parsed regexp structure. The two Tcl glob owners and the generic pattern matchers are recorded in the walker ledger. [Pinned Tcl timing cases](evidence/regex-backreference-timings.md) cover the retained backreference shapes.
   - Done when `regexp {\(a|b\)+}` gives no W303, each remaining W303 shape has a timing fixture showing a slow case on tclsh 8.6 and 9.0 (or W303 is retired), and the walker manifest lists exactly two Tcl glob owners with `tcl-cli` `glob_matches` and `dynamic_names::pattern_matches` marked non-Tcl.

Tcl 8.6 → 9.0/9.1 changes ([Tcl 9 changes](tcl9-changes-design.md)):

191. **[in progress]** One TIP 278 fact (`tcl-dialect/src/version/release_behaviour.rs:35` and `profile/engine_policy.rs:35` disagree for profiles with no base release), used by references and rename so renaming `::x` under 8.6 includes `namespace eval ns {set x …}` writes (re-run the rename row on the current source first).
   - Done when `release_behaviour.rs` and `engine_policy.rs` answer TIP 278 from one function, with a unit test for a profile without a base release, and MCP `find_references` and `rename` on `::x` in `tcl9-changes/t18` include the `namespace eval ns {set x …}` write under 8.6 and exclude it under 9.0, as tclsh does.
192. **[in progress]** Meaning-change diagnostics from release facts: `08` and `#` inside `expr` before 9.0, a leading `~` in paths under 9.0, the removed `binary` encoding name, writes to a `const`, `lseq 0 {1+2}`, `regexp \z` before 9.1 (`TclVersion::regex_z_anchor`); effect descriptors for the new mutators (`encoding -failindex` writes its variable, `ledit`, `lremove`, `lseq`); release-aware hover prose (`file_.rs:1007` still describes tilde expansion).
   - Done when `tcl9-changes/t01`, `t03`, `t08`, `t14`, `t20` and `t31` each give one diagnostic in the release where they fail and none in the other, the `const` write in `t13` and `lseq 0 {1+2}` in `t32` are reported, def-use chains show `encoding -failindex`'s variable written and `ledit`/`lremove`/`lseq` effects from their descriptors, and `file` hover under 9.0 has no tilde-expansion text.
   - 25 November 2026: W148 also reads number literals inside an expression, so `expr {08 + 0}` ("invalid octal number" in tclsh 8.6.18, 8 in 9.0.4) reports under 8.x and not 9.0 (`a_tcl9_numeral_inside_an_expression_reports_w148_before_9_0`, `t01`). The other rows remain open.
   - 25 November 2026 (second pass): the other rows are implemented over registry literal validators and release facts (`TclVersion::tilde_names_home`, `removed_encoding_names`), each with a unit test.
     - `#` in `expr` before 9.0 gives W003 (`t20`); `regexp \z` before 9.1 gives W137 through the argument-gate pass (`t31`, `TclVersion::regex_z_anchor`).
     - W146 reports a leading `~` in a `file` name under 9.0 (`t03`: `pathtype` and `join`; `split` reads `~` the same in both), the `binary` and `identity` encoding names under 9.0 for `encoding convertfrom|convertto|system` and channel `-encoding` (`t08`), and `lseq` operands that are not numbers (`t14`, `t32`). E007 reports the selected `set`, `incr`, `append` and `lappend` writes that the bound constant cell rejects (`t13`, item 14).
     - `ledit` gains world, representation and completion descriptors; `lremove` and `lseq` are declared value-only; `encoding -failindex` already declared its write (a test pins it). The `file` hover no longer claims tilde substitution.
     - Not done: `unset` of a constant (its roles come from a layout resolver, so `arg_roles()` gives none); `encoding convertto binary` under 8.x (fails there too, no catalogue says so); release-specific hover prose (the `file` text is release-neutral, with no per-release field, which would touch the SpecTcl DSL parity surface); `t03` under 9.0 now reports two W146 where the Done-when says none in the release where tclsh does not error.
     - `cargo xtask conformance -- tcl9-changes` fails its rows until the snapshots are regenerated. `analyser::` failing set 298 before and after; `tcl-registry --lib` 8 before and after.
193. **[designed]** (merged in part: release rosters is done once under item 185) Fill the engine rosters or record non-goals: `lfilter` and `unicode` (9.1) in both, and `chan`, `zipfs`, `tcl::process`, `oo::singleton` (VM), `fpclassify`, `zipfs`, `tcl::process`, `oo::define::classmethod` (runtime); conformance rows for every change in the page's table.
   - Done when `tcl9-changes/t30` (`lfilter`, `unicode`) matches tclsh 9.1 in both engines, and each other listed command (`t24`, `t27`, `t33`, `t37`, `chan`) matches tclsh in its engine or is a recorded non-goal with a refusal naming the engine, with a passing `engines.tsv` row for every change in the tcl9 page's table.

Comments and continuations ([comments and continuations](comments-continuations-design.md)):

194. **[designed]** Runtime CRLF: translate CRLF when reading scripts and in `source`, so a backslash before CRLF continues as in tclsh and the VM (with the script reader above); W115 from the lexer instead of `ends_with('\\')` (`tcl-lsp-core/src/source_style.rs:406`/`:422`); delete the text-level comment scanners (`inert_text.rs:89`, `nested_invocations.rs:925`, `source_style.rs`) in favour of the lexer-based `script_comments.rs`, with a gate; one "switch body has a comment" rule for both engines.
   - Done when `comments-continuations/c21` prints tclsh's error in the runtime as an end-to-end test, `c27` has no W115 while `c07`, `c19` and `c25` keep theirs, `inert_text.rs:89`, `nested_invocations.rs:925` and the `source_style.rs` text scanner are gone with a gate failing on any new `== '#'`/`== b'#'` comment test outside `tcl-lexer` and `tcl-value-syntax`, and `c03`, `c04`, `c26` behave the same in both engines from one shared rule.
195. **[designed]** (lead item for "did you mean `!`", shared with items 12, 175) Diagnostics: an odd-length `switch` body caused by a `#` line, a `#` line inside `switch`, `array set`, `dict create` or `foreach` data (it is data, not a comment), `#` in `expr` before 9.0; message fixes ("close-brace", no `!` suggestion); docstrings that exclude a swallowed `proc` line; the VM's error line for `c20`; the MCP analyse tool runs the same style checks as the server.
   - Done when `comments-continuations/c03` gets an error and `c04`, `c26` a warning, `#` lines in `c15`, `c23`, `c24` data get no comment diagnostic, `c18` and `tcl9-changes/t20` report under `tcl8.6` and not `tcl9.0`, messages say "close-brace" with no `!` suggestion, `c25`'s docstring excludes the swallowed `proc` line, the VM reports tclsh's error line for `c20`, and MCP `analyze` and the server give the same codes on every `comments-continuations/` fixture (regenerated `lsp-features.out`).

Jim ([Jim](jim-design.md)):

196. **[designed]** (owner decision, 23 November 2026: analysis only on `work`) Jim stays an analysis dialect with jimsh as the oracle. Dialect execution in the VM and the runtime (Jim, iRules or any other dialect) is a distant goal and not part of the `work` branch.
   - On `work`, the engines' Jim code is neither exposed nor deleted: no `--dialect` on `tclvm` or `run_script`, no references or GC, and no engine conformance rows for Jim. Any later decision about that code belongs to the dialect-execution goal.
   - Add a `jim` analysis profile reachable from `DialectProfile::find` and the MCP dialect enum, and the Jim effect descriptors in the [Jim design](jim-design.md) (`local`, `upcall`, `alias`/`curry`, `ref` finalizers, statics, `exists`, `defer`).
   - Done when: the `oo-dispatch-probe/jim/` analysis and navigation fixtures give jimsh's answers under the `jim` profile, and the conformance matrix shows jimsh as an oracle column with the engine Jim columns marked "out of scope on `work`".
197. **[designed]** Jim analysis: effect descriptors for `local`, `upcall`, `alias`, `curry`, `defer` and procedure statics so navigation follows them; fix the false W210 on statics (and its Rust `Debug` message), W001 on Jim `string` subcommands, W214 on finalizer parameters and W211 on `exists`-tested variables; one declaration route for Jim commands (`alias_.rs`, `exists_.rs` versus the tclspec pack).
   - Done when under `jimtcl` `j19` has no W210 (and W210 names the variable, no `Debug` text), `j22` no W001, `j02` no W214 and `j11` no W211 while each still reports under `tcl9.0`; definition on `[greet]` in `j04`'s `wrap` goes to line 4 and `upcall greet` to line 2, `[helper]` in `j03` and `[e v]` in `j08` resolve with no W123 (regenerated `jim/navigation.out`); and `alias_.rs`/`exists_.rs` are deleted in favour of the tclspec pack.

Across the fourteen areas:

198. **[completed]** (merged in part: conformance rows is done once under item 75) Conformance: turn every area's fixtures and `engines.tsv` into rows run by both engines (8.4–9.1 pins where relevant; Jim rows by jimsh and the analyser only, item 196) and by the analyser, optimiser (executed before and after) and LSP features, regenerating `tcl-areas-head/` instead of hand-written `analyser-mcp.out` files.
   - Done when one runner regenerates every area's (`oo-dispatch-probe/*/`) `engines.tsv`, `analyser-mcp.out` and `tcl-areas-head/` from the fixtures (both engines on the 8.4–9.1 pins, jimsh plus the analyser for Jim, the optimiser executed before and after, LSP features), CI runs it, and a hand edit or a reintroduced regression in any row fails it.
   - Verified 25 November 2026. `cargo xtask conformance` regenerates every area's `engines.tsv` and `analysis.tsv`, the `analyser-mcp.out` verdict files (a verdict survives only while its codes are unchanged) and `tcl-areas-head/`. The `Command definitions` workflow runs it after the definitions gate and passed on `5ca21818` ([run 45544162958](https://github.com/bitwisecook/tcl-lsp/actions/runs/45544162958)). A hand edit fails its row, and the run caught the phase 0b registry change's new, correct `W146` on `encodings-channels/e13` under tcl9.0.
199. **[completed]** MCP freshness. The repository launcher runs Cargo against the checkout on each start. Tool results include the build timestamp, resolved version and source revision in protocol metadata. [Build and response contract](../../design/contracts/project-layout.md#mcp-build-provenance)
   - Verified over stdio using the repository MCP configuration. A success and an unknown-tool response both reported `git rev-parse HEAD`. Adding an uncommitted field to the `analyze` implementation changed the next launch's result and build timestamp. An intentional compilation error exited with no protocol output while both a previous executable and a cached binary were available.
   - The protocol and version tests passed. The full MCP suite passed 147 of 148 tests; the failing `review_reports_the_compiler_check_families` is IRULE4002 not firing at all, a separate defect present since `work`'s first commit (item 243).

Structural corrections from reading all findings together ([findings synthesis](findings-synthesis.md)); each retires a class of items, whose symptoms become its acceptance tests:

200. **[in progress]** Effect-descriptor completeness (root cause 1): every argument that holds a script, command prefix, lambda, name, variable or callback has an evaluation-context, effect or fixed-signature descriptor in `tcl-registry`, and binding consumes them generically. Done when the counts of `DefinerFamily` matches, `Statement::Catch`/`Try` matchers and command-name matches outside the registry ratchet to zero, and items 3–5, 9, 11, 12, 14, 16, 17, 160, 162, 166, 167, 170, 173, 175, 189 and 197 pass as tests.
   - Conformance rows: `frames/`, `traces/`, `event-loop/`, `interps/` and `chan/`; each consumer item retains its exact expected answers.
201. **[completed]** A grammar owner index and bypass lint (root cause 3): a manifest naming one reader and one writer per Tcl grammar (level words, variable references, element names, lists, words, scripts, expressions, numbers, versions, indices, options, globs, regex, paths, pipelines, `tclIndex`, completion codes, interpreter paths, encodings), creating the owners that are missing (writer, `TCLLIBPATH`, value grammars, positions, waivers, docstrings, TSV, completion codes, `tclIndex`, `interp create`, pipelines, script reader, subcommand prefixes, encoding catalogue); a host-primitive lint (`split_whitespace`, `trim`, `contains('(')`, `ends_with('\\')`, `starts_with('|')` on Tcl text) whose waivers must name an owner. Done when the lint is in `make xtask-gates` with an only-shrinks waiver list.
   - The [grammar index](ownership/grammars.tsv) names the entry points. The [ownership contract](ownership/README.md) links the quoting, list and pipeline evidence. The later domain items retain their consumer migrations and engine conformance work.
202. **[in progress]** Generated release facts (root cause 4): release tables, rosters, usage lines and arity windows generated in `tcl-dialect` and the registry from the C Tcl and Jim sources and oracle captures, with `--check` gates. Done when the `V9_x` branch count, the release-enum mappings and the hand-written rosters are replaced by one generated table.
   - Conformance rows: `rust/tcl-dialect/data/release-facts.tsv`, `rust/tcl-dialect/data/expression-precedence.tsv` and `tcl9-changes/`. Usage/arity extraction and the remaining release-enum consumers remain open.
203. **[designed]** (merged in part: the engine traits is done once under item 88) One engine trait set (root cause 5): `Frames`, `Namespaces`, `Interpreters`, `Channels`, `Traces`, `EventLoop`, `Process`, `Bootstrap` and `Coroutines` in `tcl-runtime-api`, kernels in `tcl-cmd-core`, and one differential suite (umbrella for 20, 72, 88, 161, 171, 178, 186). Done when each engine's per-subsystem files only register commands and host glue.
204. **[designed]** Surfaces as thin adapters over one session (root cause 8): the LSP server, CLI and MCP contain no dialect-selection, configuration, position, workspace-scan or failure-shape logic of their own (umbrella for 104, 109, 113, 144, 146, 148). Done when the agreement harness runs identical inputs through all three and they agree.
205. **[completed]** Conformance before migration (root cause 9): every consumer migration names the vector rows it turns green, and those rows exist before the migration starts (umbrella for 25, 28, 37, 65, 75, 131, 198).
   - Done when a check over `plan.md` and the item 198 runner fails for any migration item that names no conformance rows or names a row that does not exist, and items 25, 28, 37, 65, 75, 131 and 198 pass it.
   - Verified 25 November 2026. `cargo xtask plan-rows` (in `make xtask-gates`) checks the umbrella items and every migration item in progress; all eight pass. `--all` reports the open migration items still naming missing rows (67 of 161), which is the list of migrations not yet ready to start.
206. **[completed]** (23 November 2026: every open item has a "done when"; grab bags 14, 115, 143 and 152 are split; owner crates named for 38 (deferred engine work), 40 (`tcl-dialect`), 109 and 113 (`tcl-lsp-core`); decisions 142 and 196 are made. The pass/fail thresholds in 90, 92, 94, 95, 102 and 103 are proposals for the owner to confirm, and some tests name gates or fixtures that do not exist yet, which their items build) A completion test for every item: add "done when" to items 1–152 that lack one, split the grab bags (14, 115, 143, 152), name owner crates for 40, 109 and 113, and assign owners to the open decisions (38, 142, 196). (26 November 2026: the owner of 109 and 113 is `tcl-session`, since 289 dissolves `tcl-lsp-core`.)
207. **[completed]** (23 November 2026: the shared part of each overlap group is marked on its lead item and on the others; numbers unchanged) A merge pass that keeps numbers stable: mark each overlapping item listed in the [findings synthesis](findings-synthesis.md) as "merged into N" rather than renumbering, so references across the design pages stay valid; follow [design integration](design-integration.md)'s order as the only statement of what comes first.

Channels and `chan` ([chan design](chan-design.md); fixtures in `oo-dispatch-probe/chan/`):

208. **[designed]** Stop the engines discarding channel options: store, validate and report `-blocking`, `-buffering`, `-buffersize` (clamped as in C) and `-eofchar` (a pair up to 8.6, one character from 9.0) in `tcl-cmd-core/src/channel.rs` (`ChannelConfig`, `config_value`, `set_config_value`, today `Ok(())` and hard-coded values at `:664`–`:714`), with line and none buffering in both engines' write paths. Done when `c16` and `c20` match tclsh 8.6 and 9.0 in both engines.
209. **[designed]** Real standard input and live read channels: the VM reads stdin from the host (today constant EOF, `tcl-vm/src/cmd_chan.rs:24`) and streams files instead of snapshotting at `open`; the runtime finds `stdin`; `fblocked` checks arity and the channel; `file channels` lists the standard channels in both engines; unimplemented `file` subcommands and runtime refusals name the engine (not "unknown or ambiguous", not "WASM runtime"); C's channel naming and number reuse (`file3`/`file5`). ### BIG-IP names and addresses

BIG-IP names and addresses (Q8; [BIG-IP names and addresses](bigip-addresses-design.md); 713-input matrix and oracle comparison in `oo-dispatch-probe/bigip-addresses/`). Rule: the property's value grammar from the registry selects the reader; the string never does.

227. **[completed]** **Complete.** Pool member ports come from the shared `MemberKey` reader whether or not the member has an explicit address. AS3 emits the parsed service port. The regression checks every pool member in `addresses.conf` and `device-01.bigip.conf` through SCF parsing and AS3 conversion. [Regression test](../../../rust/tcl-bigip/src/convert.rs) · [21.1 member-key checks](../../../evidence/bigip/tmsh-21.1-name-handling.md#names-route-domains-and-services)
228. **[completed]** (see the item's own status note) **Complete for the measured 21.1 address components.** `Endpoint` and `BigIpName` own typed destinations and names. Address and network readers preserve explicit `%0`, IPv4-mapped IPv6, prefixes and authored endpoint spelling. Named services use one table. The 713-input matrix was rerun against nine readers. The validator reports undeclared route domains on measured components, preserves the exception for internal IP data groups, and reports invalid service, separator and FQDN forms in configurations labelled 21.1. [Matrix rerun](bigip-addresses-design.md#synthetic-matrix-rerun) · [21.1 evidence](../../../evidence/bigip/tmsh-21.1-address-grammars.md)
229. **[completed]** (see the item's own status note) **Registry migration complete.** Every address-shaped property now declares a reader. Seven protocol and family selectors had been tagged as addresses and use their scalar shapes. The coverage test walks nested fields as well as top-level ones. Model reads for virtuals, pools, nodes, self IPs and routes select the registry grammar. Report enrichment and topology use the address, endpoint or network reader for each known field. Fields without a declared grammar can return all distinct readings. REST resource paths and iRules have their own existing boundary readers. Release-specific transport variants, including the legacy IPv6 colon spelling, remain open with item 234. [Address reader contract](bigip-addresses-design.md#design)
230. **[completed]** **Complete.** Config search, syntax highlighting, query, AS3, capture, flow matching and reporting use the shared address and service readers. The standalone report uses a small WebAssembly build of the Rust reader for listener matching and CIDR search. An invalid address fails matching even against a zero-width prefix. The original numeric-service helper remains available through its compatibility export. [Address reader contract](bigip-addresses-design.md#design)
231. **[completed]** (see the item's own status note) **Complete for the measured 21.1 grammar.** The fixture tests cover all explicit IPv6, route-domain, named-service and wildcard edges. BIGIP6011/6013 are quiet for valid address forms, including implicitly created targets. The fixture reports the omitted VLAN, undeclared route-domain references and the unmeasured legacy colon spelling. Network, capture, Wireshark and flow matching retain route domains, and the report derives the source prefix from the parsed mask. [Fixture regressions](../../../rust/tcl-bigip/src/graph.rs) · [21.1 evidence](../../../evidence/bigip/tmsh-21.1-address-grammars.md)
232. **[completed]** **Complete.** `tcl-bigip::object_names::BigIpName` owns authored paths and exposes `.app` folders. It keeps literal-address leaves and rejects malformed separator and space spellings. The query, CLI and report path consumers use the shared path rules. The pull and replace paths validate names before requesting a resource. The iControl codec round-trips nested paths and emits `~Common~10.0.0.1%252` for `/Common/10.0.0.1%2`. [Name reader](../../../rust/tcl-bigip/src/object_names.rs) · [REST codec tests](../../../rust/tcl-bigip-io/src/icontrol_path.rs) · [21.1 loader evidence](../../../evidence/bigip/tmsh-21.1-address-grammars.md#object-names-and-fqdns)
233. **[completed]** (see the item's own status note) **Complete for the measured iRules forms.** Registry port positions select the typed reader for `node`, `pool … member` and `snat`. The GUI lists documented combined address and port forms. W124 ignores clock times and MAC addresses, and redaction treats an IPv4-mapped IPv6 address as one match. `IP::addr equals` uses the shared reader for IPv4, IPv6, route domains and masks. IP data-group `class match` validates static operands and the simulator's host binding applies CIDR membership with the measured suffix behaviour. The evidence records the mixed-family IPv6-mask case that remains unmodelled. [21.1 compiler evidence](../../../evidence/bigip/tmsh-21.1-address-grammars.md#irules-compiler) · [21.1 TMM runtime evidence](../../../evidence/tcl/bigip-ip-addr-route-domains.md)

243. **[in progress]** (deep review W12: its IRULE4002 tests are conformance rows of 253's move, so they hold through the split; found 24 November 2026 while validating item 199; present since `work`'s first commit, correct on `rust`) IRULE4002 never fires on `work`: `set static::debug 1` in any handler draws nothing, so 6 of 10 `irules_checks` IRULE4002 tests and MCP's `review_reports_the_compiler_check_families` fail. Three links are missing. A handler body is reached conditionally, so its `static::` store has no proved execution target, and the conditional logical store (`variable_bindings.rs`, `definition_place`) admits handler-local cells only. The fresh hosted entry (`SourceAnalysisEntry::for_fresh_hosted_source`) supplies no `hosted_execution_context`, so the binding walk never makes `::static` addressable. An authored `::static` cell is classified as worker storage only through an `AuthoredTmmStaticCompilationContext` receipt (`scalar_binding.rs`), which only tests issue. With the first two supplied, the write resolves to `::static::debug` but classifies as `Namespace`, not `Static`.
   - Done when: a production owner issues the TMM static-storage receipt for hosted iRule handler entries; the 10 `irules_checks` IRULE4002 tests, built through the production analysis input rather than `CompilationUnit::build_for`, and MCP's `review_reports_the_compiler_check_families` pass; and `when HTTP_REQUEST { set ::static::debug 1 }` draws IRULE4002 through `tcl diag`.
   - 25 November 2026: the three links are closed. (1) `SourceAnalysisEntry::for_fresh_hosted_source` now selects its environment's hosted context (`BigIpExecutionContext::for_environment`: `TmmIRule` for `f5-irules`, `IAppImplementation` for `f5-iapps`), so the binding walk adds TMM's runtime namespaces and `static::debug` resolves to the authored `::static` cell. (2) The hosted store admission (`hosted_cell_is_local` in `logical_operation.rs`, `ConditionalLogicalScalarStore::definition_place`) also admits a cell whose authored namespace the hosted context's registry storage policy names as worker storage (`variable_bindings::is_hosted_worker_namespace_cell`), in any handler. (3) `side_effects::canonical_namespace_storage_scope` reads an authored namespace identity through the same descriptive iRules policy as an authored namespace owner, so `::static` classifies as `Static` and `::` as `Global`. No `AuthoredTmmStaticCompilationContext` is issued: that receipt names a native namespace incarnation (`NativeNamespaceContext`) source analysis does not have, and its publication observers would make the store observed; authored keys still carry no storage domain, as `naming.variable.hosted-storage-context` requires. The IRULE4002 tests build through `source_unit` (the production `ResolvedAnalysisInput` and supplied-source entry); `irule4002_ignores_a_static_in_a_dead_arm`'s reachable control moved to `HTTP_REQUEST`, because a fresh `RULE_INIT` reading an unset `$x` errors before its arm runs. `tcl diag` reports IRULE4002 for `when HTTP_REQUEST { set ::static::debug 1 }`. Failing sets: `-p tcl-compiler --lib` 0 new, 5 fixed; `-p tcl-lsp-core --lib` and `-p tcl-mcp --bin tcl-mcp` 0 new.
   - Open. (a) The `upvar #0 ::static::debug shared; set shared 1` row of `generic_static_warning_uses_resolved_root_keys_aliases_and_original_source_locations` still draws nothing: the hosted walk does not model `upvar` (lowering marks it an unseen call and both the walk and the point contexts widen to dynamic bindings), and `upvar #0 ::debug shared` fails the same way, so this is hosted alias support, not storage. (b) MCP's `review_reports_the_compiler_check_families` fails earlier, at its first assertion: `set cmd [gets stdin]; eval $cmd` under `tcl9.0` reports no T100 (also on the clean tree); with that assertion removed the test passes, IRULE4002 included. (c) Whether the receipt line is met by the hosted context rather than the native receipt needs the owner's decision.

245. **[in progress]** (slice of item 55; needed by item 210's `c05` row; [bitwisecook/tcl-lsp#2551](https://github.com/bitwisecook/tcl-lsp/issues/2551)) Script parameters: a procedure formal that the body runs as a script (`uplevel 1 $p`, `eval $p`, `uplevel #0 $p`, `after … $p`) gets a `ScriptParameter { frame, timing }` contract, and a call with one proved target analyses a literal argument as a body in that frame ([design](script-parameters-design.md)).
   - Done when: a new `dynamic/` fixture with the measured shapes reports an unknown command and a bad option inside a `Caller(1)` argument, its `Own` script does not see the caller's locals, its `Global` script writes `::z`, the two `ProcArgTrait` enums are one contract, and `chan/c05` reports `sideways` under 8.6 and 9.0 once item 210's `close` direction check exists.
   - `chan/c18` is the second row that waits on this: the wrapper's dynamic `uplevel` makes every later command unprovable, which hides the W146 its bad `chan postevent` event draws when the wrapper is absent.
   - 25 November 2026: plan step 1 done. The registry reports where each `Body` script runs (`ResolvedInvocation::script_argument_context`, carried as `InvocationFacts::script_contexts`), from the descriptor's `EvaluationContext` and, for `uplevel`, its frame-effect level. Trait inference records one use per script position and `ProcDef::script_parameters` holds the reduced `ScriptParameter { frame, timing }` per formal: `uplevel 1 $p` is `Caller(1)`, `uplevel 2 $p` `Caller(2)`, `eval`/`catch`/`while 1 $p` `Own`, `uplevel #0 $p` `Global`, `after 0 $p` `Global`+`Deferred`; disagreeing uses, partial scripts (`eval $p x`, `subst $p`) and lambdas give `Unknown`. The deep pass now also reads command substitutions in a braced expression, so `if {[catch {uplevel 1 $s} r]} …` is found. Tests: `analyser::param_traits::tests::script_parameters`, `evaluation_context::tests::script_argument_context_places_each_measured_wrapper_shape`, `analyser::handlers::tests::procedures::a_procedure_records_the_frame_it_runs_a_script_parameter_in`; contract documented in `docs/design/contracts/proc-arg-traits.md`.
   - 25 November 2026, call-site step: done for calls the source walk enters. The binding owner remembers each formal an entered call binds from a braced literal (`command_binding/source_walk/script_formals.rs`); a script body in that activation that reads the whole formal, still holding the same text, is walked from the caller's word, and `uplevel 1` there selects the recorded calling activation. The analyser then walks a braced argument the realm ran (`analyser/handlers/script_parameters.rs`) in the frame the callee's contract names: the calling scope for `Caller(1)`, a fresh scope for `Own`, the global scope for `Global` and `Deferred`. The contract is inferred on demand per callee from the deep pass; when the called wrapper's positioned proof fails (`owned-read-context` for a formal read nested in `catch` inside its `if` condition), the body is read as its own uncalled declaration instead, so the positioned formal-value proof itself is unchanged. Tests: `analyser::handlers::tests::script_parameters` (W001 and W127 inside a `Caller(1)` argument of `c05`'s wrapper, the calling frame written, `::z` written by `uplevel #0`, an `Own` script neither writing nor seeing the caller's locals, and no walk for an argument the callee does not run or a substituted one). A probe of the same shapes under the CLI reports, for `t option {close stdout sideways}` then `t typo {string frobnicate x}` then `t arity {llength}`, W127, W001 and E002 under 8.4 (with E003 for the 8.4 arity), W127 and W001 under 8.6, and W127 alone under 9.0 (see open point 1).
   - Open, in order. (1) A wrapper call still stops the walk of what follows when a command in its script is modelled as unknown rather than as completing or failing: `string frobnicate` (an ensemble's unknown subcommand), `close stdout sideways` under 9.0, and the `open` and `chan` steps in `chan/c05` and the `chan create` step in `c18`; the one-argument `puts` case is fixed (see the walk-stopping note below), and the remaining causes are listed there. (2) Calls inside procedure bodies: the analyser reads a body from its declared-frame walk, which does not enter called procedures, so `proc p {} {t a {…}}` reports nothing yet; that walk needs the callee's contract to walk the literal in the right frame. (3) Not yet covered: `uplevel $p` (one computed word; the registry selects the script only on the successful edge, so it has no `Body` role before dispatch), `if 1 $p` (no `Body` role on an unbraced `if` body), and forwarding a formal to another procedure's script parameter. (4) Merging the two `ProcArgTrait` enums (plan step 5, item 55). (5) The `dynamic/` conformance fixture (`e33-script-parameters`: the wrapper `t`, `e {…}` with `eval`, `g {…}` with `uplevel #0`, then the three checks above and the measured `p`/`q`/`r` frame probe, tclsh 8.4–9.1 printing `1:0:3` and `1:0:0 1`) is not committed: its rows need `cargo xtask conformance`, which builds the WASM runtime's `run_script` into a second target directory, which this session's one-target rule excludes. Hand-written rows would fail the row test, so `c05`/`c18`'s rows are also unchanged.
   - 25 November 2026, walk-stopping gap: root-caused and fixed for the one-argument `puts`. A one-argument `puts` writes the process `stdout`; the source walk treats it as a write that completes or fails only while `stdout` is proven free of Tcl callbacks, and otherwise as a possible re-entry that makes the binding world opaque, after which no later call is entered (`native_target.rs`, `unmodelled_native_reentry`). Two kinds of step withdrew that proof without cause: an `if`, whose branches the walk enters itself but whose handler was not among those that delegate re-entry to their walked operands (`expr` and `catch` were), and any file, channel, network, log or process effect (`open`, `close`, `exec`), none of which can give `stdout` a callback (a channel `open` or `socket` places in a closed `stdout` slot runs no Tcl when written). Now `if` delegates like `catch`, and only a step that retains a callback (`chan create`, `chan push`), an interpreter-state effect, or an opaque step withdraws the proof (`target_walk.rs`, `may_change_stock_stdout`). Test: `analyser::handlers::tests::script_parameters::a_later_call_of_a_printing_wrapper_is_still_entered` (`c05`'s printing wrapper; the call after `t b {close stdout sideways}` is entered and reports W001); `stock_process_stdout_is_withdrawn_after_channel_mutation` still holds. `chan/c05` and `c18` still report nothing under 8.6 and 9.0; a source-walk trace of each names what now stops the walk: (a) `open` has no closed normal-return receipt, so it withholds the completed command world and the `stdout` proof with it (`target_walk.rs`, `withhold_command_world(false)`), and every later one-argument `puts` in `t` re-enters; (b) `chan`'s members have no native compilation descriptor (the command's grammar is `Unresolved`), so `chan close $f read` compiles to an unknown selection and marks the walk opaque (`command_walk.rs`). Measured with `tcl::unsupported::disassemble`: 8.5.19 invokes `chan` itself; 8.6.18, 9.0.4 and 9.1.0 compile `chan close $x y` to `push ::tcl::chan::close` and `invokeStk 3`, a call outside the member's `TclCompileBasic*ArgCmd` arity to `invokeReplace` of the worker, and `copy`, `puts`, `read` and `configure` (no compile procedure) to `invokeReplace` (`tclIOCmd.c`'s `initMap` is the same in the three releases). `NamedEnsembleInvocation` descriptors like `file_.rs`'s are not enough alone: a named ensemble compiler outside an original lexical command stays unknown unless the member declares its state transitions (`original_named_selection_is_closed`); (c) an ensemble's unknown subcommand: the registry's selection is `Unknown`, while 8.6+ compiles `string frobnicate x` to `invokeReplace` of `::string` and 8.5 to a plain invoke; (d) under 9.0 and 9.1 a file or process effect is unmodelled re-entry, so `close stdout sideways` there still stops the walk; (e) `c18`'s `chan create read rc` retains a callback, which withdraws the proof although an open `stdout` cannot become the new channel; telling the two apart needs `stdout`'s open or closed state (item 212). Open point (2) is unchanged and is not a small change: the analyser reads a procedure body from its declared-frame walk, which does not enter called procedures.

247. **[completed]** (step 1a of item 90; [design](../../design/compiler/crate-split/01-module-layers.md)) The `module-layers` gate: its code and unit tests. The module-to-crate assignment (`module-layers.tsv`) and the lower-to-higher production reference counts (`module-back-edges.tsv`) are reported by `cargo xtask check`. From the start it also checks dev-dependency edges: a crate's `[dev-dependencies]` may not name a crate above it, with today's cases (items 246 and 269–272) listed as known and allowed only to shrink. The back-edge report is the work list for steps 2–12, and from step 13 on it fails on a new back-edge. It also carries the `test-support` body lint (deep review W1; AGENTS.md, *What tests test*): a module of any `src` gated on `feature = "test-support"` may hold fixtures and helpers but no test body (no `#[test]`, and no `pub fn` that a test of the same name in another crate calls), with item 267's 13 `tcl-compiler` modules listed as known and allowed only to shrink.
   - Done when: the gate runs in `make xtask-gates`. Its unit tests report a new back-edge, do not count test code, report equal-rank crates naming each other, fail on a new back-edge in enforcing mode, fail on a new upward dev-dependency, and fail on a new test body behind `test-support`.
   - 27 November 2026: done ([as built](../../design/compiler/crate-split/01-module-layers.md#as-built-27-november-2026)). `cargo xtask module-layers` (`rust/xtask/src/semantic_ownership/module_layers.rs`) runs in `cargo xtask check` and `make xtask-gates` in 0.5 s. It follows `pub use` re-exports to the defining module before matching a row, and treats `#[cfg(test)]`, `test-support` and `any(test, …)` items, and test-only module files, as test code. The `# mode:` line of `module-layers.tsv` switches reporting to enforcing at step 13. The dev-dependency check reads crate order from the normal-dependency cones and `deny.toml`'s wrapper lists; its known list, derived from the tree, is `tcl-cshim` → `tcl-engine-tclvm` and `tcl-spec-hooks` (`dev-edges.tsv`). The `test-support` body ledger is empty after item 267's public test rewrite; any new exported body fails the lint. The 6 unit tests are the done-when's cases; `cargo test -p xtask` passes (432), clippy is clean and `make xtask-gates` passes 50 of 50.
