259. **[in progress]** (measurement for item 90, 26 November 2026; [design](../../design/build-time/259-worktree-cost.md)) Worktree cost of an isolated edit. A script, `scripts/dev/worktree-cost.py`, runs in a fresh worktree with the agent build environment. It records the cost of four representative edits:
   - the edits: a `tcl-lexer` or `tcl-syntax` change, one registry command spec, one analyser rule, and one `tcl-lsp-core` feature;
   - the cost of each: crates compiled, wall time, peak RSS (sampled from `/proc`, since `/usr/bin/time` is not installed) and target-directory size for `cargo test -p <crate> --no-run`, cold and after the edit;
   - the crates compiled per profile (dev for check and clippy, test) and per feature set (with and without `test-support`), so items 296 and 297 have a baseline;
   - the peak RSS of the test run itself, not only of the build;
   - rust-analyzer's resident memory per worktree once it has indexed the workspace.
   - added by the deep review (26 November 2026): `names` and `binding` edit rows once those crates exist (F7); the `spec`, `spec-core` and `vm` rows after 307, and the two workspace-wide rows (`cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets`, `cargo test --workspace --no-run`) before and after 307's step 5 (F8, F9); the design's budget table (fresh-worktree disk, peak RSS at `-j4`, rust-analyzer RSS, worktrees per machine; F6) recorded fresh on a disk with at least 60 GB free; and latency under the `release` benchmark profile through 106a (F10). All of it runs alongside and gates nothing.
   - Done when: the baseline is recorded in [build-test-cost](../build-test-cost/current-state.md) on the current tree, and the split's design sets the per-edit targets from it. Each later step of item 90 re-runs the script in its own measurement record, not here.
   - 25 November 2026: script, tests (`make check-worktree-cost-script`) and a first baseline are in; remaining: (1) the baseline was taken in place on the main checkout (22 GB free, a fresh worktree needs 40 GB), so its cold rows are warm-target catch-up and the variants on disk are not counts; (2) test runs were measured for the lexer edit only, rust-analyzer not at all (no component installed); (3) a second run did not reproduce in place (the incremental cache keeps the first run's edit), so the 15% check needs a fresh worktree; (4) the split's design has not yet set per-edit targets from it.
260. **[in progress]** (build-time lever, Now 1, before step 1 of item 90; [design](../../design/build-time/260-shared-cache.md)) A compiler cache shared across worktrees, so third-party crates are compiled once per machine, not once per worktree.
   - CI pins sccache v0.18.0 (the `ci.yml` comment that says v0.17 is stale). v0.18.0 caches no incremental builds and does not normalise Rust paths across checkouts, so sharing works for third-party crates only: the edited workspace crates and the unedited ones below them are rebuilt in every worktree. Record what that leaves out, and set a target for how much of the edited crate's cone is rebuilt after an edit.
   - A pruning policy for target directories: what is deleted, when (for example incremental state older than a set age, and a worktree's whole target directory when it is removed), and by which script.
   - Measure disk and hit rate across three worktrees on one machine.
   - Incremental compilation stays on in the test profile for every workspace crate: the design's decision for 296 and 297 (deep review F6).
   - Done when: three worktrees on different isolated edits, with a warm shared cache, use less total disk than one worktree does today, the rebuilt share of the edited cone is at or below its target, the pruning policy runs in `scripts/dev/`, and the result and configuration are recorded in `scripts/dev/agent-build-env.sh` and AGENTS.md.
   - 26 November 2026: the configuration and the pruning policy are in. `agent-build-env.sh` sets `RUSTC_WRAPPER=sccache`, `SCCACHE_DIR` and `SCCACHE_CACHE_SIZE=20G` only when `sccache --version` succeeds; `scripts/dev/prune-targets.py` (`make prune-targets`, tested by `make check-prune-targets-script` in `xtask-check`) freed 9.2 GB from this checkout's target on its first run; AGENTS.md and the stale v0.17 comment in `ci.yml` are updated. Remaining: (1) sccache is not installed in agent containers, and installing it belongs to the session-start hook (work list 1), not done here; (2) the three-worktree measurement (disk, hit rate, C(e) ratio) needs sccache, worktrees and 60 GB free; (3) the pruning script does not yet run at session start, or sweep the targets of worktrees `git worktree list` no longer lists, or delete a target after ENOSPC.
261. **[designed]** (lead item for the registry split, beside item 90; [design](../../design/registry/registry-split-design.md); the crate units follow [dialects, libraries and environments](../../design/registry/dialects-and-libraries.md), items 301–307) `tcl-registry` becomes a core: the vocabulary, the library, compatibility and environment model, lookup over the surfaces it is given, and the hook API. Around it go one crate per dialect (`tcl-dialect-tcl`, `tcl-dialect-jim`, and `tcl-dialect-f5` under item 301), one crate per library package (owner decision, 26 November 2026). Examples: `tcl-lib-tk`, `tcl-lib-expect`, `tcl-lib-irules`, `tcl-lib-iapps`, `tcl-lib-tmsh`, `tcl-lib-itcl`, `tcl-lib-thread`, one crate per bundled Tcl library and one per tcllib package, and `tcl-catalogue`, the shipped list. Generic crates depend on the core only, and only entry points select surfaces.
   - Done when: items 262–265 are completed, and item 259's registry-spec edit rebuilds only its dialect or library crate, `tcl-catalogue`, and the crates that link the catalogue: the binaries, the WASM hosts, `xtask` and the `-it` crates.
262. **[completed]** ([design](../../design/registry/registry-split/01-surface-input.md); step 1 of item 261) The core takes its surfaces as input (`Registry::from_surfaces`). The surface list is written out in six places today (`SPEC_PACKS`, the `shared_group!` cells, `authored_groups`, `build_default`, `load_surface` and `compiled_command_names`), and Jim's surface has a seventh door; all of them become one installed `Surface` set, assembled by the entry points. A registry used before it is installed fails loudly. Tests get it from a test-install fallback that cargo-run processes enable and release builds compile out.
   - Done when: `rg 'commands::(irules|tk|tcllib|stdlib|expect|iapps|bpf|itcl|thread|argparse|ticklecharts|spectcl|sslictcl)::' rust/tcl-registry/src --glob '!commands/**'` finds nothing, and each entry point has a test that it installs the registry before first use.
   - 27 November 2026, as built ([design § As built](../../design/registry/registry-split/01-surface-input.md#as-built-27-november-2026)): `Surface`, `SurfaceSet`, `install`/`installed` and `CommandRegistry::from_surfaces` are in `registry/surfaces.rs`; `commands::SHIPPED` is the one list, and every default build, layer load and cross-dialect index reads the installed set. Jim is a `Placement::Family` surface (`tcl_spectcl::core_surfaces::JIM`); entry points that link `tcl-spectcl` install `core_surfaces::SURFACES`, whose install hook seeds Jim's rosters; `register_core_surface_specs`, `core_surface_generation` and `core_surfaces::ensure` are gone, and the Jim tests in `tcl-spectcl` and `tcl-compiler-it` live in `jim` harnesses that install that list per test. Every binary has an `installs_surfaces` test (the WASM and Python hosts are CI-verified). The done-when `rg` (run with `--glob '!**/commands/**'`) finds nothing. Tests get the shipped list from a fallback: an uninstalled read installs `SHIPPED` when `TCL_REGISTRY_TEST_INSTALL` is set, which `.cargo/config.toml` does for every cargo-run process; the constructor-section hook and its 223 harness lines are gone. The fallback is under `cfg(debug_assertions)`, so no release bundle carries it, and `make check-release-install-guard` (run by `build-server-matrix`) fails if a release-profile build still installs ([design](../../design/registry/registry-split/01-surface-input.md#as-built-part-3-onward-27-november-2026)).
263. **[completed]** ([design](../../design/registry/registry-split/02-behaviour-to-surfaces.md); step 2 of item 261) Behaviour moves to its surface. The 52 references in 13 files from the core into `commands::tcl` (the `fold_*` folds in `pack_hooks/native_catalogues.rs`, `subst_::OPTIONS`, the TclOO kinds, the `if` shape checks and a few helpers) become registrations in the Tcl surface's hook table, or move to `tcl-cmd-core` where they are pure command kernels (item 81). `native_try.rs` and `registry/control_arms.rs` read a fact the `try` spec already carries, so they need no registration.
   - Done when: `rg 'crate::commands::' rust/tcl-registry/src --glob '!commands/**'` finds nothing.
   - 27 November 2026, as built ([design § As built](../../design/registry/registry-split/02-behaviour-to-surfaces.md#as-built-27-november-2026)): `HookTable` has a slice per `-native ID` family plus `try_clauses`, `conditional` and `static_display`; `pack_hooks/native_catalogues.rs` is now the Tcl surface's `commands/tcl/hooks.rs`, and the SpecTcl loader and the studio pickers read `installed().native_hook` / `native_hook_ids`. The native `try` and `if` compiler facts are registrations, because their callers hold no spec (recorded in the design). `subst`'s switch table moved to `substitution`, the TclOO kinds to `definer::property_accessors` and `native_tcloo_info`, `scan_literal` and `jim_literal_class` to `tcl-cmd-core`, and `bpf-tcl-ir` asks `Traits::THREAD_PRIMITIVE` through `SurfaceSet::traits_in_any_surface`. Both done-when greps (run with `--glob '!**/commands/**'`) find nothing. Left open: the `commands/jim/` surface (design work list 6) and item 81's remaining kernel moves (work list 9).
264. **[designed]** ([design](../../design/registry/registry-split/03-extract-crates.md); step 3 of item 261) Extract the dialect and library crates and `tcl-catalogue`, with the about 14k lines of surface data outside `commands/` (the definer grammars and the iRules tables). Every consumer moves to the core plus the surfaces it names, and `registry-axes` treats the dialect and library crates as registry.
   - `tcl-dialect-f5` waits for item 301.
   - `tcl-dialect-jim` takes Jim's SpecTcl roster only after items 251 and 281; until then it takes the Rust rows.
   - `tcl-lib-irules` and `tcl-lib-iapps` need no edge to the BIG-IP schema crate.
   - `tcl-catalogue` must not depend on `tcl-spectcl`, which would put `tcl-compiler` under it.
   - Done when: the dialect and library crates build, `tcl-registry` has no `commands/` directory, `registry-axes` reports the same findings as before the move, and item 259's registry-spec edit rebuilds only that crate, `tcl-catalogue`, the binaries, the WASM hosts, `xtask` and the `-it` crates that take the catalogue. One crate per package (R16): `rust/tcl-lib-*` holds no crate with two packages' rows, and hookless crates set `test = false` and `doctest = false`.
265. **[designed]** ([design](../../design/registry/registry-split/04-dependency-hygiene.md); step 4 of item 261) Dependency hygiene. A gate (`cargo-deny` wrappers) rejects a normal dependency from a generic crate (the compiler stack, `tcl-lsp-core`, `tcl-codegen`) on a dialect or library crate. Tests take the surfaces they need as dev-dependencies and install them through `tcl_registry::testing::install!` (rule 1), which needs no catalogue edge. A split crate inherits no row of the gate's known list.
   - Done when: `cargo tree -p tcl-compiler -e normal` lists no `tcl-dialect-*` or `tcl-lib-*` crate, the gate has a test that rejects one, and its known list names no split crate.
288. **[designed]** (beside item 90, after item 251; [design](../../design/workspace/288-spectcl-edge.md); retires [project-layout](../../design/contracts/project-layout.md) rule 4's exception) Remove the upward edge `tcl-spectcl` → `tcl-lsp-core`, which the [tidy plan](architecture-tidy-plan.md#crates)'s dependency rules forbid. 13 `src` files name `tcl-lsp-core` in production (`vfs` and the two spec-pack notice types), plus test modules, three `tests/` files and `SourceUriPathUnavailable` (`rg tcl_lsp_core rust/tcl-spectcl`, 26 November 2026).
   - A new crate below both, `tcl-source-store`, takes `vfs`, `file_uri` and the decoder half of `source_decode`.
   - `ScriptEncoding` moves to `tcl-cmd-core`.
   - The notice types move to `spectcl_source`, the SpecTcl source grammar module (in `tcl-source` after item 251), not to `tcl-spectcl`; `tcl-lsp-core` turns them into code actions.
   - Done when: `cargo tree -p tcl-spectcl -e normal` lists no `tcl-lsp-core`, `rg tcl_lsp_core rust/tcl-spectcl` finds nothing, the browser host still loads packs from supplied bytes (its existing tests pass), and project-layout rule 4 no longer names the exception.
289. **[designed]** (the rest of the workspace, beside item 90; [design](../../design/workspace/289-lsp-core.md)) `tcl-lsp-core` (132k production lines, 245k with tests) splits into 11 `tcl-ide-*` feature crates plus the domain adapter `tcl-ide-f5` and the interim `tcl-ide-sslictcl` (deleted by 457). It links the domain crates `tcl-bigip` (58k), `tcl-irules` (5k) and `tcl-sslictcl` (9k) today. They contribute document kinds, tokens, findings, navigation and actions through traits in `tcl-ide-model`, which the adapters implement and the entry points register. Feature crates take `DocumentInput { text, environment, facts: FactView, context: &ContextIntervals, document: &dyn DocumentFacts }` (D-C); the extraction moves code with today's inputs and is not gated on these types.
   - Done when: `tcl-lsp-core` no longer exists, no `tcl-ide-*` crate's normal tree lists `tcl-bigip`, `tcl-irules` or `tcl-sslictcl`, each entry point (the native and WASM servers, MCP, the CLI) registers the adapters, the rank gate (314) passes with no exception for these crates, each `tcl-ide-*` crate is born with no consumer-purity pin (item 319's ledger has no row for it), and item 259's LSP-feature edit compiles none of the domain crates.
290. **[designed]** (the rest of the workspace, beside item 90; [design](../../design/workspace/290-lsp-server.md)) `tcl-lsp-server` (70k production lines of 120k in one crate). [Build-test-cost](../build-test-cost/current-state.md) recorded a decision against splitting it while `lib.rs` was 73k lines: a module split buys no build time, and a crate split means untangling `Backend`. That decision is reopened under item 90's goal. `lib.rs` is now 1,359 lines, but `Backend` still has `impl` blocks in 120 files. `Backend`'s state and handler groups split into crates over `tcl-lsp-session` (which holds `tcl-db`) and the `tcl-ide-*` crates each group needs, with the transport (`tower-lsp-server` wiring) on top. `tcl-lsp-session` holds only LSP state; 290 creates the shared `tcl-session` (layer 7 rank 1: dialect selection, configuration stack, rename tier plan; D-M). `PackState` is the pack watcher that sets `tcl-db`'s `UserPack` inputs, and `registry_for` becomes a generation-backed `FactView`.
   - Done when: the seven crates build, the rank gate (314) passes, no crate but `tcl-lsp-server` names `tower_lsp_server`, no crate but `tcl-lsp-session` and `tcl-db` names `salsa`, and item 259 records the server crates an LSP-feature edit rebuilds before and after.
291. **[designed]** (the rest of the workspace, beside item 90; [design](../../design/build-time/291-vm.md); its no-split decision is reopened as open decision D31: the crate move goes ahead unsplit and gates nothing in Now 1–5, and item 259's VM edit row runs alongside) `tcl-vm` (220k lines) after item 117. It takes the native algorithms from `tcl-native-model` rather than from `tcl-registry`. It keeps its normal edge to the registry core, which pins its environment and serves command specs at run time, but no surface crate or the catalogue may enter its cone. The two call sites that would bring one in are replaced: `events::EventRegistry::build()` and `default_registry()`.
   - Done when: `cargo tree -p tcl-vm -e normal` lists `tcl-native-model` and no `tcl-dialect-*`, `tcl-lib-*` or `tcl-catalogue` crate, and item 259's registry-spec edit does not rebuild `tcl-vm`.
292. **[designed]** (the rest of the workspace, beside item 90; [design](../../design/build-time/292-codegen.md)) `tcl-codegen` (69k lines) depends on `tcl-compiler` today. Owner decision, 26 November 2026: it splits in two.
   - `tcl-codegen` keeps the bytecode and WASM emitters, over `tcl-native-plan`, `tcl-lowering`, `tcl-analysis`, `tcl-values`, `tcl-model` and `tcl-ir`.
   - A new driver crate, `tcl-compile`, takes the compile service, the WASM pipeline and inlining, over the unit crate and `tcl-optimiser`.
   - [Step 11](../../design/compiler/crate-split/11-tcl-native-plan.md) follows this design's crate boundary; the split's own work re-points codegen after items 254–256 ([work list](../../design/build-time/292-codegen.md#work-list), step 2).
   - Done when: `cargo tree -p tcl-codegen -e normal` lists no `tcl-diagnostics`, `tcl-optimiser` or unit crate; consumers that need only emitters depend on `tcl-codegen` alone; and an analyser edit rebuilds neither crate.
293. **[designed]** (the rest of the workspace, beside item 90; [design](../../design/workspace/293-mcp.md)) `tcl-mcp` (12k lines) depends on `tcl-cli` and `f5-cli` for a handful of items (`help_json`, `CONVERTIBLE_CODES`, `conversion_for`, `explain_flow_value`, `ExplainFlowOptions`), so an edit to either rebuilds MCP. They move into two new library crates, `tcl-kcs-help` and `f5-explain`, as the tidy plan's rule (tools depend on feature APIs, not on CLIs) requires. `f5-xc` is fine as a library dependency; only its own edge to `tcl-lsp-core` (for `Finding` and `Producer`) is wrong. MCP and the CLI analyse through `tcl-db`'s session-free batch constructor (after 484; not gating 293); `f5-explain` takes the simulator and the iRules `FactView` from its caller; MCP keeps `simulate`.
   - Done when: `cargo tree -p tcl-mcp -e normal` lists no `tcl-cli` or `f5-cli`, `f5-xc`'s tree lists no `tcl-lsp-core`, and the MCP tool tests pass unchanged.
294. **[designed]** (the rest of the workspace, beside item 90; [design](../../design/workspace/294-explorer.md)) `tcl-explorer` (13k lines) depends on `tcl-vm` (four items in one file, `live_entry.rs`, behind the optional `live-entry` feature that `tcl-cli` enables), `tcl-codegen` (7 files) and `tcl-spectcl` (2 files, `bundled`). `live_entry` moves to `tcl-cli`, its only user. The explorer reads IR and plans through `tcl-ir` and `tcl-native-plan`, and its bytecode and WASM views use one named `tcl-codegen` API.
   - Done when: `cargo tree -p tcl-explorer -e normal` lists no `tcl-vm` or `tcl-spectcl`, and its `tcl-codegen` use goes through that one API.
295. **[designed]** (the rest of the workspace, beside item 90; [design](../../design/workspace/295-hub-crates.md)) The hub crates every worktree builds. Low hubs sit under nearly everything, so an edit to one rebuilds most of the workspace: `tcl-dialect` (32k lines), `tcl-lexer` (27k), `tcl-syntax` (48k), `tcl-core-types` (4k) and `tcl-registry` (583k, item 261), with 40, 34, 32, 27 and 29 direct dependents in the root workspace (44, 39, 33, 30 and 36 manifests name them across the tree). They hold only stable vocabulary and no per-feature data. Top hubs (`tcl-mcp`, `tcl-cli`, `tcl-lsp-server`, `tcl-lsp-core`, `tcl-compiler-it`) sit above nearly everything, and agent tooling builds them in every worktree. Cones are counted deduplicated (`cargo tree -e normal,build`, each crate once).
   - Done when: item 259 records the crates rebuilt after an edit to each low hub and the deduplicated cone of each top hub, the design sets a target for each, and the design's hub table names, for each hub over its target, an existing plan item that splits it or shrinks its cone.
296. **[in progress]** (build-time lever, Now 1, before step 1 of item 90; [design](../../design/build-time/296-300-build-levers.md)) Dev-only feature unification. There are four dev-only feature sets that duplicate cones. `tcl-compiler`'s `test-support` is enabled by one crate today (`tcl-codegen`'s dev-dependency), and by `tcl-compiler-it` after item 266. `tcl-vm`'s `test-support` is enabled by four (`tcl-vm` itself, `tcl-engine-tclvm`, `tcl-irule-test` and `tcl-spectcl`, all dev-dependencies). `tcl-lsp-core`'s `truth-table` and `transport-fixtures` are enabled by `tcl-lsp-server`, `tcl-cli` and `tcl-mcp`, and `tcl-irules`' `test-instrumentation` by `tcl-mcp`. A worktree that both tests such a crate and builds or checks another compiles the crate twice, with and without the feature, along with every crate above it in that build.
   - Done when: item 259 records, per crate with a dev-only feature, how many variants a worktree builds across its edits, and each such crate builds one variant or appears in the design's table with its measured cost.
   - 26 November 2026: measured and decided ([build-test-cost](../build-test-cost/current-state.md#build-levers-items-296300-and-260-before--after)). Each dev-only feature gives its crate and the crates above it two variants: 2 workspace crates for `tcl-codegen`'s test, 9 for `tcl-compiler-it`'s, 1–4 for `tcl-vm/test-support`'s four consumers, 6 for `tcl-lsp-server`'s, 3 for `tcl-cli`'s and 8 for `tcl-mcp`'s; the feature variant's lib unit alone is 284 MB for `tcl-compiler` and 104 MB for `tcl-vm`. No lever is implemented here: the design marks none as a standalone win, so `tcl-compiler` follows items 266 and 292, `tcl-vm` item 291 (with `native_conformance` kept, option 3), and `tcl-lsp-core` the `tcl-lsp-policy-table` check. Remaining: those conversions, the measured compile seconds for each accepted variant, and design rows for `tcl-explorer`'s and `tcl-bigip-query`'s dev-only features, which `tcl-cli`'s and `tcl-mcp`'s tests also turn on.
297. **[completed]** (build-time lever, Now 1, before step 1 of item 90; [design](../../design/build-time/296-300-build-levers.md)) Two profiles per worktree. The agent build environment runs check and clippy under the dev profile and tests under the test profile at opt-level 1 (`scripts/dev/agent-build-env.sh`), so a worktree that does both compiles every crate twice. Evaluate one profile for agents, such as clippy under the test profile.
   - Done when: item 259 records the crates compiled for clippy plus a test run in one worktree under both setups, and `scripts/dev/agent-build-env.sh` uses the setup that compiles fewer; and, once the generator exists, `CARGO_ALIAS_SPEC_IMAGES="run --profile test -p tcl-spec-images --"` sits beside `CARGO_ALIAS_XTASK`.
   - 26 November 2026: done. `agent-build-env.sh` exports `CARGO_ALIAS_XTASK` and `CARGO_ALIAS_XTASK_BACKING` with `--profile test`; check and clippy stay on dev, because check mode shares no unit with a build under either profile (measured: `clippy --profile test` shared 0 of 5 units with the test build, `build --profile test` shared all of its). `xtask` under the test profile reused 197 of its 272 units from earlier test builds and compiled 75, against 269 dev units shared with nothing; `make xtask-gates` went from 248 s to 81 s ([build-test-cost](../build-test-cost/current-state.md#build-levers-items-296300-and-260-before--after)). `CARGO_ALIAS_SPEC_IMAGES` is not added: `tcl-spec-images` does not exist yet (item 307), and it goes beside these when it does.
298. **[completed]** (build-time lever, Now 1, before step 1 of item 90; [design](../../design/build-time/296-300-build-levers.md)) `xtask` builds `tcl-vm` and `tcl-runtime` for every gate run, though only the `command-backing` gate needs them. Build-test-cost decided against an opt-in feature because CI would build `xtask` twice. Reopen it: move `command-backing` into a separate package, `xtask-backing`, so the gates never build an engine of their own.
   - Done when: `cargo tree -p xtask -e normal` lists no `tcl-runtime` and lists `tcl-vm` only through `tcl-spectcl`, `make xtask-gates` compiles no `tcl-runtime` unit, and CI builds `xtask` and `xtask-backing` once each.
   - 26 November 2026: done. `rust/xtask-backing` holds the gate; `make xtask-command-backing` runs `cargo xtask-backing command-backing --check` under `xtask-check` and the batch list drops it, so CI's drift leg builds each binary once. `cargo tree -p xtask -e normal -i tcl-runtime` finds no match, `tcl-vm` arrives only through `tcl-spectcl`, and `make xtask-gates` builds no `tcl-runtime` unit. Measured in place: `xtask` 272 → 270 units, a runtime edit no longer relinks `xtask` (27 s before), gates 251 → 248 s; the split costs 36 feature variants in `xtask-backing`'s own build (44 s warm), built only by `xtask-check` ([build-test-cost](../build-test-cost/current-state.md#build-levers-items-296300-and-260-before--after)).
299. **[completed]** (build-time lever, Now 1, before step 1 of item 90; [design](../../design/build-time/296-300-build-levers.md)) Build scripts. `tcl-version`'s `build.rs` re-runs on the git state (`HEAD`, the branch ref, `refs/tags`, `packed-refs`), so every commit in a worktree rebuilds it and its dependents: `tcl-cli`, `tcl-lsp-server`, `tcl-mcp`, `f5-cli` and `bigip-report-gen-rust`, plus `bigip-query-wasm` outside the workspace. Dev builds stop stamping the version from git; release and stamped builds keep it. `tcl-dialect` has 41 direct workspace dependents, and its `build.rs` generates a table into `OUT_DIR` that duplicates `RELEASE_FACTS`, which may also defeat a cache shared across worktrees (item 260). `tcl-dialect/build.rs` is deleted, and the table is read from `RELEASE_FACTS` under its `--check` gate (item 119).
   - Done when: a commit with no source change, then `cargo build -p tcl-lsp-server`, recompiles no workspace crate in a dev build, and `tcl-dialect` has no build script.
   - 26 November 2026: done. An unstamped dev build of `tcl-version` reports `0.1.0-dev` and watches no git path; `tcl-dialect/build.rs` is deleted and the patchlevel and source tag come from `RELEASE_FACTS`, which `release-facts --check` holds to `data/reference-toolchains.tsv`. A dev `cargo build -p tcl-lsp-server` after an empty commit went from 3 units and 40 s to 0 units, and after `git pack-refs` also 0 ([build-test-cost](../build-test-cost/current-state.md#build-levers-items-296300-and-260-before--after)). `bigip-report-gen-rust` still watches git for its own footer, outside the server's graph.
300. **[in progress]** (build-time lever, Now 1, before step 1 of item 90; [design](../../design/build-time/296-300-build-levers.md)) Generated code compiled as Rust. 25 files carry a generated header, with 80.8k lines, and 7 of them are over 2,000 lines (the earlier "99k lines in 53 files" counted generator sources too). The largest are `tcl-registry`'s `base_objects/generated_names.rs` and `generated_objects.rs` (22k and 20k), `tcl-dialect`'s `generated_release_facts.rs` (11k) and `tcl-bigip`'s `model/gen/canon.rs` (7k). Where a file is a table rather than code, embed it as data and parse it once at start-up. Where it overlaps item 119's generators, the generator emits the data form. Release facts embedded here are interim in `tcl-dialect`; their home is the Tcl core image's `DIALECTS` section (item 307). The registry's `base_objects/` files are embedded after they move (118a).
   - Done when: each generated file over 2,000 lines has its compile cost (`cargo build --timings`) and its load cost as data recorded, and each whose compile cost exceeds its load cost is embedded as data.
   - 26 November 2026: compile costs recorded ([build-test-cost](../build-test-cost/current-state.md#build-levers-items-296300-and-260-before--after)). Emptying a table cut its crate's dev build by 0.4–0.6 s each (`tcl-registry` 22.1 s; all four of its tables 1.4 s build, 1.6 s check), and `RELEASE_FACTS` by nothing measurable (`tcl-dialect` 1.14 s); `canon.rs` and `parsers.rs` are code and stay. Remaining: the load cost as data (no data form exists yet) and the test-profile A/B; nothing is embedded.
301. **[designed]** ([design](../../design/registry/model/301-dialects.md); step 1 of the [dialect and library model](../../design/registry/dialects-and-libraries.md); after item 264) The dialect model.
   - There are three families: `tcl` (8.4–9.1), `jim` (0.76–0.84) and `f5-tcl`.
   - f5-tcl's releases are the BIG-IP releases, and `tmm` and `scriptd` are its builds. `Family::F5Irules` is removed. The `irules_brace_separator` and `brace_line_continuation` axes belong to the f5-tcl family under both builds, and the rule-loader flags (`closed_load_time_resolution`, `declaration_only_top_level`) move to the iRules library.
   - The f5-tcl evidence model (`tcl-registry`'s `f5/`, item 118): its builds and releases move to `tcl-dialect`, the rest of `f5/` splits per 118 decision 1, and `tcl-dialect-f5` is created here (item 264).
   - Jim's `full` and `minimal` builds and its extension capabilities become selectable.
   - `tcl-dialect` holds dialects only: `DialectProfile`'s `CATALOG` of 19 mixed rows is retired in favour of environments.
   - Done when: `Family` has three variants. `rg 'F5Irules' rust` finds no match outside the SpecTcl 1.x/2.x shims. An environment can select `jim` `minimal` and f5-tcl `scriptd`. The lexer is configured from the dialect alone.
302. **[designed]** ([design](../../design/registry/model/302-libraries.md); step 2 of the model) The library model.
   - Libraries carry version trains, compatibility rows (a library range with a dialect family, range and build or capability predicate; the rows are OR'd, and each row is a conjunction), requirements on other libraries with ranges, tracks-host versions, and bundled placements generated per Tcl release (items 79 and 202). A requirement or floor can be per train, as `TcllibModule.core_floor` is.
   - Bundled versions come from `library/*/pkgIndex.tcl` and `pkgs/<dir>/configure.ac`; `pkgs/package.list.txt` carries no versions, and `fetch-tcl-source` keeps the `pkgs/` trees for the generator.
   - Each command, subcommand and option carries one `Availability`: a window on its library's axis, together with an optional dialect constraint. It replaces `SpecSurface` rows, `required_package`, `tcllib_package` and `lifecycle_axis`.
   - Package windows in `available` are carried (D17-P fixed), and `package require` is read as a full range with trains.
   - Tk's 16 rows keyed "8.7" are re-keyed to the real Tk release that introduced each one, read from the Tk sources in `tmp/tk*/` (owner decision, 26 November 2026).
   - Done when: Itcl 3.x/4.x, Thread 2.x/3.x, Tk 8.4–9.0, TclOO (a library on 8.5, core from 8.6) and one multi-train tcllib module each answer visibility correctly across the release matrix in a new conformance area; `required_package` and `tcllib_package` are gone.
303. **[designed]** ([design](../../design/registry/model/303-environments.md); step 3 of the model) Environments as data over items 301 and 302: a dialect selector with placed libraries (ambient, hosted or bundled), policy, editor identity, detection rows, and one alias table. The compiled-in environments live in `tcl-catalogue`.
   - Done when: every name that resolves today resolves to the same environment through the one alias table, and the hand-written `CATALOG` rows, the duplicated alias lists and the two keyed-axis enums are gone.
304. **[designed]** ([design](../../design/registry/model/304-f5-libraries.md); step 4 of the model) iRules, iApps and tmsh become libraries on f5-tcl: iRules tracks its host on build `tmm`, and iApps and tmsh run on `scriptd`. The `is_irules()` checks and `"f5-irules"` strings in `tcl-compiler`, `tcl-lsp-core`, `tcl-lsp-server` and `tcl-cli` become queries on the resolved environment (is the iRules library present, which build), and `irules_policy` leaves generic visibility.
   - Done when: `rg 'is_irules\(\)' rust` finds none outside the environment API, and the iRules and iApps conformance rows are unchanged.
305. **[designed]** ([design](../../design/registry/model/305-spectcl-3.md); step 5 of the model; needs item 302 and the compiler split's item 251; superseded in detail by the [consolidated SpecTcl 3 specification](../../design/registry/spectcl-3/README.md), whose items 400–479 carry the rest) SpecTcl 3.
   - The language gains `dialect`, `library` (with `compatible`, `requires`, trains and `tracks-host`) and `environment` forms, and per-command `available LIBRARY-RANGE ?-dialect …?`.
   - 1.x and 2.x packs load through translation shims, and `tcl spec upgrade` rewrites them to 3.
   - The loader lexes with `tcl-source` and reads files through a source-store trait, so it no longer depends on `tcl-compiler` or `tcl-lsp-core`.
   - sslictcl changes with its consumers and gets no shim.
   - Done when: every bundled pack and the test corpus load under 3. The 1.x and 2.x fixtures give equivalent answers (visibility, arity and roles across the release matrix) to their upgraded forms. `rg 'tcl_compiler|tcl_lsp_core' rust/tcl-spectcl/src/loader rust/tcl-spectcl/src/loader.rs` and the same over the 3.0 reader's modules find nothing.
306. **[designed]** ([design](../../design/registry/model/306-expect-spike.md); step 6 of the model) Spike: Expect as data. Author Expect in SpecTcl 3, compile it to a binary image that `tcl-catalogue` embeds, and load it without parsing.

308. **[designed]** (step 8 of the dialect and library model; in two parts (26 November 2026): 308a in Now 7 after items 253, 302 and 303, 308b after item 84 (stage 11); [design](../../design/registry/model/308-positioned-context.md), [integration](../../design/compiler/compiler-analyser-spectcl-integration.md#10-proposed-plan-steps) step INT-d) One positioned library and dialect query, `positioned_context(file, offset)`, built on binding's package transitions. It is computed in `tcl-binding` and memoised in `tcl-db` (integration C5).
   - It handles ranges, `-exact`, alternatives, conditional and in-procedure requires (as sets), trains, workspace `package provide` and `tclpkg.tcl`, placements, targets and the detected dialect.
   - Every version consumer uses it: the version gate, hover and documentation, completion, signature help, MCP `command_info` and the CLI.
   - 308a, whole-file selection: the selection at the end of the file from `SignaturePackageRequire`, with `Must` downgraded to `May` inside procedure bodies, from the structural walk. Opaque code (`source`, an unknown command, `eval`, `uplevel`) makes a hosted `Absent` library `Unknown`, and W120 fires on `Absent` only. Done when the version gate reads `&ContextIntervals`, `cargo tree -p tcl-diagnostics -e normal` lists no `tcl-db`, and these `positioned-versions` conformance rows are green: Tk 8.5 against 8.6, `-exact Itcl 3.4`, no require, a require behind opaque code, a require on one branch, multi-train, alternatives, iRules by BIG-IP version, and a targets range.
   - 308b, per position: procedure entry selections computed top-down in `tcl-unit` after condensation (integration C19), on item 84's single binding walk. Done when the in-procedure row and a cross-file row are green, each component's re-binding count is recorded, `package_version_floor`, `DocumentFloor` and the `V9_1` fallbacks are gone from the analyser's diagnostics (`tcl-diagnostics`) and the LSP feature crates, `ResolvedContext::require_package` has a production caller or is deleted, and the query is the only version source for those consumers.
309. **[designed]** (after item 305; blocked by D6 and D17 ([chapter 11](../../design/registry/spectcl-3/11-open-decisions.md)); assumes their recommendations; [design](../../design/registry/model/309-310-migration-and-authoring.md)) Upgrade user packs to SpecTcl 3 automatically.
   - One engine, `upgrade_to_3`, rewrites declarative packs by rule. It expands programmed 2.x packs once in the sandbox into literal 3 rows, with a generator header.
   - It proves equivalent answers across the release matrix before writing.
   - Surfaces: the CLI (`tcl spec upgrade --to 3`, with `--check` for CI), an LSP diagnostic and code action, a workspace "Upgrade all packs" command, Spec Studio's button, MCP `spectcl_upgrade` (dry run by default), and `tcl pkg` reporting.
   - Nothing writes without a user action.
   - Done when: every bundled, example, draft and fixture pack, plus a programmed 2.x pack, upgrades with a clean equivalence report. A second upgrade is a no-op. The LSP and MCP give byte-identical output to the CLI.
310. **[designed]** (with item 309; [design](../../design/registry/model/309-310-migration-and-authoring.md)) Authoring and AI tooling emits SpecTcl 3 through one canonical printer in `tcl-spectcl`.
   - The printer is used by `upgrade_to_3`, `tcl spec export`, `spectcl_expand`, `spec_import`, Spec Studio, and the equivalence-pack generator while it exists.
   - `spec_import` infers trains, compatibility rows and facets from version snapshots. `spectcl_check` validates 3. `command_info` reports facets for the positioned context.
   - The `spec-author` skill and the AI system prompts are rewritten for 3: the lowest rung first, `check` rules before hooks, no templating, and a validation loop.
   - The examples and KCS how-tos move to 3, with the 2.x files kept as shim fixtures.
   - Done when: no SpecTcl writer in the tree emits a declaration below 3. The skill's eval suite passes: three sample libraries, each producing a pack that passes `spectcl_check`, covers every exported command, uses a `check` rule where one suffices, and has no templating. `spectcl_check` rejects templating in a 3 pack.
311. **[designed]** (after item 312's steps 1–3; owner decision, 26 November 2026: it does not block item 306, which stores hook source until 311 lands; [design](../../design/compiler/bytecode-image.md)) Serialised bytecode in the tbcx format (owner decision, 26 November 2026: tbcload stops at Tcl 9.0).
   - Units use tbcx's binary format 93. *Interop* units load in C Tcl 9.1 with tbcx 1.2.1 at the stamped patchlevel, and we load theirs from any pinned 9.1.x. They target Tcl 9.1 with the plain `tcl` profile, and are compiled as tbcx compiles: plain dispatch, `startCommand` kept, and tbcx's proc and method rewrite.
   - *Extended* units (format word `0x8001_005D`) cover targets below 9.1, other dialects and private opcodes. C Tcl refuses them at the header. Interop export lowers private opcodes where a 9.1 sequence with the same semantics exists, and refuses otherwise.
   - Our metadata (profile, source namespace, native-compilation fields, specialisation stamps, source hash, command map) goes in a `TbcxUnit` wrapper in pack images, and in a `.tbcx.meta` sidecar for standalone files. There is one binary codec.
   - The writer and reader live in `tcl-bytecode::tbcx`. Validation runs on load, before anything executes.
   - Until items 423 and 485, units are `tcl` profiles 8.4–9.1 only: the writer and the reader refuse any other profile, and such units compile from source. Every loaded unit enters the VM through `Vm::admitted_foreign_unit` and the provenance contract's per-rung manifest checks.
   - Owner decisions (26 November 2026):
     - TclOO methods in foreign `.tbcx` files execute.
     - Interop units are re-exported on every Tcl patchlevel or tbcx format bump.
     - The tbcx pin is checked on every dependency update.
   - Done when:
     - `read(write(u)) == u` and `into_module(from_module(m)) == m` hold over the bytecode-compare corpus for 8.4–9.1;
     - tbcx 1.2.1 in `tclsh` 9.1.0 loads every corpus interop unit with results equal to `source`, and our VM loads every `tbcx::save` fixture (method records stay refused until decided);
     - each block's code bytes equal `tbcx::save`'s, or the block is on a listed divergence list;
     - native and WASM execution agree;
     - there is one rejection fixture per check, and a `tcl-fuzz` `tbcx-decode` campaign runs clean;
     - pack images store hook bodies as `TbcxUnit`.
312. **[designed]** (owner decision, 26 November 2026: brought forward, not deferred; in Now 6, after the splits and before item 311; [design](../../design/compiler/vm-tcl-9-1.md)) The VM on a Tcl 9.1 base.
   - `Op` gets C Tcl 9.1 opcode numbers. They are generated from the pinned 9.1.0 `tclInstructionTable[]` by `cargo xtask opcode-table --check`, with private opcodes numbered from 255 downward and a per-release admission table. Codes 0–210 must equal the pinned 9.1.0 table, because tbcx validates against the running C Tcl. The admission table gains an export-lowering column, which item 311's interop export uses.
   - The missing `dictRemove`, `arithSeries` and `foreach_index` opcodes are added.
   - Every dispatch arm follows 9.1 semantics, and deprecated opcodes stay executable for 8.4–9.0 targets.
   - Codegen selects operand width at emit time.
   - Registry release ranges say which of the eight newly compiled commands, and `switch -integer`, compile on which release.
   - The bytecode-compare corpus gains 9.1 goldens and a byte-level mode. `runtime/rust` follows through item 88.
   - The default release moves to 9.1 in `tclvm`, `tcl-engine-tclvm` and `tcl-spec-hooks`. Each release runs on its own pinned Tcl library, selected and embedded by the platform-bootstrap owner (`tcl-platform`); `TCL_LIBRARY` is honoured only for the selected release. The 9.1 commands `divmod`, `frexp`, `lfilter`, `modf`, `remquo`, `timer` and `unicode` gain handlers. All of this stays in Now 6 after the splits. `runtime/rust` stays on 9.0 until item 88, and lanes that name it pin `--tcl-version 9.0`.
   - Done when: `opcode-table --check` is green and all 211 slots have VM arms. `bytecode-compare -v 9.1 all` matches by disassembly and by bytes, or each mismatch is on a listed divergence. Releases 8.4–9.0 show no new failures (`failset.py`). The conformance rows for the new commands pass against `tclsh` 9.1.0 and 9.0.4, native and WASM. `tclvm` with no `--tcl-version` reports 9.1.0, and `tclvm --tcl-version 9.1` passes every 9.1 conformance row with the same result as `tclsh9.1`, with `TCL_LIBRARY` unset and with it set to the 9.0.4 library (each run records both).
313. **[designed]** (after items 305 and 311, in Now 8; [design](../../design/registry/model/313-consumer-hooks.md)) SpecTcl facts and hooks for every consumer: the optimiser, minifier, formatter, VM and runtime.
   - **Optimiser:**
     - permissions come from effect facts, and `optimise -no` can only withdraw them;
     - `CSE_CANDIDATE` splits into that permission and `report redundant`;
     - `const_fold` absorbs its versioned form;
     - `rewrite` rules (templates, native or Tcl) carry proof sets built by `tcl spec prove`;
     - a rule applies only when its proof set covers the positioned target set. Owner decision, 26 November 2026: proved rewrites from user packs are on by default.
   - **Minifier:**
     - `-scope`, `-frozen`, `-binds-vars` and `-value-role` facts;
     - abstention per name;
     - `tcl spec check --consumer minifier`.
   - **Formatter:** `layout` rows, and a `layout` hook family that only chooses break points.
   - **VM and runtime:**
     - execution from the library's own source by default;
     - opt-in native accelerators through `tcl-engine-api`, which never win version selection and may ship in the editor binaries (owner decision, 26 November 2026);
     - `PackFactStamp::library_versions`.
   - **Images** gain `RULES`, `TABLES`, `BODIES`, `IMPLEMENTATIONS` and `PROOFS` sections. One `hook_abi` version covers every consumer's native hook tables.
   - Done when: every example in the design is a green conformance row on 8.6 and 9.0. A rewrite whose proof set misses the target set abstains. The minifier and formatter read roles only through the resolved invocation. The formatter is idempotent over the conformance corpus. Minified and original corpus scripts print the same output under `tclsh` 8.6 and 9.0. `CSE_CANDIDATE` is gone from `traits.rs`.
314. **[designed]** (the rest of the workspace, beside item 90; lands with item 288; [design](../../design/workspace/overview.md#the-gate)) The rank gate. Today's `crate-layer` gate protects four crates through `deny.toml`'s hand-written `wrappers` lists. It becomes a rank ledger, `docs/work/name-handling/ownership/crate-layers.tsv`, with a `crate`, `layer`, `rank` and `role` row for every crate, `runtime/rust` and the excluded WASM manifests included. It fails on an edge to a higher layer or rank (dev-dependencies included), on a generic crate naming a surface crate, on a domain crate under a feature crate, on a dependency on a binary, on a crate without a row, and on a feature used only from a dev-dependency. Today's violations are listed in `crate-layer-exceptions.tsv`, which may only shrink, and `deny.toml`'s bans are generated from the ledger. Each of items 288–295 removes its own exceptions (289's and 290's done-whens check theirs).
   - Done when: the gate runs in `make xtask-gates` with a row for every crate, and `cargo xtask crate-layer --write` regenerates `deny.toml`'s entries and the gate checks they agree.
315. **[designed]** (with items 288, 289 and 270; [design](../../design/workspace/289-lsp-core.md#tests-and-dev-dependencies)) `tcl-lsp-it`, the LSP stack's test-only integration crate at layer 11, like `tcl-compiler-it` (item 266). It takes the tests that need a feature crate and a crate above it: `tcl-spectcl`'s pack-formatting test (288), the `tests/` files that name several feature groups, and `tests/conformance`.
   - Done when: the crate exists and nothing depends on it, the moved tests give the same outcomes by name (`failset.py compare`), and no crate's dev-dependencies name a crate above it.

It takes `diagnostic_policy::truth_table` and `check_payload::family_fixtures`, which sit behind `tcl-lsp-core`'s `truth-table` and `transport-fixtures` features today. `tcl-cli`, `tcl-mcp` and the server's handler crates take it as a dev-dependency, so no worktree compiles `tcl-ide-diagnostics` twice.
   - Done when: no successor of `tcl-lsp-core` has a `truth-table` or `transport-fixtures` feature, and item 259 records one variant of `tcl-ide-diagnostics` across a test-and-build sequence.
317. **[designed]** (after item 294; [design](../../design/workspace/295-hub-crates.md)) A `tcl-explore` binary for the compiler-explorer skill, which today builds the whole `tcl-cli` (227 crates) to run `tcl explore`. After 294 the explorer's cone holds no loader, VM or domain crate, so a small binary above `tcl-explorer` (or in it) gives the skill the same output for a fraction of the build. It is adopted if its cone is under half of `tcl-cli`'s.

318. **[in progress]** (in two parts: 318a in Now 1; the rest carried by items 305–307 in Now 6; [design](../../design/workspace/overview.md), [SpecTcl 3](../../design/registry/model/305-spectcl-3.md), [integration](../../design/compiler/compiler-analyser-spectcl-integration.md#10-proposed-plan-steps) step INT-i) Remove the chain `tcl-spectcl` → `tcl-spec-hooks` → `tcl-engine-tclvm` → `tcl-vm` and `tcl-codegen`. The pack loader runs packs in the VM, so the chain links the VM and the code generator into the server, the explorer, Spec Studio and MCP. Under SpecTcl 3 the reader, loader and image generator (`tcl-spec-images`) link no VM; Tcl-body tabulation is the separate `tcl-spec-tabulate` pass, and the hook runner is a dependency named entry crates link, not a feature (D-I).
   - 318a, now: `tcl-spectcl`'s dependency on `tcl-spec-hooks` moves behind a non-default feature, which the entry points that load programmed packs enable (the server, the CLI, MCP and Spec Studio), so a crate that only reads `tcl-spectcl`'s data stops linking the VM and the code generator. Done when `cargo tree -p tcl-spectcl -e normal` lists no `tcl-spec-hooks`, the pack tests pass under each entry point with the feature on, and item 259 records the cones that shrank.
   - Done when: `cargo tree -p tcl-spectcl -e normal` (default features) lists no `tcl-spec-hooks`, `tcl-engine-tclvm`, `tcl-vm` or `tcl-codegen`; `cargo tree -p CRATE -e normal -i tcl-vm` prints nothing for `tcl-spectcl`, `tcl-explorer`, every `tcl-ide-*` crate and every compiler crate; and the server, explorer, Spec Studio and MCP reach `tcl-vm` only through `tcl-spec-hooks`, linked by an entry point that enables Tcl hooks.
   - 27 November 2026: 318a done ([measurements](../build-test-cost/current-state.md)). The feature is `programmed-packs`. The server, the CLI and `tcl-cli-support`, MCP, Spec Studio, `xtask`, `tcl-compiler-it` and `tcl-lsp-db-it` enable it, and `tcl-spectcl`'s own tests enable it through a dev-dependency on the crate itself. Without it, the static reader loads every shipped pack, a pack that needs the interpreter fails its load naming the feature, and hook bodies stay dormant (no slot). `denied_axis` moved from `tcl-spec-hooks` into the loader, its only production caller. The loader records the host's `PACK_EVAL_EXTRA_COMMANDS` the way it already recorded `SANDBOX_COMMANDS`, and a unit test holds the two lists equal. `tcl-spectcl`'s cone went from 141 to 133 packages (28 to 21 workspace crates), `tcl-explorer`'s from 142 to 135. An edit to `tcl-engine-tclvm` now rebuilds none of the explorer's test cone: 0.2 s, against 4 crates in 22.0 s before. Open, for the rest of 318: a target that builds both the explorer's tests and an entry point compiles `tcl-spectcl` and `tcl-explorer` twice, one variant per feature set.

Commands declare which type they produce (by argument when needed) and which type each argument accepts, with effects (borrow, release, move, transform, store). `ObjectHandleFacts` becomes `HandleFacts`, which tracks type and state through variables, procs, containers and callbacks.
   - Steps 391a–391g: the model, the engine, channels on rows (retiring `ChannelKind`), children and cascades, iRules lifetimes (HSL, SIDEBAND), editor surfaces, and user-library fixtures (Redis, TDBC).
   - Done when: a user pack alone gives a third-party handle family the same wrong-type, use-after-release and leak diagnostics as `open`; a call with a frame effect (`upvar`, `global`, `uplevel`, a rename) or to an unknown callee demotes the caller's handle facts to `May` (391b), in a test per effect; and every object-type test passes unchanged.

The engines raise from the same table.
   - Steps 392a–392g: the model, summaries, catch and try facts, dialect forms (8.4, 8.6's `try`, Jim, f5-tcl), editor surfaces, one table for the engines, and optimiser folds.
   - Decided (chapter 11): version-gate findings over a declared target range are a separate, opt-in portability class (D24); error-code fields take the closed `FIELD-TYPE` set `command`, `variable`, `integer`, `path`, `text` (D25).
   - Done when: a `trap` arm or error-code comparison that cannot match is reported only against a complete set, where every call in the body claims `throws -complete`; other error sets are open and carry the implicit `TCL LIMIT`, `TCL READ VARNAME`, `TCL WRITE VARNAME` and `TCL LOOKUP VARNAME` codes (392b); an unknown command raises `Any` and draws no warning; the `catch` fold applies only to a body with no calls and no variable access (392g); completion inside `trap` offers the body's codes; and the conformance area runs every declared code on tclsh 8.4–9.1, jimsh and both engines.

The reader tables, printer, `spectcl` surface, Studio schema, docs and `registry-axes` vocabulary are generated from it. It adds the pack-scope forms (`variable`, `include`, `roster`, `values`, `descriptor`), the top-level `hook`, `deprecated` and `retired`, build and capability facet keys, the one `kinds` set, multi-role `arg`, a role-less prefix, decoded quoted prose and the `vsatisfies` range reading.

495. **[designed]** (step 14 of item 90; Now 5, last; [design](../../design/compiler/crate-split/14-caching.md)) Caching, limited to the caches the split leaves. Every memo row gets a disposition: a constant, deleted, an input or state. Three caches (binding per file, source words, retained modules) may become queries of the one database at the top (item 286's `tcl-db`), and only if deleting them costs more than 5% on the latency gate.
   - Done when: every memo row of the step's design has its disposition applied, and the memo gate finds zero `Mutex`, `OnceLock`, `thread_local!` memos or `Box::leak` outside `tcl-db`. Its latency gate is item 106's table, measured through 106a.

Item 272's method is the model: a test that asserts private state is rewritten against the owning crate's public API or the oracle, or deleted with a record of what it asserted.

The [ownership guide](ownership/README.md#test-cleanup-survey) describes the recognised syntax and the manual review required. This completes the survey, not the per-crate cleanup.
   - **Order:** each crate's tests are cleaned when its split step moves it (Now 3–5 rewrite tests against the new crate's public API anyway), so the cleanup adds no extra rebuild cycle; crates no split step touches are cleaned in ledger order, largest first.

### BIG-IP and iRules questions

The answers below use the lab BIG-IP VE on `tank`, running 21.1.0.1
build 0.0.26, and repository and F5 sources. Items 111, 118 and 119 and the
definition audit can use the decisions below. Item 107 remains the bounded
release-fix tranche.

- **Q1. Oracle.** What can stand in for tclsh when checking iRules and BIG-IP definitions: a BIG-IP VE or lab system (`tmsh`, iRule compile and `test`/`fakeCMP`), F5 clouddocs scrapes (the 999 iRules specs cite clouddocs URLs), captured validation output, or customer configurations? Which BIG-IP versions must be covered, and how are per-version differences recorded?

  **Answered.** Use live TMSH validation and iRule compilation for acceptance and actual TMM flows for runtime behaviour. Use the separately extracted checker as an offline compile oracle. Pin 17.1.x, 17.5.x and 21.1.x for live coverage. Use versioned F5 sources and captured validation for historical rows, and record build, module, profile, context and outcome for every measured row. Treat `fakeCMP` as a local regression tool and customer configurations as corpus inputs. [Oracle and coverage](evidence/bigip-language-and-release.md#q1-oracle-and-release-coverage) · [21.1 TMSH checks](../../../evidence/bigip/tmsh-21.1-name-handling.md) · [Offline checker cross-check](../../../evidence/bigip/tmsh-21.1-name-handling.md#offline-checker-cross-check) · [21.1 TMM flow](../../../evidence/tcl/bigip-name-handling-tls-http.md)
- **Q2. Generated data with no generator.** Where did `base_objects/generated_{objects,names}.rs` (a 17.1 base-configuration snapshot), `bigip/references.rs`, `profile_defaults/generated.rs`, `events/event_props_table.rs`, `profiles/profile_table.rs`, `event_facts/generated.rs` and `tcl-bigip/src/model/gen/` come from (tool, inputs, BIG-IP version)? Can the generators be recovered or rewritten, or should these become hand-maintained data with schema checks?

  **Answered.** Git history recovered the original model generator and an earlier profile-default generator, with their inputs. The base object snapshot is 17.1, and the reference edges and event categories entered in #796. The provenance table records which exact emitters and inputs are still unavailable, and which data should be generated or authored and checked. Use private vendor captures as data points for independently authored evidence. [Provenance by artifact](evidence/bigip-data-provenance.md#q2-table-and-generator-provenance)
- **Q3. Versions.** Is 17.1 the only base-configuration version, and how should other versions (15.x, 16.x, 17.5, 21.x) be added: per-version tables, deltas, or a version column?

  **Answered.** 17.1 is the sole embedded base-object snapshot. On the 21.1 appliance both SSL defaults match the existing 21.1 profile override. The embedded base-object snapshot is a separate dataset. Capture by exact release/build, then ship deterministic deltas or derived version intervals. Keep 15.1 and 16.1 as historical coverage and label fallback values. [Snapshot decision](evidence/bigip-data-provenance.md#q3-release-snapshots) · [Measured default fields](../../../evidence/bigip/tmsh-21.1-name-handling.md#shipped-and-effective-defaults)
- **Q4. Ownership and placement.** Should the BIG-IP configuration schema (about 176,000 lines in `bigip/`, `base_objects/`, `profile_defaults/`, `f5/`) become its own data crate, and is `tcl-bigip`'s model generated from it or is the registry generated from `tcl-bigip`? Which of `tcl-bigip`, `tcl-bigip-query`, `bigip-report-gen` and `f5-xc` own which facts (object graph, partition rules, monitor expressions, event order, profile requirements)?

  **Answered.** Use a dedicated configuration-schema data crate and generate `tcl-bigip` model projections from it. `tcl-bigip` owns SCF semantics, scoped names, typed monitor expressions and the actual device graph. Query, report and XC own their projections. The registry owns iRules event order, phases and profile requirements. [Ownership and dependency direction](evidence/bigip-data-provenance.md#q4-ownership)
- **Q5. Event and profile facts.** Which source is authoritative for event order, event-to-profile requirements and per-event command availability? Known disagreements to resolve: `f5-cli explain_flow` orders `CLIENTSSL_HANDSHAKE` before `CLIENTSSL_CLIENTHELLO`; MCP `irule_gen` gives SERVERSSL events a CLIENTSSL profile; the report generator, VS Code skeleton and `f5-xc` keep their own tables.

  **Answered.** Live TLS placed `CLIENTSSL_CLIENTHELLO` before `CLIENTSSL_HANDSHAKE`. The backend placed `SERVERSSL_CLIENTHELLO_SEND` and `SERVERSSL_SERVERHELLO` before `SERVERSSL_HANDSHAKE`. TMSH rejected CLIENTSSL alone for server handshake and accepted SERVERSSL or either tested persistence profile for attachment. Client handshake and server ClientHello send each require their own SSL profile. Use the registry for shared facts, with separate attachment, runtime and command/event results. [Event and profile decisions](evidence/bigip-language-and-release.md#q5-events-and-profiles) · [Attachment cases](../../../evidence/bigip/tmsh-21.1-name-handling.md#ssl-rule-attachment) · [Ordered TLS trace](../../../evidence/tcl/bigip-name-handling-tls-http.md)
- **Q6. iRules command definitions.** How should the 1,018 iRules spec files be checked: arity, subcommands, per-event availability, per-version availability, `disabled_in_irules` Tcl commands, deprecated commands, and return types? What counts as evidence when no runtime is available here?

  **Answered.** Audit by version, invocation form, module/profile and event, then check representative return values and effects in TMM. Record disabled, deprecated, removed and unmeasured distinctly. On 21.1, `exec` and `open` were disabled, `matchclass` still ran, and two apparent `HTTP::header` arity errors wrote headers. Follow-up probes measured exact selector spelling and three-operand `matchclass` arity. Use offline checker observations, versioned F5 manuals and pinned appliance transcripts. Keep vendor files and raw tables outside the project. [Audit method](evidence/bigip-language-and-release.md#q6-audit-of-the-1018-irules-specs) · [Compiler cases](../../../evidence/bigip/tmsh-21.1-name-handling.md#rule-compiler) · [Runtime cases](../../../evidence/tcl/bigip-name-handling-tls-http.md)
- **Q7. Monitor and other value grammars.** Is `value::monitor_expression` complete for every monitor rule form (`and`, `min N of {…}`, quoted names, partitions), and which other configuration value grammars (persistence, iRule priority, pool member and address forms, route domains) need typed parsers instead of word splitting?

  **Answered.** The monitor parser needs quote decoding, complete consumption and threshold validation. TMSH accepts both references in an `and` chain and a list of references in `min N of`. A literal `and` inside that list was looked up as a monitor name. Reuse typed persistence, member, address and route-domain values and the shared iRule priority reader. [Parser comparison](evidence/bigip-language-and-release.md#q7-configuration-value-grammars) · [Loader cases](../../../evidence/bigip/tmsh-21.1-name-handling.md#monitor-rules)
- **Q8. Names and partitions.** One rule for `/Partition/folder/name`, `~Common~name`, route-domain suffixes `%N`, IPv6 forms and the `(no partition)` rendering: which crate owns it, and which consumers must move to it?

  **Answered.** Make `tcl-bigip::object_names` the path and partition owner, `tcl-bigip::value` the typed address/service owner and iControl REST the tilde-encoding boundary. The appliance accepts `:port` on the tested qualified node names and `.port` on the tested IPv6 literal with a route domain. Render `(no partition)` in summaries from an absent partition. [Consumers and ownership](evidence/bigip-language-and-release.md#q8-partition-paths-addresses-and-transport-encoding) · [Name and address cases](../../../evidence/bigip/tmsh-21.1-name-handling.md#names-route-domains-and-services) · [21.1 address grammar evidence](../../../evidence/bigip/tmsh-21.1-address-grammars.md)
- **Q9. Taint and security facts.** Where should iRules taint sources and sinks, sensitive headers and credential arguments live (spec subcommand fields, spec-level lists or a separate table), given they are duplicated today?

  **Answered.** Keep taint and credential operand roles on resolved invocation forms in the registry, with a shared sensitive header classification. The Authorization read and implicit writes demonstrate why the effect and name/value positions must come from the selected form. [Registry placement](evidence/bigip-language-and-release.md#q9-taint-and-credentials) · [Header results](../../../evidence/tcl/bigip-name-handling-tls-http.md)
- **Q10. Scope against the release.** Which BIG-IP and iRules fixes belong in the current LSP release (wrong answers such as the orphan-monitor false positive and the SSL event order), and which wait for the data crate and generators?

  **Answered.** Ship the bounded wrong-answer fixes from item 107 with focused regressions for compound monitors, SSL order and side-specific profiles. Include individual name or command fixes when their regressions establish the change. Keep the full owner migration, data crate, generators and catalogue audit in items 111, 118 and 119 and the definition audit. [Release scope and acceptance](evidence/bigip-language-and-release.md#q10-release-scope)

Focused compiler, registry, SpecTcl loader, Studio web and VS Code checks
passed for the header forms, event order and TLS attachments. The MCP
scaffold suite passed all 25 tests. The BIG-IP data schema, editor
catalogue, iRule-test data and Tcl execution evidence checks passed. The
address fixtures, Rust report and query checks, and iControl codec
regressions also passed.

The eight failures in the earlier full registry run reproduced on
`6dc49c2452`. A full `rust-check` run stopped at a pre-existing Clippy
error in `tcl-codegen/src/codegen/native_compiler_pass.rs`. The `prep-pr`
run hit the local Make wrapper during the VS Code typecheck and an
outdated dependency build during smoke compilation. A direct GNU Make
rerun passed the VS Code typecheck and bundling, and a fresh focused
build passed the REST and capture regressions. The direct iRule host binding and SpecTcl round-trip tests passed. The full
live-session test stopped during bootstrap at the existing logical source
file provider assertion, before it could invoke the binding. The full
smoke gate and advisory equivalence-pack drift remain open. The work branch's AGENTS.md
exception permits incremental development pushes.


Before the final PR, move useful contracts and real experiments/tests into durable locations. Correct documentation/comments across the entire tracked repository, including generated guidance and files outside the implementation diff. Remove obsolete guidance, completed TODOs, development narratives, temporary ledgers/prompts/archives and their references, including this directory and the work-specific AGENTS exception. Preserve reproducible evidence and legal attribution. Complete all gates before creating a ready-to-merge PR; do not merge. This final cleanup is still required and is not completed by source-audit closure.
- **Q11. The tmsh `cli script` interpreter.** Is the interpreter that runs tmsh `cli script` objects the `scriptd` build of f5-tcl, or a third build? Item 301 needs the answer to model it. Known differences: it differs from iApps' `scriptd` in `tcl_platform` and `info vartype`.

  **Open.** For an agent with access to the lab BIG-IP on `tank`: in a `cli script`, in an iApp template's implementation and in iRules (where it applies), compare `tcl_platform` (including `wordSize`), `info patchlevel`, `info vartype` behaviour, `info commands` and the parse-parity cases in [the parser measurements](../../design/f5/bigip-irule-parser-measurements.md). Record the evidence under `evidence/bigip/`, and answer: `scriptd`, or a third build named by the evidence.

- **Q12. F5-bundled packages in the iApp context.** Which versions of `http`, `uri`, `uuencode`, `sha256` and the other packages that `package names` lists in an iApp implementation ship with each supported BIG-IP release? Are they F5's own copies or tcllib's?

  **Open.** For now they are modelled as F5-bundled libraries on f5-tcl `scriptd` with unknown versions (owner decision, 26 November 2026). For an agent with the lab BIG-IP on `tank`, in an iApp template's implementation and in a `cli script`:
  - run `package names`, then `package require` each name;
  - record `package present`, `info loaded`, and the `package ifneeded` script for each package;
  - record the results under `evidence/bigip/`, and replace the unknown versions with the measured ones.
- **Supported BIG-IP releases (owner decision, 26 November 2026).** Only releases still in public support according to F5's K5902 article are modelled. The f5-tcl release ladder's lower bound follows that article, and command history before it is not dated.
- **Q13. Both-leg event order and conditional events.** Run a full trace with `HTTP::collect` on both sides, with and without OneConnect, on 17.1, 17.5 and 21.1. **Open**, for a lab probe ([360](../../design/registry/model/360-spectcl-f5-integration.md)).
- **Q14. The event set per release.** Compile `when E {}` for all 176 registry event names on each pinned release. **Open**, for a lab probe ([360](../../design/registry/model/360-spectcl-f5-integration.md)).
- **Q15. The command × event compile matrix per release.** Generate the matrix from the facts per form, extending 360 §8's 120 pairs. Record warnings separately. **Open**, for a lab probe ([360](../../design/registry/model/360-spectcl-f5-integration.md)).
- **Q16. What `drop`, `discard`, `reject` and `event disable all` end.** Check which later events and other rules run, and what the client sees. The simulator's `discard` keeps the connection open, while the registry says `DropsConnection`. **Open**, for a lab probe ([360](../../design/registry/model/360-spectcl-f5-integration.md)). Procedure for `event disable all` across rules: [bigip-name-checks](bigip-name-checks.md) item 38 (b).
- **Q17. Field lifetimes.** Check whether `pool`, `persist` and an inserted header persist across keep-alive requests, with and without OneConnect, and whether `HTTP::header insert` is visible in `HTTP_REQUEST_SEND`. **Open**, for a lab probe ([360](../../design/registry/model/360-spectcl-f5-integration.md)). Connection variables across keep-alive: bigip-name-checks item 36.
- **Q18. Profile kinds that admit the HTTP events.** Test `http2`, `http-compression`, `web-acceleration` and `http-proxy-connect` alone; `tcl-bigip` maps them all to HTTP. **Open**, for a lab probe ([360](../../design/registry/model/360-spectcl-f5-integration.md)). `tcl_bigip::hud_chain` and BIGIP6038 type them as HTTP until answered.
- **Q19. Implicit `tcp`.** Does a TCP virtual with `profiles { http }` and no transport profile get `tcp`? `validator.rs:715` assumes it does. **Open**, for a lab probe ([360](../../design/registry/model/360-spectcl-f5-integration.md)). `tcl_bigip::hud_chain` reports the assumption as `impliedFilters`.
- **Q20. The `tmsh::`, `iapp::` and `script::` inventory.** Run `info commands` in an iApp implementation, a `cli script` and iCall on 17.1, 17.5 and 21.1. Does `tmsh::run` exist? **Open**, for a lab probe ([361](../../design/registry/model/361-spectcl-f5-iapps-tmsh.md)).
- **Q21. Transactions.** Is an iApp implementation already transactional? Which `tmsh::` commands are refused inside `begin_transaction`? Does `create` accept `replace-all-with`? **Open**, for a lab probe ([361](../../design/registry/model/361-spectcl-f5-iapps-tmsh.md)).
- **Q22. The `f5.iapp` helper library.** Find its versions and how it is included, the variable shape a presentation table produces, and whether any template has a `macro` section. Probe together with Q12. **Open**, for a lab probe ([361](../../design/registry/model/361-spectcl-f5-iapps-tmsh.md)).
- **Q23. Return shapes.** What do `tmsh::get_config`, `get_field_value` and `get_name` return, and how does `$tmsh::argv` resolve from `script::run`? **Open**, for a lab probe ([361](../../design/registry/model/361-spectcl-f5-iapps-tmsh.md)).
- **Q24. `tmsh mv ltm pool`.** Does it exist on 17.1 and 21.1, and which references does it update: VS, policy, iRule literals? **Open**, for a lab probe ([361](../../design/registry/model/361-spectcl-f5-iapps-tmsh.md)).
- **Q25. iApp folders and `strict-updates`.** Which folder (`/Common/APP.app/`) do iApp-created objects land in, and how does `strict-updates` affect a later `tmsh::modify`? **Open**, for a lab probe ([361](../../design/registry/model/361-spectcl-f5-iapps-tmsh.md)).
- **Q26. Error codes in iRules.** Which `-errorcode` does a failing iRules command set, can a rule read `$::errorCode` after `catch`, and does `catch` stop an iRules command failure from aborting the rule? **Open**, for a lab probe ([392](../../design/registry/model/392-error-codes.md)). The uncaught-error half: bigip-name-checks item 39.
- **Q27. `format %c` of a negative value under `tmm`.** It gives two characters (`fe81`) where tclsh 8.4.20 gives one. Measure it on each K5902 line. **Open**, for a lab probe ([370](../../design/registry/model/370-dialect-grammars.md)).
- **Q28. Character counts and the `scriptd` character model.** How many characters does `string length` count for a BMP literal under `tmm`, and what is the character model under `scriptd` (an iApp implementation and tmsh)? **Open**, for a lab probe ([370](../../design/registry/model/370-dialect-grammars.md)).
- **Q29. Alternate forms.** What do `format %#x 0` and `format %#o 8` print on the appliance? **Open**, for a lab probe ([370](../../design/registry/model/370-dialect-grammars.md)).
- **Q30. `clock` on the appliance.** Check `clock add`, `clock format %Ez` and `clock scan -format`. **Open**, for a lab probe ([370](../../design/registry/model/370-dialect-grammars.md)).
- **Q31. `event enable` and `event disable` scope.** With two rules of different priority on one virtual server, does `event disable` act on the rule or on the connection, in what order do the handlers run, and do they share variables? It extends Q16. **Open**, for a lab probe ([360](../../design/registry/model/360-spectcl-f5-integration.md)). Procedures: bigip-name-checks items 34 (order), 36 (variables) and 38 (b) (`event disable all`).
- **Q32. Two handlers for one event at one priority in one rule.** Does the loader accept them, and in what order do they run? **Open**, for a lab probe; procedure: bigip-name-checks item 35.
- **Q33. What `return` and a response leave running.** Does `return` end only its handler, do later handlers in other rules run after `HTTP::respond`, what does `HTTP::has_responded` report there, and what does a second respond do? **Open**, for a lab probe; procedure: bigip-name-checks items 37 and 38 (a).
- **Q34. A called proc's frame.** Does `upvar 1` in a proc `call`ed from a handler reach the connection's variables, and from `RULE_INIT` the global frame? **Open**, for a lab probe; procedure: bigip-name-checks item 40.
- **Q35. A command whose profile the virtual server lacks.** Is the attach refused, or does the handler load and never fire; is a command outside its event a load or run-time error? **Open**, for a lab probe; procedure: bigip-name-checks item 41.

The adversarial review of this plan (P1–P15) and the design fixers' handoffs, applied here:

- **P1:** 308 split into 308a (Now 7) and 308b (after item 84); item 90's done-when excludes 274, which completes with item 77; 414 sits with 313 in Now 8, after 312 and 311 in Now 6.
- **P2:** 443 performs 330's iRules upgrade; 330's done-when names the Tcl 8.6 pack only.
- **P3:** 256's done-when no longer names `bpf-tcl-ir`; 464 drops both of its edges.
